# Audit of Trace 1.22.0 (17 November 2026): every finding

The summary, the freeze diagnosis and what to act on first are in [audit-2026-11-17.md](audit-2026-11-17.md).



Each entry gives the auditor's claim and the independent check. Severity is the checker's. "Confirmed" means the checker read the code and the defect and scenario hold; "Plausible" means the code is as described but the trigger or impact was not fully established. Several entries describe the same defect from different angles; they are kept so each check and measurement stays on record.

### Freeze hunt: recurring work (13)

#### First Drive comparison of a project after each start still reads every note on the main process (1.21.3 fix incomplete)

- **Check:** Confirmed, high (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-timers-drive-first-compare`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/drive.ts:88`
- **What the code does:** Line 82's fast path needs identical ids and sizes. Otherwise lines 88-91 read the local and Drive copy of every id not in the in-memory `same` map, which is empty after a start. Measured now: ai-scribe-5688686b0a has 250 of 336 notes differing only in line endings (CRLF/LF), k-gea-382ef5dedd has 1 of 785. Drive read: 2.5 ms per note (measured, 40-note samples).
- **What goes wrong:** After Trace starts, the first channel start in AI Scribe (prepareLaunch -> AutoMemory.prepare -> trackProject -> drive.sync) or the first 30 s tick for that project reads all 336 notes on both sides: the window and terminals stop for about 0.9 s; about 2.1 s for a channel in the home folder (785 notes).
- **Blocking time:** About 0.9 s (336 notes) and 2.1 s (785 notes) on the Trace main process, once per differing project per start. Estimated from a measured 2.5 ms per Drive note read; the mismatches themselves are measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Make the comparison asynchronous (fs.promises in batches, like sizesLater) or move it to a worker, and keep the compared size pairs on disk so a restart does not repeat it. Compare parsed notes once and then rewrite neither side; optionally treat CRLF/LF-only differences by a stored hash.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. drive.ts:82 returns early only when every id and size match; otherwise line 89 skips an id only if the in-memory `same` map already holds its size pair, and that map (line 56) is empty after a start and is filled by the fast path only when it succeeds. So for a project with even one size mismatch, the first sync() after each start reads and zod-parses every note on both sides synchronously (lines 90-91). It is reached from prepareLaunch (main.ts:173 -> auto-memory.ts:260 -> main.ts:518 -> service.ts:49) and from the tick (syncLater only makes the size listing asynchronous; the reads stay synchronous). No guard elsewhere. After that one pass the pairs are remembered, so it is once per project per start, not recurring. Two corrections: (1) the stall is larger than estimated, 1.5 s and 4.7 s measured here rather than 0.9 s and 2.1 s; (2) the 250 differing notes are not CRLF/LF-only: after normalising line endings the texts still differ (local copies have CRLF plus other formatting), though the parsed notes are equal. This is the item the known history lists as left unfixed in 1.21.3 (one full comparison after each start), so it is an incomplete fix, not a new defect. High stands: the home-folder project is the one most channels start in, and 4.7 s is above the reported 1-3 s.
- **Checker's measurements:** Stat-only size comparison, local vs G:\My Drive: ai-scribe-5688686b0a 250 of 336 notes differ in size, k-gea-382ef5dedd 1 of 785; the other 8 projects match (fast path). Replayed lines 88-91 with the real MemoryService.note logic (directory checks, stat, read, zod parse) on both sides: ai-scribe-5688686b0a 1,613 ms and 1,529 ms (Drive part 1,523/1,441 ms, local 88 ms); k-gea-382ef5dedd 4,701 ms (Drive 4,456 ms, local 237 ms). 0 conflicts, 0 unreadable. That is 4.5-5.7 ms per Drive note, about twice the auditor's 2.5 ms. usage.db copy: 58 of the runs since 8 Nov started in the home folder, which maps to the 785-note project.

#### Notes are uploaded to and downloaded from Drive one by one, synchronously, on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-timers-drive-bulk-transfer`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/drive.ts:96`
- **What the code does:** Lines 96-97 call mirror.note/local.import and local.note/mirror.import per note; MemoryService.import (memory.ts:81-91) does existsSync, a readdirSync of the whole Drive project folder, writeFileSync and renameSync on G:. Drive file times (stat only) show back-to-back uploads: median 16 ms per note, 250 notes in 3.88 s on 2026-11-15T21:07Z, 146 notes in 1.78 s on 2026-11-07.
- **What goes wrong:** A project whose notes are not on Drive yet (new or moved folder key), or a batch of handoffs written by subagents within one 30 s tick, is copied in one synchronous loop: Trace froze 3.9 s for 250 notes. Notes written on another computer are read from a streaming Drive (download per note; not measured, other computers idle since 7 Nov).
- **Blocking time:** 16 ms per uploaded note (measured from Drive file times): 0.1-0.25 s for the usual 5-12 note bursts, 3.9 s observed for 250 notes; Trace main process. Three-note bursts of 1.1-2.7 s appear four times but may span two sync calls (unconfirmed).
- **Suggested fix:** Do the copies with fs.promises, a few at a time, yielding between notes, or in a worker; drop the per-note readdirSync of the Drive folder (count once per sync).
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: drive.ts:96-97 copy one note per iteration synchronously; MemoryService.import (memory.ts:81-95) does mkdirSync, existsSync, a readdirSync of the whole target folder, existsSync again, writeFileSync, renameSync and unlinkSync for each note, all on the Drive folder for uploads. The tick path (syncLater) reaches the same loops. The Drive timestamps reproduce the auditor's numbers exactly, and the local copies of those 250 notes carry CRLF while the Drive copies do not, which fits Trace's import() having re-serialised them, so the 3.9 s burst was Trace's own loop. Severity lowered to medium: multi-second transfers happened 3 times in about five weeks (a moved or new project, or a first sync); the common case is 1-19 notes per sync, 15-290 ms. The download side for files Drive has not cached could be far slower per note, but that was not measured and other computers have been idle, so it stays unproven.
- **Checker's measurements:** Drive file times (stat only, 1,908 notes): 250 notes of ai-scribe-5688686b0a written back to back in 3,876 ms from 2026-11-15T21:07:38Z (15.6 ms per note); 146 notes of k-gea-382ef5dedd in 1,780 ms on 2026-11-07 (12.3 ms per note). Bursts with gaps under 1 s: 439 single notes, 184 of 2-4, 82 of 5-19 (94-290 ms each, 11-25 ms per note), 3 of 20 or more. readdirSync(withFileTypes) of the Drive project folder, which import() repeats per note: 4.35 ms median for 785 entries, 1.8 ms for 336. Drive writes not measured (no writes allowed).

#### Every channel start and every end-of-session summary stats the project's whole Drive folder and re-reads all its notes synchronously

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-timers-launch-drive-sync`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/service.ts:49`
- **What the code does:** trackProject calls drive.sync(project) without sizes, so MemoryService.sizes (memory.ts:35-41) runs readdirSync + statSync per note on G:. auto-memory.ts:260-261 then calls memory.list(key), reading and parsing every local note. Measured: Drive 0.65 ms per note (66 ms for 105 notes, 215 ms for 336, 520 ms for 785); list 18/41/123 ms. Notes grow by 100-170 per active day; MAX_NOTES is 5000.
- **What goes wrong:** Starting or stopping a channel in AI Scribe blocks the main process about 0.3 s, in the home-folder project about 0.65 s, plus 31 ms for reg.exe. A hierarchy Round Table starts three seats together, so about 1-2 s in a row. At 5000 notes one start would block about 4 s.
- **Blocking time:** 0.1-0.65 s per channel start and per session summary today (measured parts, summed), Trace main process; grows linearly with notes (estimated 4 s at the 5000-note limit).
- **Suggested fix:** Use the asynchronous path (syncLater) from trackProject, and build launch context from a cached or bounded read (newest N files by name/mtime) instead of memory.list of the whole project.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. Every agent launch, terminal or chat, runs prepareLaunch synchronously (pty-manager.ts:228 -> main.ts:173); AutoMemory.prepare calls refresh (auto-memory.ts:260), which is trackProject (main.ts:518), which calls drive.sync(project) without sizes (service.ts:49), so drive.ts:80 runs the synchronous sizes() on the Drive folder; auto-memory.ts:261 then lists and parses every local note. No cache or guard. Two corrections: the title overstates the session-end path, where onWrite -> trackProject runs the Drive stat pass but not memory.list, and only when a summary note is actually written (summarize returns null for seats and trivial sessions); and my timings are somewhat lower than reported. The 4 s at 5,000 notes is an extrapolation (about 2.6 s Drive + 0.3 s list by my per-note figures). This is the item the known history lists as left unfixed (sync called directly on handoff write and channel launch, 0.35 s for 636 notes), now larger because the project grew. Medium rather than high: each stall is under 0.5 s today and tied to a launch or session end, but it grows linearly and notes are growing by hundreds per active day. The claim that three Round Table seats add up to 1-2 s was not verified.
- **Checker's measurements:** MemoryService.sizes replayed (readdirSync + statSync per note) on G:\My Drive: 406.6 ms for 785 notes (k-gea-382ef5dedd), 197 ms for 336, 164 ms for 304, 55.8 ms for 105; local side 16/7/7/2.5 ms. That is about 0.52 ms per Drive note (auditor: 0.65). memory.list replayed with zod: 42-57 ms for 785 notes, 17-29 ms for 336, 7-15 ms for 105 (auditor: 123/41/18 ms). So about 0.46 s per channel start in the home-folder project, 0.22 s in AI Scribe, 0.07 s in this repository. Notes created per day (createdAt): 170, 329, 69, 356, 508 on 11-15 Nov. reg.exe not measured.

#### Usage list re-reads and re-parses every usage row of every run, twice a minute or more, and grows with history

- **Check:** Plausible, medium (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-timers-telemetry-list-growth`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/telemetry.ts:233`
- **What the code does:** list() calls runUsage for up to 2000 runs; runUsage (217-232) runs a correlated NOT EXISTS query and JSON.parses each row. Measured on a copy of usage.db: 47 ms for 244 runs and 21,264 rows; 6.7 ms for one 4,542-row run. Called by device sync every 30 s (service.ts:56), the window every 60 s when sync is on (use-native-host.ts:68) and at start. Retention is 366 days (line 115).
- **What goes wrong:** The data covers about five weeks. With a year of history the same call takes an estimated 0.4-0.5 s, every 30 s and every 60 s, and runUsage for a long run (recomputed on every changed 750 ms poll) grows the same way: a regular half-second freeze that appears gradually.
- **Blocking time:** 47 ms per call now (measured), about 3 calls a minute, Trace main process; estimated 0.4-0.5 s per call after a year of use.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep per-run totals in a table (or an in-memory cache invalidated per run) updated as rows are ingested, so list() and runUsage read one row per run.
- **Checker's notes:** The code and today's cost are confirmed: list() (telemetry.ts:233) calls runUsage for up to 2,000 runs, and runUsage (217-232) runs the correlated NOT EXISTS query and JSON.parses every row with no cached totals. Callers confirmed: device sync every 30 s (service.ts:56 via device-sync.ts:167), the window every 60 s when sync is on (use-native-host.ts:68 -> 'usage', service.ts:105), plus one at start and the 90 s sweep (main.ts:522); poll() recomputes a running run's total on each changed 750 ms read (telemetry.ts:180). Retention is 366 days (line 115). What is not established is the failure scenario itself: the 0.4-0.5 s figure is a linear extrapolation from five weeks of data (21k rows now, roughly 200k in a year at this rate, capped somewhat by LIMIT 2000 runs). The arithmetic holds and the query is linear in rows, but it has not happened yet, so PLAUSIBLE. Today the cost is about 40 ms three times a minute, which overlaps the device-sync finding.
- **Checker's measurements:** On a copy of usage.db (8.4 MB + 2.5 MB WAL): 244 runs in metadata, 21,290 usage rows, first run 2026-10-11. list() equivalent (same SQL, JSON.parse per row, without usageCost): 39-43 ms over 5 runs, returning 127 records. runUsage for the largest runs: 6.7 ms (4,542 rows), 2.5 ms (1,608), 1.5 ms (799). About 1.9 microseconds per row.

#### Day usage re-reads 1.46 GB of CLI logs after every start, 16 MB at a time on the main process, and walks every log every 15 s

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-timers-dayusage-startup`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/day-usage.ts:34`
- **What the code does:** days() reads up to READ_BUDGET (16 MB) per call and keeps offsets only in memory. Measured: logs of the last 60 days total 1.46 GB (353 files); 46 passes took 37-68 ms each and were still incomplete. The window asks again 0.5 s after each incomplete answer (day-usage-store.ts:18). Steady state: 340 stats, about 20-25 ms per call (StatusBar.tsx:70-71, main.ts:860).
- **What goes wrong:** For about a minute after each start the main process is blocked 40-65 ms twice a second (about 90 passes), making typing and output stutter, and the status bar's total for today is too low until the last pass. Afterwards each 15 s read costs about 25 ms.
- **Blocking time:** 40-65 ms per pass (measured in Node 24, not in Electron), about 90 passes after each start; 20-25 ms every 15 s afterwards; Trace main process.
- **Suggested fix:** Save each file's offset and the per-day totals to disk, read in a worker or with async reads, and only stat files in folders whose mtime changed.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: days() reads up to 16 MB per call (line 15, 34) and keeps offsets only in the in-memory `files` map (line 25), so every start re-reads the whole 60-day window. The window re-asks 0.5 s after each incomplete answer (day-usage-store.ts:18), so about 90 passes over roughly 50 s after each start, each blocking the main process 30-115 ms (IPC usage-today, main.ts:704). Afterwards every call still walks and stats every log: every 15 s and 2 s after usage settles (StatusBar.tsx:70-71), every 60 s from the alerts timer (main.ts:860), and on phone usage requests (main.ts:606). No persisted offsets, no worker. The pass times are a little higher than reported at the top end (up to 116 ms). One small correction: the status bar does not necessarily show too low a total meanwhile, because dayTotal (day-usage-store.ts:26-28) falls back to Trace's own runs when they are ahead. Medium is right: this is stutter for about a minute after each start and a 20-40 ms hitch every few seconds, not a 1-3 s freeze.
- **Checker's measurements:** Replayed DayUsage.days() faithfully in Node 24 on the real logs (4 sources: ~/.claude, ~/.codex, two Claude profiles): 340 candidate files, 548 entries visited, 1,440 MB in the 60-day window (1,202 MB in ~/.claude). 91 passes to complete, 31-116 ms each (first 65 passes 33-64 ms, later ones 74-116 ms), 5,373 ms in total. The file walk alone: 13-17 ms. A complete call after catch-up: 38-40 ms (measured while other audit probes were running; the auditor reported 20-25 ms).

#### The 30-second tick's device sync is one synchronous block: Drive reads, a full usage recompute and a Drive write

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-timers-tick-device-sync`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/device-sync.ts:209`
- **What the code does:** sync() runs folder(), two reconciles, templates, usage and addressBook with sync fs calls on G:. Measured per tick: Drive reads 22 ms median, 31 ms max (40 runs); telemetry.list() 47 ms. Lines 167-170 rewrite this computer's usage file on Drive whenever any run's usage changed (its Drive copy was rewritten at 20:52 today). The write itself was not measured (no writes allowed).
- **What goes wrong:** While any agent is working, every 30 s the main process stops for roughly 90 ms (22 + 47 measured, plus a temp-file write and rename on Drive, about 16 ms judging by note uploads). Typing and terminal output hitch each time; a slow Drive call lengthens it, since nothing here yields.
- **Blocking time:** About 70-100 ms every 30 s on the Trace main process (69 ms measured, Drive write inferred); Drive read latency was steady over a 150 s probe (max 19 ms per 5 calls, measured).
- **Suggested fix:** Run device sync with fs.promises (or in a worker), publish usage at most every few minutes and only when totals changed, and reuse the last telemetry list instead of recomputing it each tick.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: sync() (device-sync.ts:209-224) runs folder(), two reconciles, templates, usage and addressBook with synchronous fs calls on the Drive folder, then saves device-sync.json locally every tick; usage() calls localUsage(), which is telemetry.list() (service.ts:56), and rewrites this computer's usage file on Drive whenever the digest of all records changes (lines 167-170), which is every tick while any run's totals move. syncDevices is on in this computer's preferences, so the path runs every 30 s (service.ts:65). Total about 65 ms measured plus one Drive write, consistent with the 80 ms already recorded in the known history as left unfixed in 1.21.3, so this is a re-statement of a known item with a breakdown. Severity lowered to low: under 0.1 s every 30 s does not explain 1-3 s stalls, and the probe showed no Drive latency spikes; the risk that a slow Drive call stretches it is real in principle (nothing yields) but was not observed.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only replay of the Drive calls one sync() makes (stat/realpath of the Drive folder, four sub-folder checks, settings.json, rules.json, templates listing (0 files), usage listing (3 files), computers (3 files)): 19.5 ms min, 21.3 ms median, 24.6 ms max over 20 runs. telemetry.list on a copy of usage.db: 39-43 ms. Own usage file on Drive is 85 KB, last rewritten 2026-11-18T04:52:48Z. 90 s latency probe of G: (4 calls every 0.5 s, 177 samples): median 3.5 ms, p95 6.4 ms, max 24 ms, none over 50 ms. Drive write not measured.

#### Opening a channel's Settings reads and parses every memory note of every project

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-timers-memory-projects`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/memory.ts:27`
- **What the code does:** EngineControls.tsx:28 calls memory-projects whenever a channel's Settings opens; MemoryService.projects() calls list() for each project folder, which readFileSyncs and zod-parses every note, only to return a count and the newest date. Measured (read + JSON.parse, no zod): 287 ms for 1,858 notes in 10 projects.
- **What goes wrong:** Clicking Settings on a tile blocks the main process about 0.3 s now; with notes growing by 100-170 per active day it passes 1 s within weeks. The Memory screen's memory-list for the home project costs 123 ms the same way.
- **Blocking time:** About 0.3 s per Settings open (measured without zod parsing), Trace main process; grows with the number of notes.
- **Suggested fix:** Count note files with readdir and take the newest from file times (or a small index file) instead of parsing every note; cache the result.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: projects() (memory.ts:23-30) calls list() for every project folder, and list() (73-80) reads and zod-parses every note, to return only a count and the newest date. It is called from MemoryProjectControl's mount effect (EngineControls.tsx:28), and EngineControls is mounted only when a tile's Settings panel is opened (SessionTile.tsx:48, 153-154; controlsOpen starts false), so the trigger is one click, not a timer. The handler runs synchronously on the main process (main.ts:772 -> service.ts:89). The cost measured here is about 0.1 s, a third of the claim, and it happens only on a deliberate click, so low rather than medium. It does grow with notes (hundreds per active day here), and it has no cache, so the fix suggested is still worthwhile.
- **Checker's measurements:** Replayed MemoryService.projects() with the real zod noteSchema on ~/.coding-hub/memory: 10 projects, 1,858 notes, 112 ms, 97 ms, 97 ms (warm file cache, Node 24). list() for the home-folder project (785 notes): 42-57 ms. The auditor's 287 ms and 123 ms were not reproduced; they may have been cold-cache or under load.

#### Round Table turn checks read and hash every changed file (up to 5000 files or 256 MB) on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-timers-repository-state`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/repository-state.ts:45`
- **What the code does:** repositoryState() lists changes with --untracked-files=all, then statSync + readFileSync + sha256 for each file in one loop (limits: 5000 files, 256 MB, line 10). round-table.ts:292 and 308 and review-chain.ts:157 and 530 call it at each turn's start and end. Today it is cheap: Coding-Hub has 3 changed files and AI Scribe is not a Git repository (checked).
- **What goes wrong:** A table in a repository with large untracked files (build output, data, media not in .gitignore) reads up to 256 MB twice per turn: an estimated 0.5-1.5 s freeze at each turn boundary, which looks random to the person. Not measured, because no open project is in that state.
- **Blocking time:** Estimated 0.5-1.5 s per call at the 256 MB limit, Trace main process, twice per Round Table turn; negligible in the two projects open now (measured: 3 files).
- **Suggested fix:** Hash with async reads or a worker, and use sizes and change times (already collected by changedFiles) for unchanged files instead of their contents.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as cited: repository-state.ts:43-46 runs statSync + readFileSync + sha256 for every changed file with no await in the loop, and pty-manager.ts:379 wires it to stateOf. Two corrections. (1) The auditor's cost estimate is wrong in direction: bytes are cheap (256 MB = 187 ms measured), file count is what hurts (5000 small files = 1.6-1.9 s measured, so roughly 0.35 s per 1000 changed files on this computer). (2) It is not always twice per turn: the start-of-turn call is unconditional (round-table.ts:160, 272, 292; review-chain.ts:157) but the end-of-turn call runs only when the review record changed (round-table.ts:307-308; review-chain.ts:529-530). verification.ts:54, 82, 97 also call it. Severity lowered because it needs a Round Table, review chain or verification run in a repository with hundreds to thousands of changed or untracked files not in .gitignore; neither the auditor nor I found a project in that state, so it does not explain the stalls reported today. Above 5000 files it returns null after the stat loop only (about 80 ms).
- **Checker's measurements:** Same loop on scratch files on C: (warm cache): 256 MB in 16 files = 187 ms; 32 MB in 8 files = 28 ms; 5000 files of 2 KB = 1634 ms and 1875 ms (about 0.35 ms per file). 5001 statSync calls = 77 ms. This repository today: 3 changed files.

#### hostEnvironment() runs reg.exe twice with execFileSync on the main process, cached for only 5 seconds

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-timers-reg-exe`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/discovery.ts:53`
- **What the code does:** readRegistryPath uses execFileSync('reg.exe', ..., timeout 3000) for two keys; the cache (lines 81-86) lasts 5 s. Callers: every channel start (pty-manager.ts:206), plan limits every 5 minutes (plan-limits.ts:79), Git status and diff (git.ts:17), task watch and Round Table state (repository-state.ts:17, 48). Measured: 31 ms median, 40 ms max for the pair (20 runs, real keys).
- **What goes wrong:** Each of those calls made more than 5 s after the last one stops the main process for 30-40 ms. If reg.exe is slow to start (antivirus, heavy load) it can block up to the two 3 s timeouts; that worst case was not observed.
- **Blocking time:** 31 ms median, 40 ms max per uncached call (measured), Trace main process; up to 6 s by the timeouts (not observed).
- **Suggested fix:** Read the two values asynchronously (execFile) and keep them for minutes, refreshing in the background; or read them with a native registry call.
- **Checker's notes:** discovery.ts:51-56 uses execFileSync with a 3 s timeout per key, two keys (line 49), and the cache at lines 81-86 lasts 5 s. Callers checked: pty-manager.ts:114 and 206 (every channel start), plan-limits.ts:79 (StatusBar.tsx:36 refreshes every 5 minutes), git.ts:17 (only when the diff view asks; I found no periodic Git status poll in src/), repository-state.ts:17 and 48, tasks.ts watch (task-store.ts:230, every 30 s and only with sent tasks open). So each of these blocks the main process about 30-70 ms when the cache is cold, a few times a minute at most. That is a small hitch, not a 1-3 s stall. The 6 s worst case needs reg.exe to hang twice; neither the auditor nor I observed it. The claim and the line are right; the severity (low) is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** Pair of reg.exe queries via execFileSync, 25 runs, both values parsed each time: min 30.6 ms, median 38.0 ms, p90 42.7 ms, max 67.7 ms (machine busy with other audit work).

#### Each saved-output flush and each usage line is its own fully synchronous SQLite commit on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `frz-timers-sqlite-fsync`
- **Where:** `electron/services/storage/workspace-storage.ts:105`
- **What the code does:** flush() runs UPDATE runs ... every 200 ms per printing channel with PRAGMA synchronous=FULL (line 30). telemetry.ts:207 inserts one row per usage line; its database also runs at synchronous=2 (measured default). Measured on a scratch WAL database on C:: 1.24 ms median, 11.9 ms max per commit over 928 commits; 0.02 ms with synchronous=NORMAL.
- **What goes wrong:** Eight printing channels make 40 commits a second, about 50 ms of main-process time per second, each an fsync that waits on the disk. A busy or stalled disk (builds, antivirus) turns directly into window stalls. No stall was seen in a 25 s test under load.
- **Suggested fix:** Use synchronous=NORMAL for both databases (WAL keeps them consistent), update recordedBytes at most once a second or at run end, and wrap one poll's inserts in a single transaction.
- **Checker's notes:** workspace-storage.ts:30 sets synchronous=FULL and flush() (line 105) runs one autocommit UPDATE per flush: at most every 200 ms per channel with new output (line 80-82), or sooner at 128 KB pending. Output reaches it from main.ts:134 (terminals) and main.ts:154 (chat text). telemetry.ts:114 sets no synchronous pragma, so usage.db runs at the default, which I measured as 2 (FULL) on Node 24; I did not check Electron 44's bundled SQLite build, so that default is assumed the same. telemetry.ts:206-207 does a SELECT plus one autocommit upsert per usage line. At about 1.1 ms per commit, eight printing channels cost about 45 ms per second, spread in 1 ms pieces: no visible stall by itself. A run whose log hit the 5 MB cap stops committing (line 77). The disk-stall scenario is reasonable but nobody observed it. Low is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch WAL database on C:, 1000 single-row UPDATE commits, Node 24.19 node:sqlite: synchronous=FULL median 1.14 ms, p99 2.9 ms, max 3.2 ms, total 1402 ms; default (no pragma) reports synchronous=2 and behaves the same (median 1.11 ms); synchronous=NORMAL median 0.012 ms, total 20 ms.

#### Trace's crash-handler pipe is inherited by every channel, so other Electron apps' crashes land in Trace's data

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `frz-timers-crashpad-leak`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/discovery.ts:89`
- **What the code does:** hostEnvironment() copies process.env and removes only Claude session variables. A channel's shell has CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME=\\.\pipe\crashpad_27468_..., and process 27468 is the installed Trace.exe. ~/.coding-hub/crash-dumps/reports holds 22 dumps (44 MB) whose command line is the source checkout's electron.exe --type=gpu-process with a temporary coding-hub-gpu profile: the GPU crash smoke test.
- **What goes wrong:** Any Electron or Chromium program an agent starts in a channel (smoke tests, a dev build of Trace, other apps) reports its crashes to the installed Trace's handler. Dumps pile up in the person's Trace folder, and a test that looks for its own crash dumps in its own storage folder does not find them there.
- **Suggested fix:** Delete CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME (and any other Chromium-internal variables) from the environment built in hostEnvironment().
- **Checker's notes:** hostEnvironment() (discovery.ts:88-96) copies process.env and removes only the Claude session variables matched at line 36; pty-manager.ts:206 uses it as every channel's base environment. I confirmed the variable in a real channel and that the pipe belongs to the installed Trace. The dumps prove the effect: the GPU crash smoke test (scripts/gpu-crash-smoke.cjs:37, 79-85, temporary coding-hub-gpu- folder, its own storage folder) wrote its GPU-process dumps into the installed Trace's folder. A likely reason the test's own handler did not take over: crashReporter.start() runs inside app.whenReady() (main.ts:491-495), after the GPU process may already have started with the inherited pipe name; I did not verify that ordering. No code in electron/, src/, shared/ or scripts/ deletes old dumps (search for crash-dumps and .dmp), so they accumulate, and they hold another program's memory. One part of the scenario is not established: the smoke test reads host-health.json, not dump files, so no test is shown to fail from this. Count is 23, not 22. No effect on responsiveness.
- **Checker's measurements:** This audit shell (started from a Trace channel) has CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME=\\.\pipe\crashpad_27468_..., and PID 27468 is the installed Trace.exe. ~/.coding-hub/crash-dumps/reports: 23 .dmp files, 44.3 MB, dated 2026-11-15 10:39 to 12:44. All 23 contain a command line of a source checkout's node_modules\electron\dist\electron.exe --type=gpu-process and the string coding-hub-gpu; none contain Trace.exe.

#### Two Codex channels in one folder can rescan Codex's session folders every 750 ms for their whole run

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `frz-timers-codex-find-forever`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/telemetry.ts:160`
- **What the code does:** poll() calls find() while w.file is null. For Codex, find() reads the tails of up to 10 log files (128 KB each), lists six date folders, stats up to 500 rollouts each and reads 64 KB of each recent one (lines 138-155). With requiresThread set and no thread id in the log, line 160 returns null every time. Whether Codex's log names the thread was not checked.
- **What goes wrong:** Two Codex channels started in the same folder whose TUI log has no thread_id never find their rollouts: each repeats the scan every 750 ms until it exits, and neither run's usage is recorded. Cost per scan not measured (estimated a few ms with today's 127 rollouts).
- **Suggested fix:** Back off the search (for example 750 ms, then 5 s, then 30 s) after the first minute, and report once that usage cannot be attributed.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described: poll() calls find() every 750 ms while w.file is null (telemetry.ts:126, 166), with no back-off, and line 160 returns null whenever requiresThread is set and no thread id was found (a known thread returns at line 151). What is missing is the trigger. Older Codex logs on this computer do name the thread, so those runs resolve. The newer Codex writes its log into a SQLite file in the same folder; the id is present as text, but find() reads only the last 128 KB of each file (line 138), so whether it is seen depends on when the poll runs. I could not establish that it is never found, and I did not run two Codex channels to test it. If it does happen, the cost is about 4 ms every 750 ms per affected channel (about 0.5% of the main process), so this is not a freeze; the real loss would be those runs' usage not being recorded, with no message to the person. Low severity, low confidence, as the auditor said.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only copy of find() against the real ~/.codex/sessions (84 rollouts in the 3-day window, 4 date folders) plus one leftover 2.5 MB run log: median 3.6 ms, max 5.3 ms per scan when no rollout is newer than the run; 14.1 ms median when all 84 are read. Leftover run logs: two codex-tui.log files (13 Oct) match the thread_id pattern in their last 128 KB; one newer folder (26 Oct) holds only logs_2.sqlite-wal and -shm, where the pattern occurs 6 times in the file but not in its last 128 KB. Codex CLI here: 0.167.1.

#### Settings' 3-second status poll starts the Tailscale CLI two or three times per poll

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `frz-timers-remote-status-spawns`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:691`
- **What the code does:** remoteControl ends with `await refreshTailscale()` for every action, including remote-status. PhoneAccess.tsx:45 and OtherComputers.tsx:30 send remote-status every 3 s while shown. refreshTailscale runs `tailscale status --json` and `serve status --json`, and autoLink (line 654) runs `status --json` again and fetches /api/hello from each computer.
- **What goes wrong:** With Settings open on Phone access or Other computers, Trace starts up to three tailscale.exe processes every 3 s (each spawn call blocks the main process 2-10 ms, measured for git and claude.exe) and probes the other computers continuously, adding load while the person is in Settings.
- **Suggested fix:** Answer remote-status from the last known state and leave Tailscale checks to the existing one-minute interval (or an explicit refresh).
- **Checker's notes:** main.ts:691 does call refreshTailscale() for every action including remote-status, and that runs `status --json`, `serve status --json` (tailscale.ts:73, 84) and, through autoLink, `status --json` again (tailscale.ts:61) plus an /api/hello fetch per computer (linked-computers.ts:141). But the stated trigger is wrong. The 3 s polls are not active whenever Settings is shown: PhoneAccess.tsx:42-47 returns early unless a pairing code is showing, and OtherComputers.tsx:28-32 likewise; pairing codes last 5 minutes (remote-server.ts:156, 361). PhoneAccess.tsx:49-55 polls every 5 s only while phone access is on and Tailscale setup is unfinished. With Settings simply open, each card sends remote-status once on mount (PhoneAccess.tsx:40, OtherComputers.tsx:25). Also autoLink shares one discovery while it is in flight (linked-computers.ts:131), so the third spawn and the probes are not repeated while an unreachable computer's 5 s timeout runs. Net: a few milliseconds every 3 s for at most five minutes while a code is displayed. A real but negligible inefficiency, unrelated to the reported stalls.
- **Checker's measurements:** remote.json here: enabled true, autoLink true. tailscale.exe spawned with execFile, 16 runs: the spawn call blocks 2.2 ms median, 6.0 ms max; `status --json` completes in 22 ms median (87 ms max), `serve status --json` in 22 ms. So one remote-status costs about 4-7 ms of main-process blocking (up to about 18 ms worst case for three spawns).

### Freeze hunt: blocking calls by trigger (10)

#### First sync of a project after each start reads every note from Drive, including pairs whose sizes match; two projects here are always on that path

- **Check:** Confirmed, high (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-syncio-first-compare-all-pairs`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/drive.ts:89`
- **What the code does:** The fast path (drive.ts:82) trusts equal sizes, but the loop at :88-92 skips a pair only when it is in `same`, which is empty after a start, so every pair is read. Here ai-scribe-5688686b0a has 250 of 336 notes with CRLF locally and LF on Drive; k-gea-382ef5dedd has 1 of 785. Measured: reading the Drive copies 902 ms and 2,257 ms (local 32 and 73 ms).
- **What goes wrong:** After each start, the first channel launched in AI Scribe blocks about 1.1 s and the first in the home folder about 2.8 s (listing included). If the project was only opened in Memory, the same block lands on a 30 s tick at an arbitrary moment.
- **Blocking time:** Main process, once per affected project per start: 0.93 s (336 notes) and 2.33 s (785 notes) measured, plus 0.2-0.5 s listing when it happens inside a launch.
- **Suggested fix:** In the loop treat size === other as same, as line 82 does, so only differing pairs are read (1 pair: 5 ms; 250 pairs: 760 ms, measured). Keep the compared set (id and both sizes) in a file so a restart does not repeat it. Test: one differing pair reads two files.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. The fast path at drive.ts:82 returns only when every size matches. Otherwise the loop at :88-91 skips a pair only if it is missing on Drive, archived, or already in `same` (a private in-memory Map, drive.ts:56, empty after every start), so local.note() and mirror.note() read and zod-parse every pair, including pairs with equal sizes. Because neither project has a content conflict, the loop completes and fills `same`, so it happens once per project per start of Trace, as claimed. Reachable two ways: the first trackProject for the project (channel launch or summary, synchronous listing included), or a 30 s tick after memory-list added the project (service.ts:87, :64; the listing is async there but the reads in sync() are not). This is the 'one full comparison (about 2.9 s) once after each start' that KNOWN HISTORY lists as left unfixed; the new part is the cause (equal-size pairs are read) and that two projects here can never take the fast path. Severity kept high because it is the only path I could measure on this computer that produces a 1-3 s whole-app stall like the one reported, though it occurs once per affected project per start, not repeatedly.
- **Checker's measurements:** Port of the compare loop (drive.ts:88-92) with an empty `same` map, on real notes: ai-scribe-5688686b0a 336 pairs, local reads 48 ms + Drive reads 1,081 ms = 1.13 s (250 pairs differ in size, all 250 local copies contain CRLF, 0 content conflicts); k-gea-382ef5dedd 785 pairs, local 103 ms + Drive 1,839 ms = 1.94 s (1 pair differs in size, 0 conflicts). Reading only the differing pairs: 2 ms (k-gea), 903 ms (ai-scribe). Add the synchronous listing when it runs inside a launch: 0.53 s and 0.22 s, so about 2.5 s and 1.35 s in total. The other four projects checked have 0 differing pairs and take the fast path.

#### Every channel start and every session-end summary still lists the whole Drive memory folder on the main process (1.21.3 fix incomplete)

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-syncio-launch-drive-listing`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/service.ts:49`
- **What the code does:** trackProject() calls drive.sync(project) without sizes, so drive.ts:80 runs memory.ts:35-42 sizes() (one statSync per note) on G: synchronously. Callers: AutoMemory.prepare refresh (auto-memory.ts:260, then list() of every note at :261), summary onWrite (:340), memory-write (service.ts:92). Measured: Drive listing 443-510 ms (785 notes), 176-200 ms (336), 58-66 ms (105); list() 58-69 / 19-22 / 6 ms.
- **What goes wrong:** Start or restart a channel in the home folder (project k-gea-382ef5dedd, 785 notes): window, terminals and phone stop about 0.55 s, and again when it ends with a summary. A hierarchy Round Table starts reviewer and planner together (src/runtime/round-table.ts:215): two blocks back to back, over 1 s. Grows with every note.
- **Blocking time:** Main process, 0.1-0.6 s per channel start and per summary, by project size. Measured parts: Drive listing 58-510 ms, note read 6-69 ms, local listing 2-26 ms. Stacks when channels start together.
- **Suggested fix:** Make trackProject use the async listing (syncLater) and let prepare() use the notes already on disk, or await the refresh before prepareLaunch. Cache list() per project keyed on the folder listing. Test: prepare() and a summary write never call MemoryService.sizes() on the mirror.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. service.ts:49 trackProject calls this.drive.sync(project) with no sizes; drive.ts:80 then runs this.local.sizes() and mirror.sizes() (memory.ts:35-42: readdirSync plus one statSync per note) synchronously. main.ts:517-518 wires both AutoMemory onWrite and refresh to trackProject; auto-memory.ts:260-261 calls refresh then memory.list(key) inside prepare(), which main.ts:173 calls from prepareLaunch on the main process; auto-memory.ts:340 calls onWrite after a summary; service.ts:92 calls drive.sync after memory-write. src/runtime/round-table.ts:215 does start reviewer and planner with Promise.all, so two blocks follow each other. No guard or cache exists. Notes: (1) this is the item KNOWN HISTORY already lists as left unfixed in 1.21.3 (0.35 s for 636 notes), now 0.52-0.55 s at 785 notes, so it is a still-open known item rather than a new defect; (2) the cost depends on the project: 0.55-0.6 s only in the 785-note home-folder project, 70-230 ms in the others. A sub-second stall at moments the person starts or ends a channel is medium, not high; it does not by itself produce the reported 1-3 s freeze (two seats starting together reach about 1.1-1.2 s in the home project).
- **Checker's measurements:** Port of MemoryService.sizes()/list() on the real folders, 3 runs each. Drive listing (sync readdir + statSync per note on G:): 522-549 ms for k-gea-382ef5dedd (785 notes), 214-230 ms for ai-scribe-5688686b0a (336), 202-231 ms (304), 165-186 ms (250), 69-83 ms for coding-hub-d0627d0a2d (105), 10-12 ms (14). Local sizes(): 17-19 / 6-8 / 2 ms. Local list() with zod parse: 44-55 ms (785), 17-21 ms (336), 6-7 ms (105). So about 0.6 s per launch in the home-folder project, about 0.08 s in this repository's project.

#### DayUsage keeps every message id and every log's state for as long as Trace runs

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said low). Category: resource-leak. ID: `frz-syncio-dayusage-unbounded`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/day-usage.ts:90`
- **What the code does:** `seen` (:25, filled at :98) and `files` (:79) are never pruned; only `used` drops days older than 60 (:39). One read of the last 60 days here left 22,835 ids and 340 file states.
- **What goes wrong:** Trace left open for weeks keeps ids of replies older than 60 days and the state of logs that left the window; memory grows slowly. No wrong totals result.
- **Suggested fix:** Store the day with each id and drop ids and file states older than the oldest day when days() prunes `used`.
- **Checker's notes:** The claim holds: `seen` (:25, added at :98) and `files` (:79) are never pruned; only `used` drops old days (:39). But the impact is understated and the important line is :90, not :98. The ids cost about 1.5 MB of heap. The real cost is `state.partial = data.subarray(start)`: when a chunk ends in a newline this is a zero-length view that still pins the whole chunk just read (up to 16 MB per log). A log that is not appended to again keeps that buffer until Trace quits, inside the 60-day window as well as after it. Measured here: 760 MB retained after the first full read, which matches the running app's 913 MB private bytes (I could not inspect the live process, so that attribution is inferred from the matching numbers). This is not slow growth over weeks: it is reached about a minute after every start. No wrong totals, no crash, and no proven link to the 1-3 s stalls, but roughly 0.8 GB of committed memory wasted on a machine with 5.9 GB of commit left warrants medium. Fix: copy the leftover bytes (Buffer.from(data.subarray(start))) or use an empty buffer when start equals data.length, and drop file states and ids older than the window.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only run of the bundled DayUsage over the same sources as main.ts:119 (default Claude home, Codex home, two profiles), node --expose-gc: 91 passes of 35-97 ms to finish, 1,440 MB of logs read, 22,853 ids (average 28 characters), 340 file states. After two forced GCs: heapUsed 5.5 MB, but arrayBuffers 760.5 MB, held by 331 distinct backing buffers reachable only through state.partial (all partials zero length). Steady-state totals() 21-23 ms. The live Trace main process (PID 27468, started 04:45 today) shows 913 MB private bytes, 125 MB working set. System: 31.8 GB RAM, commit limit 43.7 GB with 5.9 GB free.

#### Today's-usage read rescans every CLI transcript of the last 60 days on the main process every 2 to 15 seconds

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-syncio-dayusage-rescan`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/day-usage.ts:63`
- **What the code does:** days() calls candidates() each time: readdir of every project folder and statSync of every .jsonl in four CLI homes (340 logs here); read() stats each again (:80). Called from StatusBar.tsx:70 (15 s), :71 (2 s after a usage update) and main.ts:860 (60 s). Measured: 31-47 ms per call once caught up; after a start 90 passes of 41-117 ms (7.1 s in all), 500 ms apart (day-usage-store.ts:18).
- **What goes wrong:** While agents run, typing and output stall 30-50 ms every few seconds. For about a minute after each start they stall 40-120 ms twice a second while 1.5 GB of logs is read again from the beginning.
- **Blocking time:** Main process: 31-47 ms per call (measured) every 2-15 s; 41-117 ms x 90 passes after each start (measured).
- **Suggested fix:** Do the scan with fs.promises or in a worker; list folders at most once a minute and stat only files known to be growing; keep offsets and day totals in a file so a restart does not re-read 60 days.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: day-usage.ts:31-34 calls candidates() for every source on every call (:63-75: readdirSync of every project folder, statSync of every .jsonl, plus a readdir of each changed conversation's subagents folder), and read() stats each candidate again (:80). No caching of the listing; offsets live only in memory (:25), so each start re-reads the whole window. Callers verified: StatusBar.tsx:70 (15 s interval), :71 (2 s after the usage store last changed; it is a trailing debounce, so at most one call per 2 s), main.ts:860 (60 s), main.ts:606 (phone), and day-usage-store.ts:18 repeats after 500 ms while incomplete. All run through the synchronous workbench handler on the main process. Impact is smaller than a freeze: about 20-25 ms per call in steady state, which is below what a person notices, and 45-75 ms twice a second for about the first minute after each start, which can make typing feel uneven. It cannot explain a 1-3 s stall, so low rather than medium.
- **Checker's measurements:** Port of DayUsage.days() on the four real CLI homes: 340 candidate logs, 1,440 MB in the 60-day window; 199 readdirSync and 680 statSync per call. Caught-up call: 19-26 ms (12 runs; the auditor reported 31-47 ms). First passes after a start (16 MB budget each): 76, 54, 61, 44, 55, 52, 57, 51 ms; 1,440 MB / 16 MB = 90 passes, 500 ms apart, so roughly 5 s of blocking spread over about 50 s.

#### Each channel's output flush and each usage line is its own synchronous=FULL SQLite commit (a disk flush) on the main process

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-syncio-sqlite-full-commit`
- **Where:** `electron/services/storage/workspace-storage.ts:105`
- **What the code does:** storage.db is opened with synchronous=FULL (:30); flush() runs every 200 ms per channel with output (:80-82) and ends in an autocommit UPDATE (:105). usage.db keeps the default (FULL, checked on the copy) and telemetry.ts:206-207 commits once per usage line. Measured in a scratch WAL database: FULL 1.5 ms median, p99 4.4 ms, max 34 ms per commit; NORMAL 0.02 ms.
- **What goes wrong:** Eight busy channels make about 40 disk flushes a second (60 ms of each second blocked). Each waits on the disk, so a busy disk (npm install, antivirus) holds the whole app for as long as the flush takes. No stall above 34 ms was seen in 3 minutes of sampling.
- **Blocking time:** Main process: 1.5 ms median, 34 ms max per commit (measured), up to 5 commits a second per channel plus one per usage line. Tail under heavy disk load not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** PRAGMA synchronous=NORMAL on both databases (WAL stays consistent; recordedBytes is re-read from the log size at start). Keep recordedBytes in memory and write it at endRun and history(). Wrap one ingest pass in a single transaction.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described: workspace-storage.ts:30 sets synchronous=FULL, append() arms a 200 ms timer per run with output (:80-82) and flush() ends with an autocommit UPDATE (:105); main.ts:134 and :154 feed it from every terminal and chat channel. telemetry.ts:114 sets WAL and no synchronous pragma, and :206-207 runs a SELECT plus an autocommit INSERT per usage line. Two limits on the claim. First, 'FULL, checked on the copy' proves nothing about usage.db: synchronous is a per-connection setting, not stored in the file; what I could confirm is that system Node 24's SQLite defaults to FULL in WAL mode, and I could not check the SQLite build inside Electron 44. Second, the stall scenario (a busy disk holding the app for a long flush) was not reproduced by the auditor (max 34 ms) or by me (max 3.8 ms in 1,200 commits). The steady cost is real but small (about 1 ms per commit), so this is an efficiency issue with an unproven tail, not an established freeze.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch WAL database on C: (same disk as ~/.coding-hub), 600 autocommit UPDATEs per mode with the same statement as workspace-storage.ts:105: synchronous=FULL median 1.09-1.10 ms, p95 2.0 ms, p99 2.2 ms, max 2.9-3.8 ms (792-798 ms for 600); NORMAL median 0.01 ms, max 0.17 ms (10 ms for 600). Node 24.19 node:sqlite (SQLite 3.53.3) reports DEFAULT_WAL_SYNCHRONOUS=2 and PRAGMA synchronous=2 on a connection opened the way telemetry.ts:114 opens it. Eight channels at 5 flushes a second would be about 45 ms of each second.

#### The 30 s tick rebuilds all usage records and rewrites this computer's usage file on Google Drive whenever any run's usage changed

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-syncio-tick-usage-rewrite`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/device-sync.ts:170`
- **What the code does:** usage() calls localUsage() = telemetry.list() (service.ts:56; 32-34 ms measured for 244 runs), hashes it, and on any change writeJson()s the file to G: synchronously (:71-76). While agents run the hash changes every tick: the 85 KB file was rewritten at 20:44 and 20:52 during this audit. The tick's 24 Drive reads measured 18.6 ms median, 71 ms max over 4 minutes.
- **What goes wrong:** Every 30 s while any agent works: a 33 ms rebuild, then a synchronous write and rename on the Drive volume, and Drive uploads the file each time. Other computers re-read and re-validate it on their next tick. This adds to the known 80 ms.
- **Blocking time:** Main process every 30 s: 33 ms rebuild (measured) + 19-71 ms Drive reads (measured) + one Drive write and rename (not measured: writing to G: was off limits).
- **Suggested fix:** Publish usage at most every 10-15 minutes or when a run ends; do the Drive reads and writes with fs.promises; keep each finished run's totals instead of recomputing all of them.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: service.ts:65 calls devices.sync() on every 30 s tick; device-sync.ts:167 calls localUsage() = telemetry.list() (service.ts:56), which runs runUsage (two queries) for up to 2,000 runs; :169-170 hashes the records and writeJson()s the whole file to the Drive folder synchronously (:71-76, write then rename) whenever the hash differs or the file is missing. syncDevices is on in this computer's preferences. A working agent changes its run's totals, so the write happens on most ticks while any agent works. One correction: this does not add to the known 80 ms. list() and the Drive reads are inside devices.sync(), and my 36-48 ms plus 16-29 ms is that known figure broken down; only the Drive write and rename (not measured, writing to G: was off limits) may come on top. list() grows with the number of runs kept (limit 2,000, a year of history), so the rebuild would reach a few hundred ms at the limit. Today it is a sub-100 ms stall every 30 s plus avoidable Drive upload churn: low.
- **Checker's measurements:** telemetry.list() port on a copy of usage.db (244 runs in metadata, 21,290 usage events, 127 records returned): 36-48 ms over 6 runs; hashing the records 0.6 ms. Read-only replay of the tick's Drive reads (5 JSON files, 3 readdirs, stats; 3 usage files, 0 templates): 16-29 ms over 8 runs. Own usage file on Drive: 85,465 bytes. Live check: device-sync.json lastSync was 3 s old (tick running) while the usage file and usage.db-wal were both last modified about 14 minutes earlier, 4 s apart, so the file is rewritten when usage changes, not on every tick.

#### Round Tables read and hash every changed file of the project synchronously at each turn start and end

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-syncio-repo-state-hash`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/repository-state.ts:45`
- **What the code does:** repositoryState() loops over up to 5,000 changed files and 256 MB with statSync, readFileSync and SHA-256 on the main process (:43-46). It runs at each turn start and end (round-table.ts:292 and :308; review-chain.ts:157 and :530), wired in at pty-manager.ts:379.
- **What goes wrong:** A Round Table in a repository with large untracked or modified files (a build output, a data set, an unignored dependency folder): every turn start and end blocks while they are read and hashed, roughly 0.5-1 s for 256 MB. Estimated: the repositories here have only a few changed files now.
- **Blocking time:** Main process, per turn start and end; scales with changed bytes, up to about 0.5-1 s at the 256 MB limit (estimated, not measured).
- **Suggested fix:** Hash with fs.promises and a stream, or compare the size:mtime marks changedFiles() already collects instead of file contents; lower the byte limit.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: repository-state.ts:41-46 awaits changedFiles (async Git, then one statSync per entry at :27) and then, in one synchronous run on the main process, does statSync, readFileSync and SHA-256 for each changed file up to STATE_FILES 5,000 and STATE_BYTES 256 MB (:10). pty-manager.ts:379 wires it as stateOf; round-table.ts:292 calls it at each turn start (snapshot) and :308 at turn end, but only when the review record changed (:307); review-chain.ts:157 and :530 do the same; verification.ts:54, :82 and :97 also call it. The work is bounded (over 5,000 files, over 256 MB or over 1 MB of Git output returns null early), and the trigger, a Round Table in a repository with hundreds of MB of unignored changed files, is not present on this computer now: the repositories have a few small changed files and the home folder is not a repository. Real but hypothetical here, and the auditor's time was an estimate.
- **Checker's measurements:** SHA-256 of a 64 MB buffer: 25 ms (3 runs), so hashing 256 MB is about 100 ms; file reads and up to 10,000 statSync calls come on top, which I did not measure, so the worst case is likely nearer 0.3-0.6 s than the 0.5-1 s estimated. State here: this repository has 3 changed files (small); ~ has no .git, so repositoryState returns null there after Git fails.

#### The Windows registry is read with synchronous reg.exe runs (3 s timeout each) on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-syncio-reg-exe-sync`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/discovery.ts:53`
- **What the code does:** hostEnvironment() runs two execFileSync('reg.exe') calls (5 s cache, :81-86). Callers: every channel start (pty-manager.ts:206), plan-limits refresh every 5 minutes and on window focus (plan-limits.ts:79), git status and diff (git.ts:17), task watch (repository-state.ts:17). A terminal start adds a third (pty-manager.ts:39, :55). Measured: 29-37 ms for the pair, 16-19 ms for the sudo read.
- **What goes wrong:** Normally a 30-50 ms hitch per call. If reg.exe is slow to start (antivirus, a loaded computer) the app is held for up to 6 s (two timeouts), or 9 s when a terminal starts.
- **Blocking time:** Main process: 29-37 ms (pair) and 16-19 ms (sudo) measured; worst case 6-9 s by the timeouts (estimated).
- **Suggested fix:** Use async execFile (every caller is already async) or read once and refresh in the background; read the sudo setting once per start of Trace.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: discovery.ts:51-55 runs reg.exe with execFileSync and a 3 s timeout, windowsRegistryPath (:63-66) calls it for two keys, and currentRegistryPath (:81-86) caches the result for 5 s. hostEnvironment() (:88) is called from pty-manager.ts:206 on every start, git.ts:17 for every Git command, repository-state.ts:17 and :48, tasks.ts:71, plan-limits.ts:79 and others; every caller is already async, so the synchronous spawn is avoidable. windowsSudoMode (pty-manager.ts:37-42) adds an uncached third spawn at :55 for each terminal start when sudo.exe exists. One overstatement: a window focus triggers the plan-limits read only when more than five minutes have passed (plan-limits.ts:77 returns before :79), so focus does not add reg.exe runs beyond the five-minute cadence. Normal cost is a 30-45 ms hitch at most once per 5 s, at moments something else is already starting; the multi-second case needs reg.exe to hang and was not shown. Low is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** execFileSync('reg.exe', ['query', key, '/v', 'Path']) for the two keys, as discovery.ts:53 runs it, 10 runs: 29.8-42.7 ms for the pair. The sudo read (pty-manager.ts:39): 15.3-25.1 ms. No slow start of reg.exe was observed; the 6-9 s worst case is only the sum of the 3 s timeouts.

#### Opening a channel's Settings reads every memory note of every project on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-syncio-memory-projects-read-all`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/memory.ts:27`
- **What the code does:** projects() calls list() for each project folder, which reads, parses and validates every note (:73-80). The window asks for it whenever a channel's Settings opens (src/components/EngineControls.tsx:28). Measured: 144 ms for 1,858 notes with a warm cache. The Memory panel's memory-list reads one project: 58-69 ms for 785 notes (HubPanels.tsx:98).
- **What goes wrong:** Click Settings on a tile: the app stops for about 0.15 s now, more with a cold cache, and longer with every note written (a project may hold 5,000).
- **Blocking time:** Main process: 144 ms (measured, warm cache) per Settings open; 58-69 ms per Memory refresh of the largest project.
- **Suggested fix:** Count note files from the folder listing and take the newest date from file times without parsing; or keep a small per-project index updated on write.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described. memory.ts:27 calls list() for every project folder, and list() (:73-79) stats, reads, JSON-parses and zod-validates every note. service.ts:89 answers 'memory-projects' synchronously, and main.ts:772 runs it on the main process. EngineControls.tsx:28 requests it in an effect with empty deps, and SessionTile.tsx:153-154 mounts EngineControls only while the Settings panel is open, so it runs once per Settings open of an agent channel. No cache or guard exists. My timing is slightly lower than the reported 144 ms but the same order. Severity stays low: about 0.1 s, only on a deliberate click, so it cannot be the random 1-3 s stall. The 5,000-notes-per-project growth case is theoretical here (largest project holds 785).
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only run of the bundled MemoryService on the real ~/.coding-hub/memory (constructor bypassed, nothing written), 7 runs each, warm cache, machine busy with other auditors: projects() 102-127 ms for 1,858 notes in 10 projects; list() of the largest project (785 notes) 40-42 ms, returning a 0.93 MB payload.

#### Codex log folders and memory MCP config files that could not be removed are never cleaned up, though the comments say a restart does it

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: resource-leak. ID: `frz-syncio-leftover-run-folders`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/telemetry.ts:298`
- **What the code does:** finish() and abandon() (:298, :261) ignore a failed rmSync with a comment that the folder goes after the next restart, but the constructor (:112-116) only prunes database rows. The same holds for auto-memory.ts:305 and :322. Here three telemetry/<runId> folders from October (2.9 MB) and two auto-memory/*.mcp.json files from Nov 10 remain.
- **What goes wrong:** Each Codex run whose log was locked at exit, and each crash before a summary, leaves a folder or file for good; ~/.coding-hub grows slowly and backups scan more.
- **Suggested fix:** At startup remove telemetry subfolders and auto-memory/*.mcp.json whose run is not active. Test: a leftover folder is gone after constructing TelemetryService.
- **Checker's notes:** The leak and the misleading comments are real. telemetry.ts:261 and :298 swallow a failed rmSync with comments promising removal at the next restart, but the constructor (:112-115) only deletes database rows. auto-memory.ts:305 and :322 say the file goes 'with the scratch folder', yet nothing removes that folder: a search of every rmSync/unlinkSync/rm call under electron/ (tests excluded) found no startup cleanup of telemetry/<runId> or auto-memory/, and main.ts:516 only passes the path. One part of the scenario is wrong: backups do not scan more. backups.ts:86 recurses only into memory, designs, coaching and optionally logs, so these leftovers are never walked or copied. The stale .mcp.json files hold program paths, the memory root, key, engine and cwd, no credentials. Impact is 2.9 MB in five weeks, so low is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** ~/.coding-hub/telemetry holds three run folders: 2,440 KB (Oct 13), 140 KB (Oct 13), 364 KB (Oct 26), total 2.9 MB; two hold codex-tui.log, one holds only logs_2.sqlite-shm/-wal. ~/.coding-hub/auto-memory holds two stale files from Nov 10 (398 and 415 bytes) plus two from Nov 17 that match runs started today. ~/.coding-hub totals about 391 MB.

### Freeze hunt: window and GPU side (12)

#### A freeze leaves no trace: no event-loop lag, long-task or context-loss record on either side

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: improvement. ID: `frz-renderer-no-stall-record`
- **Where:** `electron/services/storage/host-health.ts:31`
- **What the code does:** HostHealth.record keeps the last 100 lifecycle events (84 of the current 100 are cli-started/exited). Grep of src/ and electron/ finds no monitorEventLoopDelay, PerformanceObserver longtask or webglcontextlost handler. Measured now: main 721 CPU-s (372 kernel), renderer 69.5 CPU-s, GPU 77 CPU-s over 16.1 h; no measured path in my scope reached 1 s.
- **What goes wrong:** The person sees a 1-3 s stop. Afterwards nothing says whether the main process, the renderer or the GPU stalled, or what ran at the time, so each audit has to guess. Rare evidence that is recorded (a GPU exit) is pushed out of the 100-event list within days by channel starts and exits.
- **Suggested fix:** In main, sample perf_hooks.monitorEventLoopDelay and record any gap over ~250 ms with the name of the last timed job (tick, flush, poll, IPC channel). In the renderer, a PerformanceObserver for longtask plus a 250 ms heartbeat sent to main. Keep stalls in their own capped list, apart from lifecycle events.
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed. HostHealth.record (host-health.ts:29-33) keeps one list capped at 100 by slice(-100) at line 31, shared by all event kinds. main.ts records only process deaths (75-81), CLI starts and exits (131-132) and delivery failures. A case-insensitive search of electron/, src/, shared/, phone/ and scripts/ for monitorEventLoopDelay, PerformanceObserver, longtask, eventLoopUtilization, webglcontextlost and 'unresponsive' finds nothing, so neither a main-process stall, a long task in the window, nor a hung-window event is ever written down. On this computer 84 of the 100 stored events are cli-started/cli-exited and the list spans only four days, so the single GPU crash will be pushed out within days. This matters for the person's report: after a 1-3 s stop there is no evidence of which process stalled or what ran, which is why this audit and the 1.21.3 investigation had to rely on external timing. Medium is right for an improvement: it does not cause a freeze, but it is what blocks finding the cause. A stall record must stay cheap and must not include prompts or output (the rule stated at host-health.ts:16-17).
- **Checker's measurements:** Search: 0 matches for the six terms. host-health.json: 100 events = 42 cli-started, 42 cli-exited, 6 host-start, 6 host-stop, 3 auto-memory-notice, 1 child-process-gone; oldest 2026-11-14T07:15Z. Running processes: main 734 CPU-s (379 kernel), renderer 78 CPU-s, GPU 84 CPU-s over 16.3 h. Seen while measuring, not verified further: a second Trace.exe main process (pid 52552, started 11-15 13:04:42) with its own GPU and network processes but no renderer is still running.

#### Chat channels: every streamed delta re-renders the whole thread and re-parses every message's Markdown

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-renderer-chat-delta-rerender`
- **Where:** `src/components/chat/ChatView.tsx:136`
- **What the code does:** chat-store.ts:67 builds a new items array per `delta`; ChatView.tsx:60-68 and 136-139 map every item to <Item>; ChatParts.tsx:87/95 Item and Markdown are not memoized, so Markdown.tsx:29 blocks() runs again for all messages. No cap or virtualization. Measured in Node (renderToString, no DOM): 29 ms per pass at 200 messages, 88 ms at 600.
- **What goes wrong:** A hierarchy Round Table thread (three agents merged; a resumed chat starts with up to 200 history messages) reaches a few hundred items. An agent streams 20-40 deltas a second, each its own IPC message and render. Each pass costs more than the gap between deltas, so the window's UI thread stays busy for seconds: typing, clicks and all terminals stall until the reply ends.
- **Blocking time:** Renderer thread (whole window UI): 30-90 ms per delta measured in Node for 200-600 messages; multi-second stall while streaming is an estimate for the real app. Only when chat channels are open (used 11-15/16; today's workspace is 2 terminals).
- **Suggested fix:** Wrap Item and Markdown in React.memo with stable props (answer callbacks, per-item result), render the streaming message from its own selector, coalesce deltas per animation frame, and cap or virtualize the thread. Add a test that a delta does not re-render settled items.
- **Checker's notes:** The mechanics are as described. chat-store.ts:67 builds a new items array for every delta; ChatView.tsx:26 selects the whole thread, so ChatView re-renders, and lines 136-139 give every Item a fresh answer callback. Item (ChatParts.tsx:87) and Markdown (Markdown.tsx:8) have no memo, so every settled message is parsed again. Deltas are not coalesced: claude-stream.ts:115 emits one event per text_delta (CHAT_ARGS has --include-partial-messages) and main.ts:155 sends each one to the window. A resumed thread starts with up to 200 messages (transcript.ts:7) and grows without a cap. What does not hold is the size of the cost and so the stall. The earlier benchmark loaded react-dom/server from node_modules without NODE_ENV=production, so it timed React's development build. With production React I measured about a third of that on the same synthetic thread, and far less on real conversations. At 20-40 deltas a second that is extra load on the window while text streams, not a UI thread that is busy for seconds, and input is still handled between renders. Not established: React's client-side diff and the layout forced by the scrollHeight read at ChatView.tsx:56 (no DOM available here), and the real delta rate. It applies only to chat channels; the workspace open now is two terminals. Worth fixing as a scaling problem (memo, coalesce per frame, cap the thread), but it is not shown to cause the reported 1-3 s stops.
- **Checker's measurements:** Node 24, production React 18 bundled with the repo's own Item/Markdown, median of 9 passes. Synthetic thread (same as the earlier auditor's): 200 messages 10.4 ms renderToString / 3.1 ms render functions only; 600 messages 22.8 / 7.9 ms (earlier auditor: 29 and 88 ms). Real conversations read with Trace's own chatHistory from this project's Claude transcripts (13 MB, 9 MB, 4 MB files): 181-200 messages, 136-215 KB text: 2.5-3.6 ms renderToString, 0.3-0.7 ms render functions only; tripled to 543-600 items: 8.2-10.1 ms and 1.0-1.7 ms. chatHistory itself took 12-24 ms.

#### Main process commits to SQLite with a disk flush up to 5 times a second per channel

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-renderer-main-fsync-per-flush`
- **Where:** `electron/services/storage/workspace-storage.ts:105`
- **What the code does:** Line 30 sets synchronous=FULL; flush() (timer at 80-82, 200 ms) runs UPDATE runs SET recordedBytes per channel with output. telemetry.ts:207 inserts one autocommit row per usage line (synchronous measured 2 = FULL). Measured on a copy: FULL commit p50 1.3 ms, p99 2.7, max 12 ms over 1,290 commits; NORMAL 0.07 ms.
- **What goes wrong:** With several channels printing, the UI process waits on FlushFileBuffers 5-40 times a second. When the disk is busy (agents running installs, builds, tests; Drive syncing), one flush can take far longer than the 12 ms seen in a quiet 40 s sample; no PTY byte or IPC call is handled meanwhile. I did not observe a flush over 12 ms: the 1-3 s tail is an estimate.
- **Blocking time:** Main process (whole window). Measured 1.3 ms typical, 12 ms max per commit; seconds under disk contention is estimated, not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Use synchronous=NORMAL on storage.db and usage.db (WAL stays consistent), write recordedBytes at most every few seconds or only at endRun (the constructor already takes the file size for interrupted runs, line 40), and wrap one telemetry poll's inserts in a single transaction.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described. workspace-storage.ts:30 sets synchronous=FULL in WAL mode; append() arms a 200 ms timer (lines 80-82) and flush() runs an autocommit UPDATE of recordedBytes at line 105, with a new prepare() each time, so a channel with steady output commits up to 5 times a second, on the main process (called from the PtyManager event callback at main.ts:134 and from onChat at main.ts:154). telemetry.ts:114 sets no synchronous pragma and node:sqlite's default is FULL (measured 2); telemetry.ts:207 is one autocommit per usage line. The impact claim is not supported by measurement: on this computer, while the other auditors were loading the disk, no commit took longer than 3.7 ms. Eight busy channels at 5 commits a second and 1.3 ms each is about 5% of the main thread at worst, and one channel is under 1%. The 1-3 s tail under disk contention is speculation that neither the earlier auditor nor I could produce. Switching to synchronous=NORMAL and writing recordedBytes less often is a cheap, safe improvement, but this is not shown to be a cause of the freezes.
- **Checker's measurements:** On a copy of storage.db (102 runs, 4 KB pages, autocheckpoint 1000) in my scratch folder, same pragmas and a prepare() per commit as flush(): synchronous=FULL, 969 commits over 30 s: p50 1.29 ms, p90 2.18, p99 2.69, max 3.7 ms; none over 50 ms. synchronous=NORMAL, 400 commits: p50 0.12 ms, max 0.6 ms. usage.db copy: PRAGMA synchronous reports 2 (FULL), 21,263 usage rows. Trace main process now: 734 CPU-s (379 kernel) in 16.3 h, about 1.25% of one core on average.

#### With WebGL terminals on, a lost graphics context stops terminals drawing for up to 3 s, then falls back for good, and nothing records it

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-renderer-webgl-context-loss`
- **Where:** `src/runtime/terminals.ts:119`
- **What the code does:** This computer's preferences.json has terminalWebGL true. terminals.ts:119-123 loads WebglAddon and disposes it on onContextLoss. @xterm/addon-webgl WebglRenderer.ts:110-120 waits 3000 ms for webglcontextrestored before firing that. main.ts:76-81 records only process deaths. host-health: one GPU exit 34 (11-15); the GPU process now running started with the app (measured), so none this session.
- **What goes wrong:** The driver resets or the GPU process exits (exit 34 was recorded once in four days; I believe it means exit-on-context-lost, not verified). Every terminal canvas stops updating until the context is restored or, after 3 s, the addon is disposed and the terminal continues on the slower DOM renderer until restart. The person sees a 1-3 s stop that comes back; host-health shows nothing unless the process died.
- **Blocking time:** All WebGL terminals (whole window if the GPU process restarts): up to 3 s by the addon's constant. Frequency here looks rare: 1 GPU process death in 4 days of health records, 0 in the current 16 h session (measured).
- **Suggested fix:** Listen for webglcontextlost/restored and report them to host-health through a small IPC channel; on loss switch to the DOM renderer at once and re-create the WebGL addon after restore instead of staying on DOM. Consider defaulting this computer to DOM while testing whether freezes continue.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described. terminals.ts:119-123 loads WebglAddon when preferences.terminalWebGL is true and disposes it on onContextLoss; nothing re-creates it, so the terminal stays on the DOM renderer until the channel is removed or Trace restarts. @xterm/addon-webgl 0.19.0 WebglRenderer.ts:110-120 calls preventDefault on webglcontextlost and waits 3000 ms before firing onContextLoss; lines 122-131 rebuild the atlas and redraw if the context is restored first. This computer's preferences.json has terminalWebGL true, and the preference is loaded before terminals are made (the preferences request is sent before loadWorkspace at use-native-host.ts:66 and 108, and both handlers are synchronous), so the addon is in use. No webglcontextlost listener exists in src/ or electron/. Two corrections. First, the trigger is rare here: one GPU process death in the four days host-health covers and none in the current session, so it cannot explain stops the person calls random and recurring. Second, 'nothing records it' is only partly right: a GPU process death is recorded by main.ts:76-81 (that is how the one event is known); what goes unrecorded is a context loss without a process death and the permanent fallback to DOM. I did not verify that exit code 34 means exit-on-context-lost, and no context loss was observed.
- **Checker's measurements:** host-health.json: 100 events from 2026-11-14 07:15Z to 2026-11-18 04:31Z; exactly one child-process-gone ('GPU: crashed', exit 34, 2026-11-15T16:25:42Z). Running Trace 1.22.0: main pid 27468 started 11-17 04:45:57 local, its GPU process pid 46800 started 04:46:01 and is still the same process 16.3 h later (84 CPU-s), so no GPU restart this session.

#### Pasting a screenshot decodes, resizes and PNG-encodes it twice on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-renderer-clipboard-image-main`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:816`
- **What the code does:** The clipboardImage handler (812-820) runs nativeImage.createFromBuffer, resize and two toDataURL calls synchronously and returns both data URLs. terminals.ts:142 and 153 call it on Alt+V and on an image-only paste. Measured with the repo's Electron 44 on synthetic screenshots: 79 ms at 1920x1080, 251 ms at 2560x1440, 311 ms at 3840x2160, with a 5 MB reply.
- **What goes wrong:** The person pastes a 4K screenshot into a Claude channel. The main process is busy about 0.3 s encoding it; every channel's output and input waits. Then 5 MB of data URLs cross IPC and are kept in the renderer store for the thumbnail strip. Several pastes in a row feel like repeated short freezes.
- **Blocking time:** Main process (whole window): 80-310 ms per image paste, measured in a throwaway Electron 44 script on synthetic images; clipboard.read time and IPC transfer not included.
- **Suggested fix:** Return only a small thumbnail (JPEG, 160 px) from the handler and fetch the full image when the person opens the viewer; or do the resize and encode in a utility process or with createImageBitmap/OffscreenCanvas in the renderer.
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed by reading and by measurement. The handler at main.ts:812-820 runs on the main process: createFromBuffer decodes the PNG (816), then one or two resizes and two toDataURL calls (818-819), each a synchronous PNG encode, and returns both data URLs to the window. An image of 2000 px or less is still re-encoded at full size. It is reached from terminals.ts:142 (Alt+V, or Ctrl/Cmd+V on a Mac) and terminals.ts:153 (a paste with only an image on the clipboard on Windows), in Claude Code and Codex terminal channels only (terminals.ts:30); with no image on the clipboard it returns at line 815 before any image work. The main process is blocked for roughly 0.1-0.5 s per paste, during which no channel's output or input is handled. Severity is lower than claimed: the block follows a deliberate paste, is under about half a second, and happens once per paste, so it does not match stops the person describes as random and 1-3 s long. Doing the encode off the main process, or returning only a small thumbnail and fetching the full image when the viewer opens, removes it.
- **Checker's measurements:** Throwaway Electron 44 script (the repo's electron.exe, own profile in my scratch folder, not Trace), median of 3, same calls as lines 816-819, while other auditors were loading the machine. Text-like image: 1920x1080 101 ms (reply 0.46 MB); 2560x1440 244 ms (2.89 MB); 3840x2160 297 ms (2.99 MB). Noisier image: 78 ms, 409 ms, 552 ms (reply up to 3.72 MB). The full-size toDataURL is 63-463 ms of each total; decode is 4-28 ms. clipboard.read() and the IPC transfer are not included.

#### Every finished tool call and every usage update re-renders the whole workspace shell

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: performance. ID: `frz-renderer-sessions-array-churn`
- **Where:** `src/state/workspace-store.ts:199`
