- **What the code does:** applyEvent returns a new sessions array for every non-data event, including `answered`, which attention.ts:107/114 makes Claude Code send after every tool (PostToolUse). use-native-host.ts:63 does the same on each usage event with a conversationId. App.tsx:34, SessionGrid.tsx:7, SessionTile.tsx:60, StatusBar.tsx:61 and Toasts.tsx:16 subscribe without a selector; use-native-host.ts:115-117 then stringifies the workspace.
- **What goes wrong:** Eight channels with subagents finish dozens of tools a second. Each one re-renders App, the grid, all tiles, the status bar and toasts, and re-serializes the workspace, although no session changed. With 2 channels the renderer is idle (69.5 CPU-s in 16 h, measured); with 8 busy channels this becomes steady UI-thread load that lengthens any other stall. Cost per render is an estimate.
- **Suggested fix:** Return `state` from applyEvent and from the onUsage update when no session object changed (as clearAttention already does), and give App, SessionGrid, SessionTile, StatusBar and Toasts selectors (useShallow) so persistenceStatus and unrelated fields do not re-render them.
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed in code. applyEvent (workspace-store.ts:199-209) returns { sessions: state.sessions.map(...) } for every event except data, so the array is new even when every session object is unchanged; clearAttention at 195-198 shows the guarded form. 'answered' events come from Claude Code's PostToolUse and Stop hooks (attention.ts:107-114, pty-manager.ts:126-130), a phone's input (pty-manager.ts:156) and a chat approval closing (pty-manager.ts:283). The window's own keystrokes do not send one, so typing is not affected. use-native-host.ts:63 also builds a new array on every usage event that has a conversationId. App.tsx:34, SessionGrid.tsx:7, SessionTile.tsx:60, StatusBar.tsx:61, Toasts.tsx:15 and Topbar.tsx:49 subscribe to the whole store, so all re-render, and use-native-host.ts:115-118 serializes the workspace on each change. One effect the finding missed: a chat channel's tile re-renders its ChatView, which re-parses the whole thread. The impact is small and was not measured in the app: the saved workspace is about 1.2 KB, a tile's render is a few passes over the usage list, and tools finish a few times a second at most rather than dozens. On this computer the window's process has used 0.13% of a core. It is a real inefficiency with a two-line fix, not a cause of 1-3 s stops.
- **Checker's measurements:** Renderer process pid 8676: 77.7 CPU-s (61.7 user, 16 kernel) in 16.3 h with two terminal channels, about 0.13% of one core. Saved workspace snapshot in storage.db: 1,218 bytes. Per-render cost in the running app not measured (no DOM test library is installed and Trace may not be driven).

#### A covered or minimized window throttles terminal parsing and acknowledgements, which can pause CLIs and leave a backlog

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-renderer-background-throttling`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:418`
- **What the code does:** createWindow's webPreferences (418-423) leave backgroundThrottling at its default (on). xterm parses writes in 12 ms slices behind setTimeout (WriteBuffer.ts:28,124,238) and acks go out on a 50 ms timer (terminals.ts:213). pty-manager.ts:393-395 pauses a CLI at 1 MB unacknowledged and resumes after 5 s regardless.
- **What goes wrong:** Trace is minimized or fully covered while a channel prints heavily. Chromium limits hidden-page timers to one a second (one a minute for chained timers after 5 minutes, as I understand Chromium; not tested here), so parsing and acks lag, the CLI is paused in 5 s steps, and on return the window works through the backlog. No output-acknowledgement-stalled event is in host-health, so the 5 s path has not fired recently.
- **Blocking time:** One channel paused up to 5 s per MB while hidden; catch-up jank in the window on return. Estimated, not observed; no stall event recorded in the last 4 days.
- **Suggested fix:** Set backgroundThrottling: false for the main window (terminals must keep up while hidden), or acknowledge on receipt when document.hidden and let xterm catch up; add a pty-manager test with a slow acker.
- **Checker's notes:** The code facts hold. createWindow's webPreferences (main.ts:418-423) set no backgroundThrottling, so Electron's default (on) applies; the only place that sets it to false is the hidden screen-capture window (screen-capture.ts:31), and main.ts adds no command-line switch that turns throttling off. xterm 6.0.0 WriteBuffer.ts parses in 12 ms slices (line 28) behind setTimeout (lines 124 and 238); acknowledgements wait on a 50 ms timer unless 64 KB is pending (terminals.ts:213); pty-manager.ts:393-395 pauses a CLI above 1 MB unacknowledged and resumes after 5 s, recording output-acknowledgement-stalled (main.ts:158). The consequence is not established. It applies only while the window is hidden or minimized, so the person cannot see it as a stop; the cost on return is a backlog of at most about 1 MB per channel, which xterm works through in 12 ms slices with the UI responsive between them. An agent's terminal output is usually far below what even a throttled window can parse, and host-health has no stall event in four days, so the 5 s path has not fired. Whether Chromium treats a fully covered Trace window as hidden on this Windows build, and the exact hidden-timer rate, were not tested. Setting backgroundThrottling: false is a reasonable hardening for a terminal app, but this is not shown to produce the reported freezes.
- **Checker's measurements:** host-health.json: 0 output-acknowledgement-stalled events among the 100 stored (2026-11-14 to 2026-11-18 UTC). Search for backgroundThrottling and the related Chromium switches in electron/, src/ and scripts/: one match, screen-capture.ts:31. No timing of a hidden window was taken (Trace may not be driven).

#### Notices inside a tile change the terminal's height, so each approval prompt resizes the CLI twice

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `frz-renderer-notice-resizes-pty`
- **Where:** `src/components/SessionTile.tsx:162`
- **What the code does:** The attention banner (162), error rows (163-165), TaskStrip, LiveTipStrip and PastedImages (169-171) are in-flow children of the flex column (styles.css:31) around .terminal-frame (flex:1, styles.css:42). TerminalViewport.tsx:11-13 refits on every ResizeObserver callback; terminals.ts:157 sends each new size to the PTY (pty-manager.ts:438-439 flushes and resizes).
- **What goes wrong:** Claude asks for approval: the banner appears, the terminal loses about two rows, the CLI is resized and redraws; answering removes the banner and it redraws again. A coaching tip after each prompt does the same. These logs show Claude Code in the alternate screen, where a redraw is one screen (cheap); a CLI that reprints its history on resize would flicker for longer. One channel.
- **Suggested fix:** Overlay the attention banner and tip strip on the terminal frame (absolute, like .tr-tile-detail) or reserve their height, and debounce fit() so a resize is sent only when the size has been stable for ~100 ms.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. SessionTile.tsx:162 renders .session-attention as an in-flow child of the flex column (styles.css:31); .terminal-frame is flex:1 with min-height 0/70px (styles.css:42, workbench.css:63), so it shrinks by the banner's ~34 px (workbench.css:278: 6 px padding x2, 14 px text, two borders). TerminalViewport.tsx:11-14 calls fit() on every ResizeObserver callback with no debounce; terminals.ts:157 forwards each xterm resize to resizeSession, and pty-manager.ts:438-439 flushes and calls pty.resize. The banner is set on each 'attention' event and cleared on 'answered' or the person's keystroke (workspace-store.ts:196-204), so one approval prompt gives two PTY resizes. LiveTipStrip (tile.css:196, flex-shrink 0, up to 132 px) and session-error rows behave the same; the tip strip only when live coaching is enabled. Only the row count changes (columns stay), so xterm does no scrollback reflow; cost is one CLI redraw per resize in one channel. Not a main-process stall and not the reported 1-3 s freeze. Severity low is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** Not timed. Geometry from CSS: banner about 34 px; one terminal row is fontSize x 1.45 (terminals.ts:111), so the terminal loses 1-2 rows per banner.

#### Trace's status line starts curl on every Claude Code status refresh only to re-send a size it already has

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `frz-renderer-statusline-curl`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/attention.ts:113`
- **What the code does:** claudeAttentionSettings adds statusLine: `curl -s -m 2 -X POST --data-binary @- <url>/status` when the person has no status line (main.ts:168-169). This computer's Claude settings have none (checked keys only), so both terminal channels get it. The value is used once: telemetry.ts:102 ignores a size equal to the one stored.
- **What goes wrong:** While a Claude terminal works, Claude Code runs the status-line command through its shell on each refresh, so a shell and curl.exe start repeatedly per channel for the whole run. On Windows each start costs tens of milliseconds and a security scan. It does not block Trace's main process, but it adds machine-wide process churn with 8 channels. Rate not measured: estimate.
- **Suggested fix:** Get the window size without a status line where possible (the chat path already reports it; a model-to-window table as fallback), or have the endpoint answer with a marker and use a script that exits before starting curl once the size was accepted.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described: attention.ts:113 adds a statusLine command that runs curl with -m 2 against <url>/status when main.ts:168 finds no status line of the person's own and the channel is not a chat; telemetry.ts:102 drops a size equal to the stored one, so repeats do nothing. Live check: both claude.exe processes whose parent is Trace.exe (pid 27468) carry 'statusLine' in their --settings argument. What is not established is the rate, which is Claude Code's behaviour, not Trace's: in two samples (25 s and 40 s, polling about every 22 ms) I saw no curl.exe start at all, although one of the two Trace Claude terminals used 3.69 CPU-seconds in the 40 s window. A 17 ms process can slip between polls, but five or more starts would very likely have shown, so the 'repeatedly for the whole run' churn looks small at least in that state. The work happens in the CLI's process tree; Trace's main process only answers a tiny loopback POST. Also note the size is not strictly needed once: a model switch inside the terminal can change the window. Low at most; a design trade-off more than a defect.
- **Checker's measurements:** curl.exe start-to-exit posting a small body to a loopback test server: median 17 ms direct, 32 ms through cmd (15 runs each). curl.exe starts observed machine-wide: 0 in 25 s and 0 in 40 s (1,187 and 1,673 polls); CPU used by the two Trace Claude terminals in the 40 s: 0.86 s and 3.69 s.

#### After each start the status bar's usage read parses 16 MB of CLI logs per pass on the main process, about 15 times

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `frz-renderer-dayusage-catchup`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/day-usage.ts:34`
- **What the code does:** days() reads up to READ_BUDGET (16 MiB, line 15) of transcripts synchronously and JSON.parses each line; day-usage-store.ts:18 asks again 500 ms later until complete; StatusBar.tsx:70-71 also asks every 15 s and 2 s after usage changes. Measured on this computer's logs (4 homes, 147 files, 240 MB in the 60-day window): 61 ms per catch-up pass, 8 ms per steady pass.
- **What goes wrong:** Trace starts. For the first 8 seconds or so the main process spends about 61 ms of every 500 ms reading logs, on top of the known one-off Drive comparison, so typing into a channel started straight away stutters. Later passes cost about 8 ms and are not a freeze.
- **Blocking time:** Main process (whole window): 61 ms per pass, about 15 passes 500 ms apart after each start (measured against real logs with a copy of the read loop); 8 ms every 15 s afterwards.
- **Suggested fix:** Read the backlog with fs.promises in small chunks (or in a worker) and keep the per-file offsets in a small cache file so a restart does not re-read 240 MB.
- **Checker's notes:** Mechanism confirmed, but the reported sizes are about six times too small. day-usage.ts:31-34 reads up to 16 MiB synchronously per call and JSON.parses every line; day-usage-store.ts:18 asks again 500 ms later until complete; StatusBar.tsx:70-71 asks every 15 s and 2 s after usage settles; main.ts:860 also calls totals() every minute and main.ts:606 for the phone. Every start re-reads everything because offsets live only in memory (day-usage.ts:25). With a faithful copy of candidates/read/line run against the real logs I get 340 files and 1,440 MB in the 60-day window (default Claude home 1,202 MB including 565 MB of subagent logs, two profiles 127 MB, Codex 111 MB), not 147 files / 240 MB. So catch-up is 91 passes, not 15: about 45-50 s after each start during which the main process blocks roughly 50 ms of every 550 ms. Steady passes cost 20-40 ms (readdir + stat of 340 files, then a second stat each), not 8 ms. Each block is short, so this is jitter after start and a small recurring hitch, not the reported 1-3 s freeze; severity stays low, but it is longer-lived than reported and grows with log volume. Not measured: a cold file cache after a reboot, where 1.4 GB comes off disk on the main thread.
- **Checker's measurements:** Two runs of the copied read loop on this computer (Node 24, warm cache): 91 passes until complete, 1,440.2 MB read, 278,347 lines parsed; catch-up pass mean 51.4 / 53.5 ms, range 33-94 ms, total 4.6 / 4.9 s of blocking; directory scan alone 12-26 ms per pass; ten steady passes 18-41 ms.

#### A storage or memory notice stays as a red banner until restart and cannot be dismissed

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `frz-renderer-logerror-sticky`
- **Where:** `src/runtime/use-native-host.ts:92`
- **What the code does:** onStorageError sets logError; nothing in src/ ever clears it (workspace-store.ts:163 is the only null). App.tsx:42 shows it as .persistence-alert above the workspace. main.ts:519 sends auto-memory notices through the same reportStorageError; host-health holds three auto-memory-notice events.
- **What goes wrong:** One auto-memory notice or a single failed log write arrives. The banner appears above the channels, shrinks every tile (each terminal is refitted and its CLI resized once) and stays for the rest of the session, however long ago the problem ended. The person cannot close it.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the banner a dismiss button, clear logError when a later save or append succeeds, and send auto-memory notices as a toast instead of a storage error.
- **Checker's notes:** use-native-host.ts:92 sets logError from every storageError message; a search of src/ finds no other write to it besides the initial null at workspace-store.ts:163, so it is never cleared. App.tsx:42 renders it as .persistence-alert above every view with no close control. main.ts:519 routes auto-memory notices through reportStorageError, alongside real storage and telemetry errors (main.ts:136, 141, 154, 176, 181, 183, 504). auto-memory.ts:208 de-duplicates per notice id per app run, so each distinct notice shows once, but it then stays until restart, and a later message silently replaces an earlier one (single slot). host-health.json on this computer has three auto-memory-notice events (2026-11-15 15:00, 16:33, 21:06 UTC), so the person has seen this. In the workspace view main-shell is a non-scrolling flex column (shell.css:5), so the banner (padding 12 px, margin 14 px top and bottom, styles.css:98) takes height from every tile and each terminal is refitted once. UX only; low is right. Minor: the banner uses the 'limit' colour token, not necessarily red.
- **Checker's measurements:** host-health.json: 100 events from 2026-11-14 to now, 6 host starts, 3 auto-memory-notice events. No timing involved.

#### 23 GPU-process crash dumps from 2026-11-15 sit in the real data folder with no matching health record

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: improvement. ID: `frz-renderer-gpu-dumps-unrecorded`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/discovery.ts:89`
- **What the code does:** ~/.coding-hub/crash-dumps/reports holds 23 dumps (45 MB) written 10:39-12:44 local on 11-15, each annotated ptype gpu-process, _productName ai-coding-hub, _version 1.21.3, NVIDIA driver module. host-health.json covers that period and lists no child-process-gone then (only one at 08:25 local, exit 34, which has no dump).
- **What goes wrong:** Either a test or source run shared the person's real storage folder while crashing the GPU process on purpose (bursts 16 s apart suggest a script; its health records would be overwritten by the installed app's in-memory list), or the installed app's GPU process crashed 23 times unrecorded. I could not tell which from the files. In both cases the evidence misleads the next audit.
- **Suggested fix:** Confirm where the dumps came from (the 11-15 GPU work). Make every script that crashes a process refuse to run without CODING_HUB_STORAGE_DIR, record the app version and pid with each health event, and prune or list old dumps in Settings.
- **Checker's notes:** The dumps exist as described (23 files, about 45 MB, 10:39-12:44 local on 11-15) and host-health.json has no matching record. I resolved the open question by reading the dumps: every one is from node_modules\electron\dist\electron.exe in a source checkout (20 from ~\Coding-Hub, 3 from this repository), type gpu-process, with CODING_HUB_STORAGE_DIR set to a Temp\coding-hub-gpu-* folder and CLAUDECODE=1 in its environment: they are scripts/gpu-crash-smoke.cjs runs started by a Claude Code agent inside a Trace channel. The installed app's GPU process did not crash 23 times (that alternative is refuted), and the test did not share the real storage folder (the auditor's other guess is wrong in mechanism). The dumps crossed over because hostEnvironment (discovery.ts:89) hands Trace's own CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME to everything a channel starts; the test app's GPU process starts before its main process calls crashReporter.start (main.ts:495, inside whenReady), so it reports to the installed Trace's crash handler, which writes into ~/.coding-hub/crash-dumps. The last step (a Chromium child taking the pipe name from the environment) is from my knowledge of Chromium, not from this repository; the evidence fits it exactly. The proposed fix (require CODING_HUB_STORAGE_DIR) would not help: remove CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME in hostEnvironment and in the smoke script's env. Real effect: any Electron app an agent starts from a channel can drop crash dumps into Trace's folder, which is never pruned. Low.
- **Checker's measurements:** All 23 dumps carry the same CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME=\\.\pipe\crashpad_35628_... (the Trace main process of 11-15). Today my own shell, started under Trace, has CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME=\\.\pipe\crashpad_27468_..., and pid 27468 is ...\AI Coding Hub\Trace\Trace.exe (started 11-17 04:45), confirming the variable reaches channel processes. Dump folder: 23 files, 45,396 KB.

### main.ts, preload and IPC contracts (9)

#### A freeze leaves no record: nothing measures a stalled main process or an unresponsive window, and the 100-event health log is mostly channel starts

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: improvement. ID: `main-ipc-no-stall-record`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:75`
- **What the code does:** main.ts:75-81 records only uncaught exceptions and child-process-gone. No 'unresponsive' handler or event-loop timer exists anywhere in electron/ (searched). host-health.ts:31 keeps the last 100 events; in this computer's file 84 are cli-started/cli-exited, so it reaches back four days and holds one 'GPU: crashed, exit 34' (2026-11-15).
- **What goes wrong:** Trace stops for 1-3 s. Crash diagnostics show nothing, so a blocked main process cannot be told from a blocked window or a restarting GPU process. About eight more channel starts push the one GPU event out of the log.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a main-process timer that records 'host-stall' with its length when it fires more than 500 ms late; record the window's 'unresponsive'/'responsive' events and unhandledRejection; keep cli-started/cli-exited in their own list so rare events stay.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. electron/main.ts:75-81 records only uncaughtExceptionMonitor and child-process-gone; a search of electron/, src/, shared/ finds no 'unresponsive' handler, no unhandledRejection handler in the main app (only screen-service.ts:39), no monitorEventLoopDelay or late-timer check, and no renderer long-task observer. host-health.ts:31 keeps the last 100 events and main.ts:131-132 writes cli-started/cli-exited into the same list. So a 1-3 s freeze leaves nothing behind, and the one GPU event will be pushed out. Two corrections: (1) the GPU event sits at index 24, so it takes 25 more events (about 12 channel start+exit pairs), not about eight starts; (2) the suggested window 'unresponsive' event would not catch the reported stalls: Chromium raises it only after a hang of several seconds (from memory, not checked against Electron 44 source), so the useful parts are the main-process late-timer record and a renderer-side long-task record. Severity lowered from high to medium: it is a diagnostics gap, not a defect that itself harms the person, but it is the reason the reported freeze cannot be attributed today.
- **Checker's measurements:** ~\.coding-hub\host-health.json (read only): 100 events from 2026-11-14T07:15Z to 2026-11-18T04:31Z; 42 cli-started + 42 cli-exited = 84, 6 host-start, 6 host-stop, 3 auto-memory-notice, 1 child-process-gone (GPU: crashed, exit 34, 2026-11-15T16:25:42Z) at index 24. 0 renderer-process-gone, 0 stall events of any kind.

#### Electron's default menu is still active: Ctrl+R reloads the window and Ctrl+W closes it, and both stop every running channel without asking

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `main-ipc-default-menu-reload`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:417`
- **What the code does:** No Menu.setApplicationMenu call exists in electron/ (searched), so the default menu's shortcuts work behind autoHideMenuBar (main.ts:417). A menu reload is not a will-navigate (:426); did-navigate (:460-463) then stops every channel and sign-in. The close handler (:465-482) does the same. Not run: Trace was in use.
- **What goes wrong:** With focus outside a terminal (a chat box, a button), the person presses Ctrl+R or Ctrl+W, both everyday terminal keys. All running agents stop with no question; Ctrl+W also quits Trace. Ctrl+Shift+I opens DevTools in the installed app.
- **Suggested fix:** On Windows and Linux call Menu.setApplicationMenu(null) before the window is made (on a Mac keep a menu without Reload), or cancel those keys in before-input-event. Add a desktop-smoke step that presses Ctrl+R while a channel runs.
- **Checker's notes:** No Menu.setApplicationMenu, setMenu, removeMenu or before-input-event exists anywhere in electron/ (the only Menu use is the screen-service tray, screen-service.ts:59), so Electron's default menu stays behind autoHideMenuBar (main.ts:417). The probe shows its Reload is not stopped by will-navigate (:426); did-navigate (:460-463) then calls accounts.stopOwner and manager.stopOwner with no question. Close goes through the close handler (:465-482), which stops every channel and destroys the window without asking unless a workflow or delivery is running, and window-all-closed (:868) then quits. The renderer cancels none of these keys: its key handlers cover Alt+1-8, Ctrl+Shift+F, F1, Ctrl+Shift+P and G-sequences only (src/components/AccessibilityControls.tsx:11-24, CommandMenu.tsx:44-47, runtime/view-shortcuts.ts:13-21). With focus in a terminal xterm consumes plain Ctrl+R and Ctrl+W on Windows, so the trigger is focus in a chat box, field or button, as the finding says. The one link not exercised is a physical key press reaching the hidden menu's accelerator in the installed app; that is standard Electron behaviour on Windows. Medium stands: one stray key stops up to eight agents mid-turn.
- **Checker's measurements:** Hidden probe app (not Trace) run with the repository's Electron 44.9.3 binary and its own profile folder under <scratch>\agents\verify-main-ipc\menu-probe: with no menu set and autoHideMenuBar true, Menu.getApplicationMenu() is present with Reload (CmdOrCtrl+R), Force Reload (Shift+CmdOrCtrl+R), Toggle Developer Tools (Ctrl+Shift+I), Close (CommandOrControl+W), Minimize (Ctrl+M). Running the Reload item reloaded the page with 0 will-navigate events although the handler cancels all of them (did-navigate count 1 -> 2). A synthetic Ctrl+R sent to the hidden, unfocused window did not reload (inconclusive for a hidden window). Real key presses in the installed Trace were not tried: it was in use.

#### Today's usage is read from the CLIs' logs on the main process: 89 blocking passes after every start, then one every 15 s

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `main-ipc-dayusage-main-thread`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:704`
- **What the code does:** main.ts:704 ('usage-today') and :860 (60 s interval) call dayUsage.totals(), which reads up to 16 MB of logs with readSync and JSON.parse per call (day-usage.ts:15,34,78-91). The status bar asks every 15 s (StatusBar.tsx:70) and every 500 ms until complete (day-usage-store.ts:18). Offsets are kept in memory only.
- **What goes wrong:** This computer has 340 logs (1.44 GB) from the last 60 days. After every start the main process runs 89 passes 500 ms apart; typing and channel output wait during each one. Afterwards a pass runs every 15 s and every 60 s.
- **Blocking time:** Measured with the repo's DayUsage class in Node 24 on the real logs (outside Trace): 36-119 ms per pass, 89 passes, 6.8 s in total after each start; then 33-45 ms per pass. Blocks the main process.
- **Suggested fix:** Read the logs in a worker thread (or async, in small slices), answer usage-today from the last result, and save offsets and day totals so a restart does not read 1.4 GB again.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. main.ts:704 (usage-today), :705 (usage-days), :606 (phone usage) and :860 (60 s interval) call DayUsage.days() synchronously on the main process; day-usage.ts:34 reads up to 16 MB per call with openSync/readSync and JSON.parse per line (:84-87), and every call also re-lists and stats every .jsonl (:63-75). Offsets live only in the in-memory Map (:25), so each start re-reads everything. The renderer asks on mount, every 15 s, 2 s after each usage update (StatusBar.tsx:70-71) and again 500 ms after every incomplete answer (day-usage-store.ts:18). My numbers agree in shape with the auditor's (91 passes vs 89; 20-79 ms vs 36-119 ms). Severity lowered to low: no single block exceeded 0.08 s here (0.12 s in the auditor's run), so this is about 45-50 s of repeated short hitches after each start and about 20 ms every 15 s afterwards. It cannot by itself produce the reported 1-3 s freeze; it adds to any other main-process work in that first minute. Not measured inside Trace itself.
- **Checker's measurements:** Replica of day-usage.ts run in Node 24 outside Trace on the real logs (<scratch>\agents\verify-main-ipc\dayusage.mjs), warm file cache: 340 candidate logs, 1,440 MB read, 91 passes until complete, 4.4 s total; per pass min 20 ms, median 47 ms, p90 61 ms, max 79 ms. Steady state afterwards: 18.5-23.7 ms per pass (680 statSync + 199 readdirSync calls per pass, no file reads). Cold-cache cost after a reboot not measured.

#### Trace passes its crash-handler pipe to every CLI and terminal, so other Electron apps' crash dumps land in ~/.coding-hub/crash-dumps, which is never pruned

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: security. ID: `main-ipc-crashpad-pipe-leak`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/discovery.ts:89`
- **What the code does:** main.ts:495 starts the crash reporter; hostEnvironment() (discovery.ts:89) copies process.env, CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME included, to every launch. A shell under Trace here has it, naming Trace's main PID. ~/.coding-hub/crash-dumps holds 23 dumps (45 MB) from GPU smoke runs whose own storage was a temp folder. No code deletes dumps.
- **What goes wrong:** An agent or terminal in a channel starts an Electron app (npm run dev, a smoke test). Crashes of its early child processes go to Trace's handler: 2 MB dumps holding that app's environment variables collect in the person's data folder and stay.
- **Suggested fix:** Remove CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME in hostEnvironment() (beside withoutAgentSession), with a unit test; at start delete crash-dumps/reports files older than about 14 days.
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed end to end. discovery.ts:89 copies all of process.env; withoutAgentSession (:44-47) removes only Claude Code session variables, and pty-manager.ts:206/215 passes the result to every channel. main.ts:495 starts the crash reporter, which sets the pipe variable in Trace's own environment. scripts/gpu-crash-smoke.cjs:40 spreads process.env and sets CODING_HUB_STORAGE_DIR to a temp folder, yet its GPU-process dumps are in the installed Trace's ~/.coding-hub/crash-dumps: the smoke app's GPU process started before that app's own crashReporter.start and used the inherited pipe. No code in the repository removes dumps (only main.ts:495 and UI text in src/components/CrashDiagnostics.tsx:12 mention the folder). Two cautions: Crashpad's own handler prunes its database by age and size (about one year or 128 MB, from memory, not verified for Electron 44), so 'never pruned' is true of Trace's code but growth is probably bounded; and the dumps stay on the person's own disk and are never uploaded, so this is misrouted diagnostics and disk clutter rather than an exposure to anyone else. Severity lowered to low.
- **Checker's measurements:** This audit shell (a child of a Trace channel) has CHROME_CRASHPAD_PIPE_NAME=\\.\pipe\crashpad_27468_...; PID 27468 is the installed Trace main process, whose crash handler (PID 59068) runs with --database=~\.coding-hub\crash-dumps. That folder holds 23 .dmp files, 45 MB, all dated 2026-11-15 10:39-12:44 local. Three dumps opened read-only: each is ptype gpu-process with --user-data-dir=...\Temp\coding-hub-gpu-XXXXXX\electron-profile (the temp root scripts/gpu-crash-smoke.cjs:37 creates). host-health has only one child-process-gone for Trace itself, at 08:25 local that day.

#### hostEnvironment() runs reg.exe twice with execFileSync on the main process, cached for only 5 seconds

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `main-ipc-sync-reg-exe`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/discovery.ts:53`
- **What the code does:** readRegistryPath (discovery.ts:53) uses execFileSync with a 3 s timeout, twice per hostEnvironment(), cached 5 s (:81-86). Main-process callers: each channel start (pty-manager.ts:206), each git call (repository/git.ts:17), plan limits (plan-limits.ts:79), task details (tasks.ts:71), discoverEngines. windowsSudoMode (pty-manager.ts:39) is the same pattern; sudo.exe is absent here.
- **What goes wrong:** A Git review refresh, a channel start or the 5-minute plan-limit read stops the main process while two reg.exe processes run. With CPU or disk busy (agents building), process starts slow down; the worst case is two 3 s timeouts.
- **Blocking time:** Measured here while idle (Node 24, outside Trace): 30-45 ms per uncached call. Under load not measured; bounded at 6 s. Blocks the main process.
- **Suggested fix:** Read the registry with async execFile, keep the last value for about 60 s and refresh it in the background, so hostEnvironment() never waits.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: readRegistryPath (discovery.ts:51-56) uses execFileSync with a 3 s timeout for each of two keys, cached for 5 s (:81-86), and hostEnvironment() is called on the main process at each channel start (pty-manager.ts:206), each Git call (repository/git.ts:17, repository-state.ts:17,48, delivery.ts:28,34), task details (tasks.ts:71), plan limits (plan-limits.ts:79), diagnostics, verification, project checks and discovery. So at most once per 5 s the main process blocks about 33 ms; with open tasks the 30 s task watch (src/state/task-store.ts:230) alone triggers it. One correction to the evidence: sudo.exe is present here, so shellProgram (pty-manager.ts:55) does run windowsSudoMode on every Terminal channel start, a third blocking reg.exe of about 18 ms (it fails because the key is missing and returns 0). The 6 s worst case needs reg.exe to hang twice, which I could not produce; slower process starts under load are plausible but unmeasured. Low is right: real, cheap to fix, and far below the reported 1-3 s.
- **Checker's measurements:** Node 24 outside Trace, machine otherwise in normal use (<scratch>\agents\verify-main-ipc\reg.mjs): the two PATH queries together took 29.7-42 ms, median 33 ms, over 15 runs. The sudo query (pty-manager.ts:39) took 15.5-27.3 ms over 8 runs. C:\Windows\System32\sudo.exe exists on this computer (970,272 bytes) and the Sudo registry key is missing. Behaviour under heavy CPU or disk load not measured.

#### When the window's process dies, every channel and sign-in terminal is stopped, although the CLIs run in the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: improvement. ID: `main-ipc-renderer-gone-stops-all`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:446`
- **What the code does:** render-process-gone (main.ts:443-456) calls manager.stopOwner(owner) and accounts.stopOwner(owner), then offers 'Reload workspace'. The runs live in PtyManager in the main process, the window's webContents id survives a reload, and RemoteServer already keeps a replay of each channel for phones.
- **What goes wrong:** The window's process crashes or runs out of memory while agents work. All of them are killed mid-turn; the person reloads, starts each channel again and pays for the resumed context a second time.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the runs alive when the renderer goes. After the reload let the window ask for manager.running() and a replay, as a phone does; stop them only if the person chooses Close.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described and deliberate: main.ts:443-456 records renderer-process-gone, then stops every run and sign-in owned by the window (manager.stopOwner, accounts.stopOwner) before offering 'Reload workspace', and the dialog text says so ('Coding and login terminals have been stopped'). The premises hold: PTYs live in PtyManager in the main process (electron/services/cli/pty-manager.ts), PtyManager.running() exists (:143), RemoteServer keeps a per-channel screen replay for phones (remote-server.ts:178, 450-458), and a webContents keeps its id across reload, so keeping runs alive is feasible. It is a design improvement rather than a defect, and there is no sign it has happened here: the health log holds no renderer crash. What the finding leaves out is the work involved: after a reload the window restores idle tiles and has no path to re-adopt running sessions, and flow-control acknowledgements (pty-manager.ts:64-67) would need resetting. Severity lowered to low: rare event, high cost when it happens (all agents killed mid-turn), no evidence of occurrence on this computer.
- **Checker's measurements:** host-health.json on this computer: 0 renderer-process-gone events in its 100 events (2026-11-14 to 2026-11-18 UTC, six starts, 42 channel starts).

#### Opening Trace again while the old process is still shutting down shows nothing

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `main-ipc-relaunch-while-quitting`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:489`
- **What the code does:** second-instance (main.ts:489) returns when the window is gone. After the window is destroyed, before-quit (:869-891) keeps the process and its single-instance lock (:490) while channels stop and autoMemory.drain waits up to 10 s for session summaries (:889).
- **What goes wrong:** The person closes Trace with agents running and opens it again within a few seconds. The new process fails the lock and quits; the old one has no window to show. Nothing appears, then the old process exits.
- **Suggested fix:** In second-instance, when there is no window and quitting is true, remember the request and call app.relaunch() just before the final app.quit().
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. second-instance (main.ts:489) returns when window is null or destroyed; the second process quits silently at :490 because the first still holds the lock. On close the window is destroyed only after all channels stop (:470-480), then window-all-closed (:868) calls app.quit and before-quit (:869-891) stops the rest and awaits autoMemory.drain(10000) (:889) before closing storage and quitting. Each summary needs the run's last telemetry read plus git status and git log (auto-memory.ts:319-341), so with agents running the window-less period is real, and the health log shows it reached the 10 s cap twice in six shutdowns here. Opening Trace in that period shows nothing; a second attempt after the old process exits works. Low is right: a confusing few seconds, no data loss. Side observation from the same numbers, not verified further: a drain that runs to its full 10 s cap may mean a session summary did not finish before storage closed.
- **Checker's measurements:** host-health.json, time from the last cli-exited to host-stop at the six recorded shutdowns: 10.4 s, 1.0 s, 8.7 s, 0.03 s, 0.09 s, 0.34 s. host-stop is written at main.ts:890 right after autoMemory.drain(10000), so two of six shutdowns kept the process alive about 9-10 s after its channels had stopped. Whether the window was already destroyed for that whole gap is inferred from the code order, not observed.

#### A linked computer's update request to an unsigned Mac copy checks only for running channels, not other long-running jobs

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `main-ipc-mac-script-update-busy`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:301`
- **What the code does:** remoteUpdate (main.ts:295-305) checks only manager.hasSessions before startMacUpdate. The updater's own check (:754) also covers sign-ins, project setup, project checks, verification, backups, delivery, workflows and worktrees. lastFlushSucceeded is not checked and updateRestart is not set.
- **What goes wrong:** Another computer asks a Mac copy without the Developer ID to update while a backup, verification run or sign-in is in progress there. The install script quits Trace in the middle of it (AGENTS.md rule 9).
- **Suggested fix:** Use one shared busy check for UpdateService and remoteUpdate, require a successful workspace flush, and set updateRestart while the script runs.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as claimed. remoteUpdate (main.ts:295-305) takes the script path only when the updater is disabled on a packaged Mac (:296-297); there it checks only manager.hasSessions (:301), awaits flushWorkspace without reading lastFlushSucceeded (:302) and never sets updateRestart, then spawns the detached script (:303). The self-updating path is stricter: UpdateService.remoteInstall (update-service.ts:92) uses the full list at main.ts:754, and prepareRestart (:759-762) sets updateRestart, rechecks and requires a successful flush. Reachable in shipped builds: the 1.21.1 commit message (74be8fe) says unsigned Mac releases use exactly this script. Not reachable on this Windows computer itself (:297 throws). Two corrections that narrow the impact: (1) the script quits Trace with osascript (mac-script-update.ts:71), which goes through before-quit; that handler refuses while delivery, workflows or worktrees run (main.ts:872), so the script gives up after 90 s (:72-73) and Trace is left as it was, with a dialog on the Mac and a wrong 'Installing' answer already sent. Those three are therefore not cut off. (2) before-quit flushes the workspace again (:874), so the unchecked flush at :302 loses nothing more than a normal quit. What is really cut off: sign-ins (accounts.close, :874), verification (cancel, :890), project checks (stop, :890), project setup, and any channel started during the DMG download, because nothing refuses new work in that window. Backup creation is mostly synchronous (backups.ts:82-99), so it can only be interrupted at its awaits (flush, file dialogs). Same outcome as a person quitting by hand, but unattended. Low is right; I could not confirm whether the person's own Mac is an unsigned copy.

#### A workspace flush that fails validation is never answered: closing waits 2 seconds and updates and backups refuse without saying why

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `main-ipc-flush-invalid-waits`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:794`
- **What the code does:** finishWorkspaceFlush (main.ts:792-798) throws INVALID_FLUSH before the try/finally that resolves pendingFlush when the payload fails savedWorkspaceSchema. flushWorkspace then waits its 2 s timer (:383) and lastFlushSucceeded stays false (:379).
- **What goes wrong:** The window's workspace does not pass the schema (for example kept Round Table images over the 6,000,000-character limit, schemas.ts:40). Closing waits 2 s, and 'Restart and install' and backups answer that the workspace could not be saved, with no reason. The trigger was not reproduced.
- **Suggested fix:** When the requestId and owner match, always resolve pendingFlush, and return the schema's message as INVALID_WORKSPACE so the person sees what is wrong.
- **Checker's notes:** The mechanism is real: finishWorkspaceFlushSchema embeds savedWorkspaceSchema (schemas.ts:107), so an invalid workspace makes parsed.success false and main.ts:794 throws INVALID_FLUSH before the try/finally at :795-796 that resolves pendingFlush. The flush then ends only on the 2 s timer (:383), lastFlushSucceeded stays false (:379), and the update (:762) and backup (:552 readyForBackup) refuse with generic messages. The renderer ignores the failed result (use-native-host.ts:106). But the finding's example trigger is guarded: the host never publishes waitingImages over the limit; keptImages replaces them with waitingImagesDropped (round-table.ts:91-94, review-chain.ts:121, KEEP_IMAGES = 6_000_000, same as schemas.ts:40), and rememberTable saves only what the host published (src/runtime/round-table.ts:133). A different trigger does exist in code: messages sent while a discussion table is busy are joined with no length cap (round-table.ts:153; review-chain.ts:283 likewise), each up to 200,000 characters (schemas.ts:125), while the saved 'waiting' field allows 200,000 in total (schemas.ts:40). That needs more than 200,000 characters of waiting text, which is rare, and I did not reproduce it in the running app. 'With no reason' is overstated: the ordinary autosave fails for the same workspace with INVALID_WORKSPACE and the schema's messages (workspace-storage.ts:62-63), shown in a persistent alert (App.tsx:42) and as 'Storage needs attention' in the status bar (StatusBar.tsx:98). Not a cause of the reported 1-3 s freezes: the timer does not block the main process, and this computer's workspace is valid. Impact is 2 s extra at close plus unhelpful update/backup wording in a rare state; low stands.
- **Checker's measurements:** Copy of this computer's storage.db (229 KB + 4.1 MB WAL) opened read-only: saved workspace is 1,218 characters, 2 sessions, 2 tiles, no Round Table record; savedWorkspaceSchema.safeParse passes in 3.7 ms. Synthetic flush payload with a 300,002-character roundTable.waiting (two 150,000-character messages joined with a blank line): finishWorkspaceFlushSchema.safeParse fails with 'workspace.sessions.0.roundTable.waiting: Too big: expected string to have <=200000 characters'. The wait itself is the fixed 2000 ms timer at main.ts:383.

### CLI and chat services (11)

#### Images attached to plan feedback are dropped, while the thread shows them as sent

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `cli-chat-plan-feedback-images-dropped`
- **Where:** `electron/services/chat/review-chain.ts:265`
- **What the code does:** At plan-review, send() calls `this.plan(leadRunId, false, text)` without the images; plan() (line 338) calls turn() with no images and the revision keeps text only. PtyManager then emits the person's message with `images: request.images.length` (pty-manager.ts:349). No test covers images at plan review.
- **What goes wrong:** A plan waits for approval. The person types "match this layout" with a screenshot. The planner receives only the text and revises the plan without the picture. The conversation shows the message with one image as delivered, so nothing tells the person it was lost.
- **Suggested fix:** Give plan() an images parameter, pass it to turn(), and keep the pictures with the owed revision so Continue or a blocked planner still gets them. Add a test for a table-send with images at plan-review.
- **Checker's notes:** review-chain.ts:265 calls this.plan(leadRunId, false, text) and drops the images argument. plan() (:329-339) has no images parameter, stores the text alone in chain.revision and calls turn() with no images. On approval the builder gets chain.images, which are the original task's pictures only (:333). The path is reachable: ChatView.tsx:84 sends table-send with the attached images at any step, and line 81 shows the message with its attachments before sending. pty-manager.ts:349 then reports the message with images: request.images.length. A message with only images is refused (TABLE_NOTE), so the loss needs text plus a picture. It applies only to a hierarchy table at plan review. I did not check the test files for coverage of this case.

#### Round Table and hierarchy steps hash every changed file synchronously on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `cli-chat-table-state-hash`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/repository-state.ts:45`
- **What the code does:** `stateOf: (cwd) => repositoryState(cwd)` runs at every hierarchy step (review-chain.ts:157, :530) and every Round Table round (round-table.ts:292, :308). repositoryState (workbench/repository-state.ts:27, :45) calls statSync, readFileSync and sha256 in a loop over every changed or untracked file, up to 5,000 files / 256 MB. Measured here in plain Node: 500 files 0.32 s, 2,000 files 1.7 s, 5,000 files 4.0 s.
- **What goes wrong:** A table works in a folder with many changed or untracked files (generated output not ignored, a large refactor). Each step start, and each answer that changes the findings record, blocks the main process that long: terminals stop drawing, input and IPC wait. Not confirmed that the person's folders had that many changed files; recent history holds 74 chat runs and 26 Codex runs.
- **Blocking time:** Main process. Measured in plain Node on this computer: about 0.65-0.8 ms per changed or untracked file per snapshot (0.3 s at 500 files, 1.7 s at 2,000, 4.0 s at the 5,000 limit). A hierarchy task takes roughly 8-10 snapshots.
- **Suggested fix:** Make repositoryState asynchronous (fs.promises with a yield every few files, or a worker), or build the hash from the size:mtime marks changedFiles already collects. Take the turn-start snapshot only for builder and reviewer turns; planner turns never use it (review-chain.ts:521).
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described. pty-manager.ts:379 wires stateOf to repositoryState. review-chain.ts:157 calls it at every turn, planner turns included, and :530 again when findings changed; round-table.ts:292 and :308 do the same. repository-state.ts:27 and :45 run statSync, readFileSync and sha256 in loops with no await between files, so the main process is blocked for the whole loop. The trigger is absent on this computer: every project folder has at most 4 changed files, which costs about 2 ms. Half a second needs roughly 1,500 changed, non-ignored files. Above 5,000 files, or 1 MB of status output, it returns null after the stat loop only (about 0.1 s). So this is a real latent stall but not the reported one; high is inflated. The defect itself is in repository-state.ts, not at the cited wiring line.
- **Checker's measurements:** Same loops in plain Node on this computer, 4 KB files: 4 files 2 ms, 100 files 36 ms, 500 files 0.17 s, 2,000 files 0.68 s, 5,000 files 1.7 s per snapshot (about 0.33 ms per file, half the auditor's figure); one 200 MB file 0.14 s. Changed or untracked files in the nine Git folders under ~\Claude\Projects today: 4, 3, 2 and six with 0.

#### hostEnvironment() runs reg.exe twice synchronously on the main process whenever its 5-second cache has expired

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `cli-chat-host-env-sync-registry`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/discovery.ts:53`
- **What the code does:** readRegistryPath uses execFileSync('reg.exe', timeout 3000) for two keys; currentRegistryPath (lines 81-86) caches 5 s. hostEnvironment() is called per Git command (repository/git.ts:17), per channel start (pty-manager.ts:206), by repository-state, tasks, accounts and the model catalog. windowsSudoMode (pty-manager.ts:39) adds a third query per Terminal start (sudo.exe exists here). Measured: 28-37 ms per refresh, 16-18 ms for the sudo query.
- **What goes wrong:** Any Git refresh, task watch (every 30 s) or channel start more than 5 s after the last one blocks the main process for both queries. Normally a 30 ms hitch. When process creation is slow (agents compiling, antivirus scanning), each query can run to its 3 s timeout: a stall of up to 6 s with nothing logged. Not shown to be the reported stall.
- **Blocking time:** Main process. 28-37 ms per refresh measured in plain Node on this computer (not measured inside Electron or under load); bounded only by the 3 s timeout per query, so 6 s worst case; at most once per 5 s.
- **Suggested fix:** Read the registry PATH with async execFile, keep serving the last value while a refresh runs, and refresh on channel start and discovery only (a minutes-long cache is enough for Git calls). Read the sudo setting the same way, or once per start of Trace.
- **Checker's notes:** discovery.ts:51-56 uses execFileSync for reg.exe with a 3 s timeout, and :82-86 caches the result for 5 s. pty-manager.ts:37-41 adds a third synchronous query per Terminal start. The callers are as listed. The frequency is overstated: Git status is asked for only by the Changes view (src/components/DiffViewer.tsx:24), and TaskService.watch (tasks.ts:90-94) calls changedFiles only for tasks that were sent and are still open. So this runs on channel starts, the Changes view, account checks and the model catalog, not on a timer. The normal cost is a 30-55 ms hitch. The 6 s worst case needs both reg.exe calls to hit their timeouts, which nothing I measured supports. A synchronous child process on the main process is still worth removing.
- **Checker's measurements:** Plain Node on this computer: the two PATH queries together took 29-38 ms (median 36 ms, 15 runs); the sudo query took 15-22 ms (10 runs). C:\Windows\System32\sudo.exe exists. No tasks.json exists under %APPDATA%\AI Coding Hub or ai-coding-hub, so the 30 s task watch has no open tasks and runs no Git here.

#### One large line from a chat CLI costs quadratic time in ClaudeStream.push and CodexStream.push

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `cli-chat-stream-line-quadratic`
- **Where:** `electron/services/chat/claude-stream.ts:78`
- **What the code does:** push does `this.buffer += data` then `this.buffer.indexOf('\n')` over the whole unfinished line on every chunk (same in codex-stream.ts:122); the only limit is 64 MB (line 89). Measured with the same loop: a 4 MB line 28-210 ms, 16 MB 0.45 s (64 KB chunks) to 3.5 s (8 KB chunks), 48 MB 5.6-40 s.
- **What goes wrong:** In a chat channel a tool result arrives as one multi-megabyte JSON line (a large image or PDF read, a big MCP result). While it streams in, the main process re-scans the growing buffer, so every channel and the window stall. How large such lines get in this person's chats was not measured; the saved workspace now holds terminal channels only.
- **Blocking time:** Main process, spread over the chunks of one line. Measured in plain Node: 0.03-0.2 s for a 4 MB line, 0.45-3.5 s for 16 MB, 5.6-40 s for 48 MB.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep unfinished chunks in an array and search only the new chunk for a newline, joining once when one arrives; share that splitter between both streams. Cap a line well below 64 MB and show a notice when one is dropped.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described at claude-stream.ts:78-89 and codex-stream.ts:122-132: each chunk is appended and the whole unfinished line is searched again, with a 64 MB limit. My timings match the auditor's. Two things weaken the scenario. First, the cost is spread over many separate data events (chat-process.ts:27), each a few milliseconds, so other work runs in between; it is a busy main process, not one stall. Second, the largest line in this person's transcripts is 4.3 MB, which costs 34-250 ms in total. Transcript lines are a stand-in for stream lines, not the same thing, and I could not observe the real chunk size inside Electron. A multi-second effect needs lines of 16 MB or more, which this data does not show.
- **Checker's measurements:** Same loop in plain Node, 64 KB / 8 KB chunks: 1 MB line 3 / 16 ms; 4 MB 34 / 248 ms; 16 MB 0.58 / 4.9 s; 48 MB 5.4 / 44.7 s. Longest single push call: under 3 ms at 4 MB, 8-14 ms at 16 MB, 47 ms at 48 MB. Saved transcripts on this computer (379 .jsonl files, 1.5 GB, 289,369 lines in ~/.claude/projects, the Trace Claude profiles and ~/.codex/sessions): longest line 4.32 MB, 64 lines over 1 MB, 1 over 4 MB, none over 16 MB.

#### A Round Table takes a failed turn as an answer and keeps discussing with "(no answer)"

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `cli-chat-roundtable-error-as-answer`
- **Where:** `electron/services/chat/round-table.ts:238`
- **What the code does:** observe() handles `done` without reading event.isError (lines 230-239): the seat's answer becomes whatever text it had, and settle()/discuss() go on. discuss() stops only when both answers are empty (line 271). ReviewChains stops on an error (review-chain.ts:435). round-table.test.ts only sends isError: false.
- **What goes wrong:** One agent's turn fails (usage limit reached, API error, a Codex thread that never started and fails each send at once). The other agent is sent "answered: (no answer)" for every remaining round (up to 5), spending its turns, and the table bar never says a seat failed.
- **Suggested fix:** On an error `done` that Stop did not cause, end the discussion (round = rounds), show a notice naming the seat, and do not relay. Add tests with isError: true.
- **Checker's notes:** round-table.ts:230-239 handles done without reading event.isError: the seat's answer becomes whatever text it produced and settle() runs. discuss() (:268-284) stops only on agreement, round >= rounds or both answers empty, and sends the other seat "(no answer)" for an empty one. ReviewChains does stop on an error (review-chain.ts:435). round-table.test.ts:26 is the only done event in that test file and it uses isError: false. Stop is unaffected, because stop() sets round = rounds first. The failed seat's own error still reaches the window through onChat (pty-manager.ts:285), so the thread can show it; only the table bar and the relay ignore it. The waste is bounded by the rounds setting, at most 5.

#### Each Stop on Windows starts a node-pty helper that fails, then signals the stopped CLI's old process id five seconds later

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `cli-chat-windows-kill-fallback`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/stop-process.ts:24`
- **What the code does:** After taskkill, `pty.kill()` runs because node-pty reports exit about 1 s after the process ends (windowsPtyAgent.js:23, :237). kill() forks conpty_console_list_agent (a second Trace.exe as Node) for the dead pid; AttachConsole fails (conpty_console_list.cc:21), no reply comes, and after 5 s it calls process.kill(innerPid) (windowsPtyAgent.js:140-149, :189-193). Read in node-pty 1.1.0; not run under Electron.
- **What goes wrong:** Windows gives that process id to a new process within the 5 s (agents start many). node-pty then terminates that unrelated process of the person's. Rare: 0 reuses in 4 trials of 150 process starts here. Every Stop also costs one extra helper process, eight when quitting with eight channels.
- **Suggested fix:** After a successful taskkill, wait about 1.5 s for the exit event and call pty.kill() only if it has not come. When it must be called, first replace the agent's console-list lookup with an empty list (a helper in native-pty.ts), since Trace already stopped the tree.
- **Checker's notes:** stop-process.ts:24 calls pty.kill() whenever the exit event has not arrived. node-pty reports exit only when its output socket closes, 1,000 ms after the process ends (windowsPtyAgent.js:23, :237; windowsTerminal.js:97), so after taskkill this call normally runs. kill() forks the console-list helper for innerPid (windowsPtyAgent.js:140, :184). Only a message clears its timer, so after 5 s it calls process.kill(innerPid) (:189-193, :143). conpty.cc:101-105 closes the process handle as soon as the process ends, so Windows may give that id to another process. Chat channels are not affected: chat-process.ts:41 kills through the child handle. Real harm needs the id to be reused within 5 s by a process this user can end, which is rare. The certain cost is one extra helper process per Stop. Quitting ends the app before the timers fire.
- **Checker's measurements:** Forked node-pty 1.1.0's lib/conpty_console_list_agent.js under plain Node 24 with the id of a process that had already ended: it exited with code 1 after 45 ms, sent no message, error "AttachConsole failed". Not run inside Electron; process id reuse not re-measured.

#### At Full access Trace approves every non-edit request from a "read-only" planner or reviewer, including shell commands that write

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: security. ID: `cli-chat-readonly-seat-full-access`
- **Where:** `electron/services/chat/round-table.ts:36`
- **What the code does:** allowAtFull() answers allow for any permission request when the run's level is `full`; only names in EDITS (line 31) are declined first (round-table.ts:225-229, review-chain.ts:420-425). The name compared is Claude's display_name when present (claude-stream.ts:147). The briefs tell these seats "Trace keeps you read-only". The comments say this is intended.
- **What goes wrong:** A planner or reviewer seat at Full asks to run a shell command that changes files (git commit, a redirect, sed -i, a Codex command outside its sandbox) or an MCP tool that writes. Trace allows it with no card or alert, so a seat presented as read-only changes the project. Whether Claude's plan mode asks for such commands was not tested.
- **Suggested fix:** For read-only seats, auto-allow only requests known to read, and keep the approval card for shell and MCP requests. Match on tool_name, not display_name. Say in the brief and on the table bar what Full means for these seats.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described (round-table.ts:36-39, :225-229; review-chain.ts:420-425), but the main claim is documented product behaviour, not a defect. docs/reference.md:205 says that at Full access the read-only seats run commands without an approval card, that their file edits are still declined, and that such a command can still change files. The Codex part of the scenario is not reachable: at Full, codex-stream.ts:12 and :49-50 send approvalPolicy 'never' with a readOnly sandbox, so Codex sends no approval requests and stays sandboxed. What is left: the seat brief says "Trace keeps you read-only" (review-chain.ts:128), and EDITS is matched against display_name before tool_name (claude-stream.ts:147). If the CLI's display name for an edit tool is not in EDITS, an edit would be allowed at Full, against the documented behaviour. I could not establish what the installed CLI sends; the repo's test and fixture both use display_name equal to tool_name.

#### Every channel start runs four CLI probes, and a named profile is refused when the sign-in probe is slow

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `cli-chat-discovery-every-launch`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/pty-manager.ts:220`
- **What the code does:** start() calls discoverEngine each time: --version and --help, then `auth --help` and `auth status` (discovery.ts:201, :186-194, :215), with no cache. The sign-in result is used only for named profiles (pty-manager.ts:221), which are refused unless it says authenticated; probes time out at 8 s (discovery.ts:172). Measured here: claude --version 52 ms, --help 226 ms, auth --help 142 ms; auth status not run.
- **What goes wrong:** Starting or restoring several channels launches four CLI processes each before any terminal opens, adding roughly half a second or more per start. If `auth status` for a profile times out or prints something unexpected on a busy machine, that channel fails with "Connect this Claude profile…" although it is signed in.
- **Suggested fix:** Cache discovery per executable path and modification time (and per profile folder for sign-in) for a few minutes. Run the sign-in probe only for named profiles. Treat a timed-out probe as unknown with its own message instead of a login error.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. PtyManager.start() calls this.discover for every non-shell start (pty-manager.ts:220); main.ts passes no `discover`, so it is the uncached discoverEngine. That runs --version and --help in parallel (discovery.ts:201), then `auth --help` and `auth status` one after the other (discovery.ts:190-192, called at :215). Inside start() the sign-in result is read only at pty-manager.ts:221, and a named profile is refused unless state is 'authenticated'; a timed-out or non-JSON `auth status` classifies as 'unknown' (auth-status.ts:8-21), so the refusal path is real. Two limits on impact: (1) the probes use async execFile, so they delay the channel opening but do not block the main process, and are not the reported 1-3 s freeze; (2) `auth status` took about 0.19 s here against an 8 s timeout, so a false 'Connect this Claude profile' needs a roughly 40x slowdown. That half is reachable in code but I could not make it happen. The only synchronous work on this path is hostEnvironment()'s two reg.exe reads (discovery.ts:53, cached 5 s). Codex probes were not timed (npm shim).
- **Checker's measurements:** Claude probes on this computer, 3 rounds, same sequence as discoverEngine: total 448-478 ms per start (--version 32-38 ms, --help 135-142 ms, auth --help 123-136 ms, auth status 186-201 ms, exit 0, loggedIn true). Synchronous reg.exe x2 (hostEnvironment, main thread, once per 5 s): 31-41 ms.

#### When one Round Table seat's process ends, the other keeps working and gets its edit rights back mid-turn

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `cli-chat-roundtable-ended-partner`
- **Where:** `electron/services/chat/round-table.ts:209`
- **What the code does:** ended() only calls close(lead, false); close() (line 205) sets both seats to not read-only and removes the table. It does not interrupt the seat still at work, unlike ReviewChains.ended (review-chain.ts:411). For Claude, setReadOnly(false) sends set_permission_mode at once (claude-stream.ts:46-49).
- **What goes wrong:** The driver's CLI crashes during a round. The Claude reviewer, part way through its review turn in plan mode, is switched to the channel's own level (accept edits or full) and finishes the turn with no table watching it. Its answer goes nowhere and it may now edit. A reviewer actually editing was not observed.
- **Suggested fix:** In RoundTables.ended, interrupt seats that are busy before closing, as ReviewChains does, and lift read-only only for seats that are not in a turn (or after their `done`).
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. RoundTables.ended (round-table.ts:209-212) only calls close(lead, false); close (201-207) deletes the table and calls host.setReadOnly(seat, false) for both seats with no interrupt. ReviewChains.ended (review-chain.ts:409-413) interrupts busy seats first. PtyManager.finish sets run.finished before tables.ended (pty-manager.ts:456-457), so only the surviving seat receives the call. For a Claude survivor, ClaudeStream.setReadOnly(false) sends set_permission_mode at once (claude-stream.ts:46-49, 43). The window does not stop the surviving seat on an unexpected exit (it stops both only when the person stops the lead). The trigger is narrow, which keeps this low: by default Claude leads and drives and Codex reviews (src/runtime/round-table.ts:171-172), and CodexStream.setReadOnly only applies from the next turn (codex-stream.ts:45). A mid-turn change therefore needs the driver swapped to Codex and Codex's process to die while Claude reviews. At the 'ask' level Claude then asks for approval in the ordinary way; only at 'edits' or 'full' could it edit unprompted. The reviewer was briefed not to edit, and no edit was observed. Not run live.

#### Trace's status line starts curl on every status refresh in each Claude terminal

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `cli-chat-statusline-curl`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/attention.ts:113`
- **What the code does:** claudeAttentionSettings sets `statusLine` to a curl command posting the status JSON to /status whenever the person has no status line of their own (main.ts:168). Only context_window_size is used (attention.ts:81), and telemetry ignores it unless it changed (telemetry.ts:102). The default home and both profiles here have no own status line (checked), so both terminal channels use it.
- **What goes wrong:** While Claude works, Claude Code refreshes the status line repeatedly. Each refresh starts curl.exe (through a shell) and makes a loopback request, per channel, for a number that changes only with the model. With several busy channels this is steady process churn beside the agents' own work. The cost per refresh was not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Have the command skip the request when the size equals the last one it posted (kept in a small per-run file), or take the window size from another source and drop the status line.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. main.ts:168-169 sets statusLine for every non-chat Claude run whose home and project have no status line of their own, and attention.ts:113 makes it a curl POST to /status. The handler uses only context_window.context_window_size (attention.ts:81), and telemetry.setContextWindow returns early when the size is unchanged (telemetry.ts:102). I checked the default home and both profile folders: none defines statusLine, and both Claude terminal processes now running carry Trace's status line. The cost is process churn outside Trace: the receiving process spends about 0.2 ms per post, so this does not explain the reported 1-3 s freeze. How often Claude Code refreshes the status line was not measured here; its documentation says at most once per 300 ms while messages update, which would be up to about 3 shell-plus-curl starts per second per busy terminal channel. Severity low is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** One run of the exact command against a private loopback server (15 runs each): curl.exe direct median 20 ms; through cmd.exe 37 ms; through Git Bash 55 ms (max 63 ms). Receiving process: mean 0.185 ms per post over 45 posts. Live now: 15 claude.exe processes, 2 with --settings and both include Trace's statusLine; 0 chat runs. statusLine key absent in ~/.claude and both profile settings files.

#### Resizing in the second after a CLI ends on Windows shows "The terminal could not be resized"

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `cli-chat-resize-after-exit`
- **Where:** `electron/services/cli/pty-manager.ts:439`
- **What the code does:** node-pty reports exit about 1 s after the process ends (windowsPtyAgent.js:23, :237); until then its resize throws "Cannot resize a pty that has already exited" (windowsPtyAgent.js:120). resize() turns that into RESIZE_FAILED, and the window shows every resize error except STALE_SESSION on the tile (src/runtime/terminals.ts:169). resizeRemote (line 165) does the same. Not reproduced.
- **What goes wrong:** A CLI exits and, before Trace has heard the exit, the window or a tile is resized (or a phone opens the channel at its own size). The tile shows an error for a channel that simply ended.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat a failed resize as harmless: return without an error when the pty throws (the exit event follows), or check the pty's exit state first.
- **Checker's notes:** Reproduced at the node-pty level with the repository's node-pty 1.1.0 on this computer. After the process ends, _$onProcessExit sets _exitCode (windowsPtyAgent.js:223) and the exit event waits for the 1000 ms flush timer (:23, :237) and the socket close (windowsTerminal.js:97-98); until then resize throws 'Cannot resize a pty that has already exited' (windowsPtyAgent.js:120-121). PtyManager.resize turns that into RESIZE_FAILED (pty-manager.ts:439-440), with no check of the pty's exit state, and sendSize shows every error except STALE_SESSION on the tile (src/runtime/terminals.ts:168-169). The finding overstates how long it lasts: the exit event that follows sets the tile's error to `event.diagnostic ?? null` (src/state/workspace-store.ts:205), so after a clean exit the message disappears within about a second. It needs a resize inside that one-second window and a natural exit (a run being stopped skips the resize, pty-manager.ts:436). The phone path (resizeRemote, :165-166, called at remote-server.ts:517) returns the error to the phone's request. Cosmetic and brief; not shown in the running app, only in node-pty and by reading the code.
- **Checker's measurements:** Short-lived cmd.exe through the repo's node-pty under Node 24: resize threw 33 of 33 attempts (every 25 ms) from 71 ms until the exit event at 1065 ms, a 994 ms window. Message: 'Cannot resize a pty that has already exited'.

### Hub services (rules, memory, sync, usage) (14)

#### Known leftover, now bigger: every channel launch and every note Trace writes lists the Drive folder synchronously (0.36-0.5 s here, growing about 0.5 ms per note)

- **Check:** Confirmed, high (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `hub-drive-listing-on-launch`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/service.ts:49`
- **What the code does:** trackProject calls drive.sync() synchronously; AutoMemory.prepare calls it at each launch (auto-memory.ts:260, main.ts:518), each summary calls it (auto-memory.ts:340, main.ts:517), memory-write too (service.ts:92). sync() stats every note on Drive (drive.ts:80, memory.ts:35-41, archive included). Measured: 355-502 ms for 785 notes, 160-205 ms for 336; then memory.list() 48-72 ms (auto-memory.ts:261).
- **What goes wrong:** A channel, Round Table seat or task starts in the home-folder project, or a session there ends with a summary: the main process blocks about 0.45-0.6 s each time; several seats in a row give 1-3 s. Notes grow 100-170 per active day here (785 now, 636 on Nov 14), and archived notes are still listed, so at 5,000 notes each launch blocks about 2.5 s.
- **Blocking time:** Main process, per launch and per Trace-written note: 0.36-0.5 s Drive listing plus 0.05-0.07 s note read for the 785-note project; 0.16-0.2 s for the 336-note project. Measured read-only with Node 24 on this computer.
- **Suggested fix:** Make trackProject fire `void this.drive.syncLater(project).catch(() => {})`. For the pre-launch pull, await syncLater in the already-async launch path before prepareLaunch (pty-manager.ts:203 onward) or rely on the 30 s tick. Use the same for memory-write. Skip archived notes in sizes().
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed by reading the whole path: pty-manager.ts:228 calls prepareLaunch synchronously for every agent launch (terminal and chat, not plain shells); main.ts:173 calls AutoMemory.prepare; auto-memory.ts:260 calls refresh, which is trackProject (main.ts:518); service.ts:49 calls drive.sync(project) with no sizes, so drive.ts:80 stats every note on both sides synchronously (memory.ts:35-41, archive folder included), then auto-memory.ts:261 reads every local note. automaticMemory is not set in preferences here, so it is on. About 0.5 s of main-process block per launch in the home-folder project, 0.25 s in the ai-scribe ones, 0.08 s in this repo's project; bursts of launches stack. One correction: the 'every note Trace writes' trigger is rare in practice. Only session summaries (auto-memory.ts:340) and Memory-panel writes (service.ts:92) run in the main process; most notes here come from the MCP server process, which does not call sync. Kept high because it is frequent, matches the reported symptom when several runs start, and grows linearly with notes.
- **Checker's measurements:** Synchronous sizes() on G:\My Drive: 440 ms for 785 notes, 236 ms for 304, 225 ms for 250, 203 ms for 336, 71 ms for 105 (0.56-0.9 ms per note). Local sizes 17.5 ms and memory.list 45 ms for 785 notes. host-health.json: 42 launches in 4 days, 15 of them within 5 s of the previous one. Home-folder project: 785 notes, only 86 are automatic summaries (0-13 per day); 158 notes were added on 2026-11-15.

#### Known 1.21.3 leftover is larger than recorded: first Drive sync after each start reads every note on both sides, 1.3 s and up to 6 s here

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `hub-drive-full-compare-after-start`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/drive.ts:89`
- **What the code does:** Lines 88-91 read the local and Drive copy of every note not yet in the in-memory `same` map, even when sizes are equal, although the fast path (line 82) already trusts equal sizes. Read-only replica on this computer: home-folder project 785 pairs = 5,967 ms (1 pair differs in size); ai-scribe project 336 pairs = 1,262 ms (250 local copies have CRLF).
- **What goes wrong:** After every start, the first channel launch (synchronous, via trackProject) or the first 30 s tick for either project runs this on the main process: window and terminals stop for about 1.3 s and 3-6 s. A project's fast path can never succeed while one copy differs in bytes, so it repeats on every start.
- **Blocking time:** Main process, once per start per affected project: 1.26 s (336 notes) and 5.97 s (785 notes) measured in a read-only replica without zod; the known history measured 2.9 s at 636 notes.
- **Suggested fix:** In the slow path skip pairs whose sizes are equal (`if (size === other) { same.set(id, ...); continue; }`), as line 82 already does; read the pairs that differ with fs.promises inside syncLater; optionally persist `same` so 250 CRLF pairs are not re-read each start. Add a test with one byte-different pair asserting only that pair is read.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. drive.ts:82 returns early only when every size matches; otherwise line 89 skips a pair only if `same` already holds its size pair, and `same` (line 56) is in memory only, so after a start lines 90-91 read and zod-parse both copies of every pair, equal sizes included. Reads are synchronous on the main process whether reached from trackProject (service.ts:49, at launch) or from the tick (syncLater awaits only the sizes, then calls sync). After one pass every pair is cached, so it is once per start per affected project, not recurring; I checked that later passes do no reads. Two projects here are affected permanently. Downgraded from high: it is the already-recorded leftover, happens once per start (6 starts in 4 days in host-health.json), and I measured 2.2 s and 1.0 s rather than 6 s. It does grow about 2.8 ms per note pair.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only replica of sync() with zod parsing, empty `same` map, Node 24, real folders. k-gea-382ef5dedd: 785 pairs, 1 differs in size, slow path 2,200 ms (Drive reads 2,091 ms, local 101 ms). ai-scribe-5688686b0a: 336 pairs, 250 differ in size, 1,040 ms (Drive 997 ms). The other 7 projects present on both sides take the fast path (0 reads). All pairs had equal content (0 conflicts, 0 unreadable). Drive files were probably warm; the auditor's 5,967 ms was not reproduced.

#### Configured price rates match no recorded model, so all usage on this computer is unpriced

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: ux. ID: `hub-price-rates-never-match`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/telemetry.ts:49`
- **What the code does:** rateFor (lines 49-51) matches a rate only by exact model ID or the ID without case and release date. preferences.ts:3 accepts any 1-120 character string. This computer's rates are named "Claude Fable 5.2" and "Claude Opus 5.5"; a copy of usage.db holds claude-opus-5-5 (18,495 events), claude-fable-5-2 (2,715), claude-haiku-5-5, gpt-6.1-sol, gpt-6-astra: none gets a rate.
- **What goes wrong:** usageCost returns null for every event (telemetry.ts:53), so runs are 'unpriced', the day's cost stays null (day-usage.ts:44), and a daily cost budget has no cost to compare (usageAlerts itself was not read). Settings gives no hint that the name must be the CLI's model ID.
- **Suggested fix:** In rateFor, compare a normalized form of both names (lowercase, runs of non-alphanumerics to '-'), so "Claude Opus 5.5" matches claude-opus-5-5. In the price editor, list the model IDs seen in usage and flag a rate that matches none. Add a unit test for display-name rates.
- **Checker's notes:** telemetry.ts:49-51 matches a rate only by exact string or by lower-cased ID with a trailing -YYYYMMDD removed; 'claude opus 5.5' never equals 'claude-opus-5-5', so usageCost (line 53) returns null for every event and all usage on this computer is unpriced, in runUsage and in day-usage.ts:44. Two parts are overstated. First, Settings does give a hint: the pricing table's column header is 'Model ID' (src/components/HubPanels.tsx:125) and the help text says 'exact model ID reported by the CLI' (shared/help-content.ts:123); what is missing is any check or list of seen IDs. Second, the budget consequence does not apply here, since no daily cost budget is set. The Usage screen does label runs as unpriced. Still a real shortfall for this person: the rates they entered do nothing.
- **Checker's measurements:** preferences.json prices: "Claude Fable 5.2" and "Claude Opus 5.5". Scratch copy of usage.db (21,290 events, 244 runs): claude-opus-5-5 18,495; claude-fable-5-2 2,739; claude-haiku-5-5 25; gpt-6.1-sol 18; gpt-6-astra 8; <synthetic> 5. No recorded model equals either rate name after baseModel(). preferences.json has no dailyCost or dailyTokens.
