#### Shared rules live as blocks in tracked CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md: dirty working trees, mixed line endings, refused launches after a checkout, and the same rules four times in Claude's context

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: ux. ID: `hub-rules-in-tracked-files`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/rules.ts:19`
- **What the code does:** The block is built with '\n' (lines 19, 24) whatever the file uses; `file` reports this repo's CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md as 'with CRLF, LF line terminators', and `git diff --stat` shows the 21 added lines in each are exactly the block. verifyLaunch (lines 86-87) requires the block in each folder's own files; the home folder, Projects and this repo each have it, and CLAUDE.md imports AGENTS.md.
- **What goes wrong:** Every project shows two modified tracked files. `git checkout -- CLAUDE.md`, a stash, a reset or a fresh clone removes the block and the next launch there is refused. An agent's `git add -A` commits it. A Claude session in this repo receives the 1,878-character rules four times (confirmed in this session's own context).
- **Suggested fix:** Pass the rules at launch as memory already is (--append-system-prompt, Codex developer_instructions) and stop editing project files; or, keeping files, write the block with the file's own line ending and skip CLAUDE.md when it imports AGENTS.md or an ancestor folder already carries the same block.
- **Checker's notes:** All stated facts hold. replaceRules builds the block with '\n' (rules.ts:19, 24) regardless of the file's line endings, giving mixed endings in CRLF checkouts (applied() at line 29 tolerates this, so it is cosmetic). verifyLaunch (lines 86-87) refuses a launch unless both of the folder's own files hold the current block, so a stash, checkout or fresh clone that drops the uncommitted block blocks the next launch until rules are applied again; only Trace's own task worktrees re-apply automatically (src/runtime/task-worktree.ts:15-22). The fourfold copy is visible in this session's own context: home CLAUDE.md, Projects CLAUDE.md, this repo's CLAUDE.md and the imported AGENTS.md each carry the block. This is a design cost rather than a coding error, and the refusal has a clear message, but two permanently modified tracked files in every project and refused launches after routine Git commands are real friction, so medium stands.
- **Checker's measurements:** Registry holds ~, ...\Claude\Projects and ...\Coding-Hub among 9 folders; rules text is 1,878 characters, block 1,954. Repo CLAUDE.md: 3 CRLF and 19 bare LF line ends; AGENTS.md: 91 CRLF and 19 bare LF; git ls-files --eol reports w/mixed for both; core.autocrlf=true. git diff --stat: 21 insertions in each file, and the CLAUDE.md diff is a blank line plus the block.

#### After every start the status bar re-reads 60 days of CLI logs (1.44 GB here) on the main process in about 90 synchronous 16 MB steps

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: performance. ID: `hub-day-usage-reread-after-start`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/day-usage.ts:34`
- **What the code does:** Offsets and totals live only in memory (line 25); each days() call reads up to READ_BUDGET = 16 MB synchronously (lines 15, 34, 83-84). The window calls again every 500 ms until complete (src/state/day-usage-store.ts:18). Measured with the real class from dist-electron: passes of 34-69 ms; 340 logs, 1,440 MB in the window. Steady state is 20-24 ms per call (548 names, 199 folders), every 15 s.
- **What goes wrong:** For about the first minute after each start the main process takes a 35-70 ms pause twice a second (about 4-5 s in total), on top of the Drive stalls above; typing and terminal output stutter. It grows with LOG_DAYS = 60 and the size of transcripts.
- **Suggested fix:** Read with fs.promises (or a worker thread) and persist per-file offsets plus per-day totals in a small cache file, so a start only reads what the logs gained. Prune `seen` and `files` entries that left the window.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: offsets and totals live only in memory (day-usage.ts:25), each days() call reads up to 16 MB synchronously and JSON-parses every line (lines 15, 34, 83-87), and the window asks again 500 ms after each incomplete answer (src/state/day-usage-store.ts:18), so about 50 s of catch-up follows every start. The numbers match the auditor's. Downgraded to low: each pause is 50-100 ms, about a tenth of the time during that first minute, which is jitter rather than the 1-3 s stall reported. Also true, and not in the finding: StatusBar.tsx:70-71 calls totals() every 15 s and 2 s after each usage update, and main.ts:860 calls it every minute, each costing about 20 ms in steady state.
- **Checker's measurements:** Compiled DayUsage (dist-electron, source unchanged since the build) on the real logs, 4 sources: 340 candidate logs, 1,440 MB, 12 files over 16 MB (largest 138 MB). 91 passes to complete, 5,030 ms in total; per pass min 21, median 51, p90 82, max 99 ms (machine under load from parallel auditors). Steady state 18.7-19.9 ms per call. Candidate scan alone 21 ms.

#### Usage recording commits to disk once per reply line (1.2-1.5 ms each here) and prepares its statements again for every line

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: performance. ID: `hub-telemetry-commit-per-line`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/telemetry.ts:206`
- **What the code does:** Lines 206-207 run a SELECT and an upsert per usage line, each as its own transaction, with `database.prepare` inside the loop; line 114 sets WAL but not `synchronous` (the copy reports synchronous=2, FULL). Measured in scratch on this disk: 100 such upserts 141-149 ms, 2 ms in one transaction, 8 ms with synchronous=NORMAL. Reads are 512 KB per poll (line 199); large transcripts here hold about 31 reply lines per 512 KB.
- **What goes wrong:** While a run catches up (run end, a conversation found late, many subagents writing at once), each 750 ms poll blocks the main process 40-150 ms per run; with eight channels the pauses add up. Not a 1-3 s stall by itself.
- **Suggested fix:** Wrap each ingest() call in BEGIN/COMMIT, prepare the two statements once in the constructor, and set `PRAGMA synchronous=NORMAL` (safe with WAL; a power cut can lose only the last commits, which the next read re-ingests).
- **Checker's notes:** telemetry.ts:206-207 calls database.prepare twice per usage line and runs each statement as its own autocommit transaction; line 114 sets WAL and busy_timeout but not synchronous, so each upsert syncs the WAL. Measured cost matches the claim. Impact is small: a poll reads at most 512 KB per file (line 199), so a catch-up poll costs roughly 40-150 ms per run and a normal poll a few milliseconds; a run with many subagents writing at once pays this every 750 ms. The finding itself says it is not a 1-3 s stall, and I agree, so low rather than medium. The fix is cheap and safe (one transaction per ingest call, statements prepared once).
- **Checker's measurements:** New connection to a scratch copy of usage.db reports synchronous=2 (FULL), journal_mode=wal. Fresh scratch database with the app's schema and pragmas, 100 SELECT+upsert pairs prepared per line as ingest() does: 137-146 ms as shipped (1.4 ms per line), 2.1-3.0 ms inside one transaction, 3.3-3.7 ms with synchronous=NORMAL. runUsage's query plus JSON parse for the largest run (4,542 events): 9.1 ms.

#### Shared rules registry and launches canonicalize folders differently, so a correctly applied project can be refused

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `hub-rules-path-spelling`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/rules.ts:59`
- **What the code does:** directory() uses fs.realpathSync (JavaScript implementation) and `applied` needs `state.directories.includes(directory)` (line 70). Launches pass `await realpath(request.cwd)` (cli/pty-manager.ts:203, native). Checked on this computer: realpathSync of a lower-cased home path returns it unchanged (`c:\users\…`), the native one returns `C:\Users\…`.
- **What goes wrong:** Rules are applied to a folder whose path was typed in another letter case, or reached through a subst or mapped drive. Both files carry the block, but verifyLaunch looks up the native spelling, finds no registry entry and throws RULES_NOT_APPLIED; applying again with the same spelling changes nothing. Which spelling the window sends was not traced, hence medium confidence.
- **Suggested fix:** Use fs.realpathSync.native in directory() and compare registry entries case-insensitively on win32 and darwin (normalize when reading shared-rules.json). Add a test that applies with a lower-case path and verifies a launch with the canonical one.
- **Checker's notes:** rules.ts:59 uses the JavaScript fs.realpathSync, which keeps the typed letter case on Windows, and line 70 needs an exact string match in the registry. Launches pass the native spelling (pty-manager.ts:203, fs/promises realpath) to verifyLaunch (main.ts:159). The auditor did not trace which spelling the window sends; I did: the tile has a free-text 'Repository path' field (src/components/SessionTile.tsx:156) used as the launch cwd (src/runtime/use-native-host.ts:153-157) and as a Rules panel target (RulesPanel.tsx:12, binding.cwd while not running). So a typed path in another letter case produces a refusal that re-applying cannot clear; choosing the folder with Add project (the picker returns the canonical spelling) registers a second entry and clears it. Low: it needs a hand-typed, differently cased or subst path. The subst and mapped-drive variants were not tested.
- **Checker's measurements:** Reproduced with the compiled RulesService in a scratch root and scratch project: applied with a lower-cased path, registry stored the lower-case spelling, panel reported applied=true, both files held the block, verifyLaunch with fs.promises.realpath's spelling threw RULES_NOT_APPLIED, and applying again did not help. On this computer all 9 registered project folders are already spelled canonically under both realpath implementations, so the person is not affected now.

#### Known 80 ms tick, detail: half is recomputing all usage, and this computer's whole usage file is rewritten to Drive on every tick while any agent runs

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `hub-device-sync-usage-churn`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/device-sync.ts:167`
- **What the code does:** usage() calls localUsage() = telemetry.list() (telemetry.ts:233: runUsage per run, two fresh prepared statements each): 33-40 ms on a copy of this database (244 runs, 21,266 events). It runs each 30 s tick (service.ts:65), and the window asks for 'usage' every 60 s too. Lines 169-170 rewrite `usage/<machine>.json` whenever any run's numbers changed. The read-only part of the Drive calls measured 14-20 ms (max 78 ms in 90 s).
- **What goes wrong:** While agents work, the main process spends about 35-40 ms three times a minute rebuilding the same list, and Drive receives a new revision of the usage file every 30 s. The cost of that Drive write was not measured (writing to G: was off limits).
- **Suggested fix:** Cache list() and invalidate it when ingest() or metadata changes; publish the usage file when a run ends or at most every few minutes; skip folder() mkdirs after the first success.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. device-sync.ts:167 calls localUsage() = telemetry.list() (telemetry.ts:233, runUsage per run with fresh prepared statements) on every 30 s tick (service.ts:65), and use-native-host.ts:68 requests 'usage' every 60 s when syncDevices is on (it is on here). Lines 169-170 rewrite usage/<machine>.json whenever digest([folder, records]) changes, which is every tick in which any run's token numbers moved. Supporting observation: the Drive usage file's mtime (20:52:48) matches the minute of the last usage.db WAL write (20:52), and it was not rewritten during 75 s of sampling while no tracked run changed, so the rewrite is tied to usage changes, not unconditional. Cost per tick is tens of milliseconds, so this cannot be the 1-3 s stall; it is churn (a new Drive revision of an 85 KB file every 30 s while agents work) plus about 40 ms of avoidable main-process work three times a minute. The Drive write itself was not measured (writing to G: is off limits).
- **Checker's measurements:** On a copy of usage.db (244 runs, 21,290 events): list() equivalent 35.3 / 40.7 / 52.5 ms (min/median/max of 15, machine under parallel load); 127 records, 54.7 KB JSON; stringify+digest 0.45 ms. Read-only replica of the tick's Drive reads on G:\My Drive\AI-Coding-Hub-Sync: 18.0 / 20.2 / 28.2 ms (12 samples). Drive usage file 85,465 bytes, mtime unchanged over 7 samples in 75 s while device-sync.json lastSync advanced.

#### One note whose two copies differ in content silently stops all Drive sync for its project, and resolving it on one computer recreates the conflict on the other

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `hub-drive-conflict-blocks-project`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/drive.ts:91`
- **What the code does:** Line 91 throws MEMORY_CONFLICT inside the preflight loop, before the copy loops at lines 96-97, although line 85 says one conflicting file must not stop the rest. service.ts:49 and :64 swallow the error. resolve() (lines 44-52) overwrites the Drive file with this computer's copy.
- **What goes wrong:** A note is altered on one side (a manual edit, a Drive duplicate). From then on nothing in that project uploads or downloads on this computer, with no message unless Sync now is pressed. After Keep both here, the other computer's local copy differs from Drive, its sync throws, and its only resolution writes its copy back. The ping-pong is reasoned from the code, not reproduced.
- **Suggested fix:** Record the conflict in `failure` and continue, as unreadable copies already do; report a conflict count through drive-status so the Memory panel can show it. In resolve(), leave the shared file as it is and remember the pair as settled (or rename the Drive copy) instead of overwriting it.
- **Checker's notes:** drive.ts:91 throws MEMORY_CONFLICT inside the preflight loop, before the copy loops at 96-97, so one content-different note stops all upload and download for that project; this contradicts the comment at line 85, and hub.test.ts:302 expects the throw. service.ts:49, :64 and :92 swallow it, so nothing is shown unless Sync now or Inspect Drive conflicts is pressed (HubPanels.tsx:100,107). resolve() (drive.ts:44-52) overwrites the Drive file with the local copy. The ping-pong holds only when the two computers' LOCAL copies differ (for example a local file edited by hand): each Keep both then rewrites Drive and adds another 'Drive conflict:' note. If only the Drive copy was altered, the first resolve makes Drive equal to every computer's local copy and it ends there. Severity lowered: notes are immutable and written by rename, a truncated or half-synced copy takes the non-throwing path at line 93, and Drive duplicates named 'x (1).json' do not match the note file pattern, so a true content conflict needs a manual edit or a schema difference between versions. None exists on this computer.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only comparison of all 10 local memory projects against G:\My Drive\AI-Coding-Hub-Memory: 0 content conflicts, 0 unreadable copies. Two projects have size-different pairs with identical parsed content (ai-scribe-5688686b0a: 250 of 336; k-gea-382ef5dedd: 1 of 785), which line 91 treats as the same. One local project (rce-research-b00ef49a66, 13 notes) has no Drive folder yet.

#### The window can write an arbitrary command into the user-level Claude or Codex MCP configuration with no confirmation on the host

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: security. ID: `hub-add-server-no-confirmation`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/service.ts:24`
- **What the code does:** The add-server schema accepts any command up to 4,096 characters, 100 arguments and scope 'user'; ConfigService.add (config.ts:144-148) writes it to ~/.claude.json mcpServers or ~/.codex/config.toml at once. Other actions that run programs ask in a native dialog (main.ts:552). AGENTS.md rule 1 says the renderer is untrusted and must not get arbitrary executables.
- **What goes wrong:** Script running in the window (for example through a rendering flaw in chat or note content) sends add-server with scope 'user' and a shell command. The next Claude Code or Codex start in any folder launches it as an MCP server; that user-level servers start without a trust prompt is my understanding of the CLIs, not verified here.
- **Suggested fix:** Show a native confirmation with the exact file, command and arguments before updateConfig, as Verification does, and refuse while a restart is pending. Add a test that add-server without confirmation writes nothing.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described: service.ts:24 accepts any command, up to 100 arguments and scope 'user'; service.ts:86 calls ConfigService.add (config.ts:144-148), which writes ~/.claude.json or ~/.codex/config.toml at once; main.ts:772 adds only the sender check. But the trigger and the added impact are not established. The window runs with contextIsolation, sandbox, navigation denied and a production CSP of script-src 'self' (main.ts:418-436), and src has no dangerouslySetInnerHTML, so no script-injection path was shown. More important, a compromised window can already run any command with no confirmation: startSession with engine 'shell' plus writeSession (main.ts:825-839; shared/schemas.ts:48) is the app's core function. add-server therefore gives persistence, not a new privilege. The phone remote cannot reach it (hub.execute is called only from main.ts:614 with memory-write). A confirmation would be defence in depth only. That user-level MCP servers start without a trust prompt was not verified here.

#### A Codex terminal that switches conversation is never followed, so its later usage is not recorded

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `hub-codex-conversation-switch`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/telemetry.ts:90`
- **What the code does:** follow() returns for any engine but Claude (line 90); find() runs only while the watcher has no file (line 166); the thread id is the first regex match in the last 128 KB of each log (line 138). A leftover codex-tui.log on this computer names two thread ids (2,992 and 228 mentions). On the usage.db copy, 33 of 59 Codex runs have no usage rows; why was not established.
- **What goes wrong:** The person runs /new or /resume inside a Codex terminal channel. Codex writes to another rollout; Trace keeps reading the first file, so the tile's tokens, cost and the Usage screen stop growing for that channel. Newer Codex writes logs_2.sqlite instead of codex-tui.log, where the regex matches only by chance.
- **Suggested fix:** For Codex, re-check the per-run log for the newest thread id on each poll (take the last match, not the first) and switch files as follow() does for Claude; add a test with two thread ids in one log.
- **Checker's notes:** Code facts hold: telemetry.ts:90 returns for any engine but Claude, the only caller is the Claude SessionStart hook (main.ts:129, attention.ts:83), find() runs only while w.file is null (line 166), and line 138 takes the first regex match in each log's last 128 KB. So a Codex terminal that starts writing another rollout is never followed. Not established: that Codex's /new or /resume inside the terminal writes a different rollout file (not tested; the CLI was not run), and the cited evidence does not show a switch. In the leftover codex-tui.log the main thread id appears from line 22 to the last line, with three other ids interleaved in the middle, which looks like secondary threads rather than a conversation switch. The '33 of 59 Codex runs without usage' figure is not evidence either: Claude runs show a similar share. Impact if real is limited to usage and cost display for that channel; token limits are Claude-only (line 180).
- **Checker's measurements:** usage.db copy: 59 Codex runs, 33 without usage rows (9 of those have a conversationId); 185 Claude runs, 82 without usage rows. Leftover codex-tui.log (2.49 MB, 5,075 lines): thread 01a137ff-397b… 2,767 mentions on lines 22-5075; three other ids with 53, 172 and 228 mentions confined to lines 159-1282. A third leftover run folder holds only logs_2.sqlite-shm/-wal. Installed codex-cli 0.167.1.

#### Memory installation writes this computer's absolute paths into project files it describes as for teammates, and Trace later rewrites those files unasked

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `hub-memory-install-machine-paths`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/memory.ts:116`
- **What the code does:** install() writes `command: process.execPath` and `args: [server, this.root, project]` (lines 99, 116) into .mcp.json and .codex/config.toml; the panel says it is for 'teammates who clone it' (src/components/HubPanels.tsx:108). AutoMemory.configured (auto-memory.ts:224-229) rewrites an entry whose paths do not exist, at each launch and in the sweep 90 s after start (main.ts:522); TOML is re-serialized without comments (config.ts:42).
- **What goes wrong:** The files are committed and pulled on another computer or by a teammate: the paths do not exist there. With Trace, the tracked files are rewritten automatically (dirty tree, Codex config comments lost) and the two computers overwrite each other's paths with every pull; without Trace, the server fails to start. This repo has no such files, so it is not active here.
- **Suggested fix:** Do not write machine paths into committed files: write the entry to the user-level or local-only config (.claude/settings.local.json scope, ~/.codex/config.toml), or resolve the program through a stable launcher name. Make the stale-path repair ask first for files inside a Git work tree.
- **Checker's notes:** memory.ts:99 and :116 write command = process.execPath and args = [memory-server.js path, this computer's memory folder, key] into the project's .mcp.json and .codex/config.toml; the panel text recommending it for 'teammates who clone it' is at src/components/HubPanels.tsx:104 (not 108). auto-memory.ts:224-229 rewrites an entry whose program paths do not exist, unasked, at each launch (prepare, line 264) and in the sweep 90 s after start (main.ts:522); config.ts:47 re-serializes TOML without comments. The repair replaces only the command and server path, so the other computer's memory folder (args[1]) stays and the session is told it uses 'another notes folder'. Scenario holds for a committed file shared between computers with different install or user paths. Not active on this computer: the only installed entry is in ~\.codex\config.toml (the home folder, not a Git work tree), and none of the registered project folders has one.
- **Checker's measurements:** Checked 8 registered project folders for .mcp.json and .codex/config.toml: one coding-hub-memory entry, in ~\.codex\config.toml (lines 213-218, key k-gea-382ef5dedd, absolute paths to this install). ~\.mcp.json exists without the entry.

#### Folders registered for shared rules are never removed, and each task worktree adds one, up to a hard limit of 100

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `hub-rules-registry-never-pruned`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/rules.ts:105`
- **What the code does:** save() keeps every earlier directory and prunes only prepared coaching folders (line 105), then throws RULES_LIMIT above 100 (line 106). No other code removes entries. src/runtime/task-worktree.ts:16-20 applies rules to every new worktree through rules-save. This computer has 10 registered folders and has not used worktrees.
- **What goes wrong:** After about 90 more worktree tasks, or projects, rules-save fails with 'At most 100 projects can be registered', so rules cannot be applied to a new worktree and verifyLaunch refuses its sessions. Removed worktrees stay registered and show as errors in the Shared rules panel.
- **Suggested fix:** Drop registered folders that no longer exist when they are under Trace's own worktrees folder (as is done for coaching folders), remove the entry when a worktree is removed, and offer Remove for a missing project in the panel.
- **Checker's notes:** rules.ts:105 keeps every earlier directory and drops only missing folders directly under <storage>/coaching (transient(), lines 40-43); line 106 throws RULES_LIMIT above 100 non-transient folders. No other code removes entries (adopt() at line 138 carries the list over). Worktrees are created under <storage>/worktrees/<id> (worktrees.ts:33,55), which transient() does not cover, and task-worktree.ts:16-20 calls rules-save for each new worktree whenever rules exist, because read() reports applied only for registered folders (rules.ts:70), even when the block is already in the checked-out files. After the limit, setUpWorktree fails and verifyLaunch (rules.ts:86-87) refuses sessions in the new worktree. RulesPanel.tsx:27-28 lists every registered folder, so removed ones show as errors. Far from the limit here, and worktrees have not been used on this computer.
- **Checker's measurements:** shared-rules.json on this computer: 10 registered folders, 9 of them non-transient (one is a coaching folder). No worktrees.json or worktrees folder under ~/.coding-hub, so about 91 more registrations before the limit.

#### Automatic session summaries list every file changed in the repository during the session, including other channels' work

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: improvement. ID: `hub-summary-misattributes-files`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/auto-memory.ts:327`
- **What the code does:** prepare() records `git status` lines at launch (line 296) and summarize() reports the lines that are new at the end (lines 325-327) under 'Files changed during the session'. Git status is per repository, not per channel. Lines already present at launch are dropped even if the session changed the file further.
- **What goes wrong:** Three channels work in one repository. Each one's summary lists the files all three changed, and the next session's launch context (memoryContext) presents them as that session's work; a file that was already modified at launch is left out even when the session rewrote it.
- **Suggested fix:** Word the list as 'Files changed in the repository while this session ran' when other runs shared the folder (PtyManager knows), or take the files from the session's own transcript (Edit and Write tool calls) instead of git status.
- **Checker's notes:** auto-memory.ts:296 stores the `git status --porcelain` lines at launch, and summarize() at lines 325-327 reports the lines that are new at the end under 'Files changed during the session (git status)' (line 335). Git status covers the whole repository, so with several channels in one repository each summary lists the others' files too; and a file already modified at launch keeps the same status line, so further edits by the session are left out (the comment at line 326 says so on purpose). Reachable in normal use, since several channels in one folder is the app's main use. Impact is limited to note accuracy: at most two automatic summaries enter the next launch context (line 73, 104), and they are presented as history to verify, not instructions.

### Phone server and Tailscale (11)

#### Auto-link gives a full-control computer key to anything on the same Tailscale login, or on this computer, with no code or confirmation

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: security. ID: `remote-server-autolink-no-consent`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:637`
- **What the code does:** owner() (637-640) trusts only the Tailscale-User-Login header; /api/auto-link (692-699) then issues a 'computer' key for any machineId. No prompt, no device-type check, no server-side list of removed computers. Read-only check on the installed 1.22.0: GET /api/hello on loopback with a hand-written header returned 200. screen-service-server.ts:92-94 accepts any computer key.
- **What goes wrong:** Any program on the person's phone or other same-login device, or another Windows account or local process here, POSTs /api/auto-link and gets a computer key. It can add a shell channel, start it and type commands, or open the Windows screen. Nothing is shown on this computer, and Remove under Other computers does not stop it asking again. Not exercised end to end: the POST would have added a device.
- **Suggested fix:** Ask on this computer the first time a new machineId auto-links (a dialog or a pending row in Settings), and keep removed machineIds in a server-side deny list. Alternatively require a secret both computers read from the Drive sync folder. Correct the test comment at remote-server.test.ts:111 and the dialog text at main.ts:680, which say a phone must be paired.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:637-640 owner() checks only autoLink (default true, line 260), tailscale.serving, and the Tailscale-User-Login header; :692-699 then calls issue('computer', ...) for any machineId with no prompt, no device-type check and no server-side deny list (revoke() at :380-389 only deletes the row; the 'stays unlinked' list is client-side, linked-computers.ts:17,146). A computer key passes every canRun gate (me() :400) and screen-service-server.ts:92-94 admits any kind==='computer' key. The Origin check (:662-664) stops web pages, so the attacker needs native code on a same-login tailnet device (the client-side android/ios filter in tailscale.ts:63 is not enforced by the server) or a local process/other Windows account that knows the login. Skeptic notes: this is a deliberate, user-visible feature with a switch (src/components/OtherComputers.tsx:41-42), the loopback case is acknowledged in the comment at :631-636, the linked device does appear in Settings, and the screen service names the viewer by the clock. Because it needs attacker code already running on one of the person's own devices, I rate it medium rather than high; the result (shell channel plus Windows screen, silently) is severe when it happens. The dialog text at main.ts:680 ('each phone still has to be paired') is indeed inaccurate for this path.
- **Checker's measurements:** Installed 1.22.0, loopback GET http://127.0.0.1:47821/api/hello (read-only): no header 403, wrong login 403, hand-written Tailscale-User-Login 200. remote.json here: enabled true, autoLink true; screen-share.json enabled true; tailscale serve status shows / -> 47821 and /api/screen -> 47822. POST /api/auto-link not exercised (it would add a device).

#### Turning phone access off removes every Tailscale handler on port 443, including /api/screen, and nothing puts it back until restart

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `remote-server-serve-off-drops-screen-path`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/tailscale.ts:101`
- **What the code does:** stopServing runs `serve --https=443 off` with no --set-path. Tailscale 1.104.7 (installed) then removes every mount on the port (serve_v2.go removeWebServe; its confirmation defaults to yes without a terminal). ScreenShare.serve() (screen-share.ts:137) runs only from ensure() at start (main.ts:620) and set(true); watch() only restarts the process. Live serve status shows / and /api/screen on 443.
- **What goes wrong:** Share this screen is on. The person turns Phone access off: /api/screen, and any path they added on 443 themselves, is deleted, despite the comment at tailscale.ts:96-100. They turn it on again: serveRemote (line 95) adds only /. Linked computers cannot open this Windows screen until Trace restarts.
- **Suggested fix:** In stopServing use ['serve','--https=443','--set-path=/','off'] so only Trace's root mount goes. After serveRemote succeeds in remote-serve (main.ts:683), call screenShare.ensure(). Have ScreenShare.watch() re-add the path when serve status lacks it. Extend the test at remote-server.test.ts:518-525, which pins the current command with a single handler, to a two-handler config.
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed, but this is the already-known open item ('turning phone access off and on drops the /api/screen serve path until restart'); what is new is the verified root cause. tailscale.ts:101 runs ['serve','--https=443','off'] with no --set-path, guarded only by tailscaleState().serving (true when / proxies Trace's port), so the comment at :96-100 ('one the person set up for something else is left alone') is false for other paths on 443. ScreenShare.serve() (screen-share.ts:137) is called only from set(true) (:79) and ensure() (:91); ensure() runs only at start (main.ts:620); watch() (:98-105) only calls start(); the remote-serve handler (main.ts:676-690) calls serveRemote only. ScreenShare.set(false) itself correctly uses --set-path (:84), which shows the intended form. Severity lowered: it needs the person to toggle Phone access off and on, and a restart (or toggling Share this screen) restores it.
- **Checker's measurements:** Tailscale installed: 1.104.7. Its public source at that tag (cmd/tailscale/cli/serve_v2.go removeWebServe, lines 1563-1590; util/prompt/prompt.go YesNo): with no --set-path every mount on the port is collected, and the confirmation returns its default (true) when stdin/stdout are not terminals, so all handlers are removed. Live serve status on this computer has two handlers on 443 (/ and /api/screen). Not exercised (would change Tailscale config).

#### A chat approval's text is sent uncapped, so a long one gets no phone alert and hides the channel on linked computers

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `remote-server-attention-unbounded`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:420`
- **What the code does:** pty-manager.ts:282 builds `Approve ${toolName}: ${description}` with no cap (codex-stream.ts:148 passes the whole command). remote-server.ts:420 sends it as the push body and line 569 as channel.attention. web-push.ts:55 declares one 4096-byte record. linked-computers.ts:21 allows attention up to 2000 characters and line 386 drops a channel that fails.
- **What goes wrong:** A Codex chat asks to run a 5 KB shell command. The push payload exceeds what push services accept, sender returns 'failed' and alert() ignores it, so the phone is not told. On a linked computer the channel fails channelSchema and vanishes from the list while it waits. Not reproduced; inferred from the code and the 4096-byte limit.
- **Suggested fix:** Cap the message where it is made (pty-manager.ts:282), as attention.ts:9 does at 200 characters, and slice the body in RemoteServer.alert() as desktopAlert does (main.ts:211). Add a test with a 5,000-character description.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described. pty-manager.ts:282 builds `Approve ${toolName}: ${description}` with no cap; codex-stream.ts:148 uses text(params.reason) || command and claude-stream.ts:147 text(request.description), and text() (line 5 / 9 of each) does not truncate, unlike attention.ts:9 (200 chars) for terminal channels. remote-server.ts:420 passes event.message as the push body; alert() (:235-236) acts only on 'gone', so a 'failed' send is silently dropped; web-push.ts:55 declares a single 4096-byte record. :567-569 put the same text in channel.attention, and linked-computers.ts:21 limits attention to 2000 characters while :386 drops any channel that fails the schema (and :387-388 then dispose its mirror and chat replay). So the linked-computer half is deterministic once the message passes 2,000 characters, and the push half once it nears 4 KB. Not established: how often a real approval description is that long (Codex uses `reason` when present; only ask-level chat channels raise approvals) and the push service's actual rejection. Hence PLAUSIBLE, and low rather than medium: narrow trigger, but when it hits the person misses a 'needs you' on the phone and the channel disappears on the linked computer.
- **Checker's measurements:** Proxy only: in this computer's 127 Codex rollouts (~/.codex/sessions), 47 of 752 exec tool inputs (6%) are longer than 1,985 characters and 6 (0.8%) longer than 3,900; max 6,209. These are whole tool inputs, not the approval request's command field, and not necessarily from Trace chat channels, so they show long commands exist but not that a long approval occurred.

#### A phone that reconnects can miss a channel-list change, because the last-sent list is not updated for it

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `remote-server-stale-channel-list`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:473`
- **What the code does:** The debounced check (473, 585) broadcasts only when JSON.stringify(channels()) differs from sentChannels. sentChannels is set only in broadcastChannels (578), which returns early with no streams (577). A new stream gets its list through opening() at 736 without updating sentChannels.
- **What goes wrong:** Phone connected, list X is broadcast. Phone disconnects. A linked computer drops, so the list becomes Y with nothing sent. Phone reconnects and receives Y. The linked computer returns, the list is X again, it equals the stale sentChannels, and nothing is broadcast. The phone does not show that computer's channels until something else changes.
- **Suggested fix:** Set sentChannels (or clear it) when a stream is given its opening list at line 736, or compare per stream. Add a test: broadcast, disconnect, change, reconnect, change back.
- **Checker's notes:** Read the code; the logic holds. sentChannels is assigned only at remote-server.ts:578 inside broadcastChannels(), which returns at :577 when there are no streams. The debounced paths (:472-473 channelsChanged, :585 relayChanged) return early with no streams and otherwise broadcast only if JSON.stringify(channels()) differs from sentChannels. A new stream gets its list via opening() at :736, which does not touch sentChannels. So A (broadcast) -> disconnect -> B (nothing sent) -> reconnect (gets B) -> A again compares equal to the stale value and is not sent. Direct broadcastChannels() calls (started, local attention, answered, exit, :415-428, :760) do not compare, so they are unaffected; the affected changes are workspace edits, sizes and linked computers' channel states. A realistic variant: a linked computer's channel starts waiting while the phone is asleep, the phone reconnects and shows it waiting, it is answered, the relay list returns to the earlier bytes, and the phone keeps showing 'waiting' until something else changes. The phone has no polling fallback (phone/ uses only the SSE 'channels' event, phone/main.ts:638). It needs a byte-identical return to the previous list (same runId, cols, rows, topic), so it is occasional and self-heals on the next change: low.

#### The phone Screen pipe ignores backpressure, so a slow phone makes Trace's main process buffer screen data without limit

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: resource-leak. ID: `remote-server-screen-pipe-no-backpressure`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:336`
- **What the code does:** pipe() does `server.on('data', (data) => socket.write(frame(data)))` and never checks the return value, pauses the source, or caps socket.writableLength. frame() also copies every chunk. The SSE path has a 2 MB cap (line 600); this path has none.
- **What goes wrong:** A phone on a weak mobile link opens Screen on a Mac. macOS Screen Sharing sends updates faster than the phone reads them, and Node queues them in the main process's memory until the phone catches up or disconnects. Not measured; the Windows service limits itself to two updates in flight, so this mainly affects a Mac target.
- **Suggested fix:** When socket.write returns false, pause the source and resume it on the socket's 'drain'; or destroy the connection once socket.writableLength passes a few megabytes, as write() does for streams.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described: remote-server.ts:336 does server.on('data', (data) => socket.write(frame(data))) with no check of the return value, no pause/resume and no writableLength cap (the reverse direction at :328 is the same), whereas the SSE path caps at 2 MB (:600). But 'without limit' is not established. The phone client is noVNC (phone/main.ts:229), and standard RFB is request-driven: the server sends an update only after a FramebufferUpdateRequest, which noVNC sends after it has processed the previous one, so what queues in the main process is bounded by roughly one update unless the server pushes unrequested updates (not shown for macOS Screen Sharing). On Windows the source is Trace's own service, which waits on socket drain and limits updates in flight (rfb-server.ts:249-250), though that drain is on the loopback hop, not the phone hop. This computer is Windows, at most two screens are allowed (MAX_SCREENS, :80), and remote-devices.json shows no authenticated phone request since 2026-11-16. Missing hardening rather than a demonstrated leak.

#### Phone and linked-computer requests do synchronous file work on Trace's main process (measured; none near 1 s)

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `remote-server-sync-work-on-main`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:917`
- **What the code does:** /api/memory (915-919) reads every note of each channel's project to return 30. /api/transcript (808-816) reads and parses up to 8 MB. body() (613-617) and chatRequestSchema parse up to 147 MB. devices() (254) re-reads remote-devices.json several times per request. ScreenMirror parses all output in the main process while phone access is on.
- **What goes wrong:** The person opens Memory or Conversation on the phone, or sends images, while working at the desktop: the main process pauses for the measured times. This does not explain the reported 1-3 s stops. A six-minute probe of the installed app (1,766 requests through this server) saw a median 0.5 ms and one 132 ms answer.
- **Blocking time:** Main process, measured on this computer's data: memory list 13-40 ms per project (largest 785 notes), 95 ms for all ten; transcript tail of 8.4 MB 23 ms; chat body at the 147 MB limit about 220 ms; mirror snapshot 7-28 ms per channel; workspace load 0.06 ms; Tailscale CLI start 4-8 ms.
- **Suggested fix:** Read notes and transcripts with fs.promises or in a worker, and list only the newest 30 notes. Cache the device list in memory and reload it when the file changes. Skip building the output event when there are no streams (line 451).
- **Checker's notes:** Code confirmed: /api/memory (remote-server.ts:915-919) calls hub.memory.list() per channel project (main.ts:613), which synchronously reads, parses and validates every note (memory.ts:75-79) to return 30; /api/transcript goes to chatHistory, a synchronous read of up to 8 MB (chat/transcript.ts:5,19); body() (:613-617) buffers and JSON.parses up to CHAT_BODY (147 MB, :139) on the main process; devices() (:254) re-reads and zod-parses remote-devices.json on each call (local-store.ts:6-11), several times per request; with phone access on, every channel's output is written into a headless xterm in the main process (:450, screen-mirror.ts:17) and an SSE payload string is built at :451 even with no streams. All of it is small on this data and the request-driven parts run only when a phone or linked computer asks; none has asked since Nov 16, so these paths cannot be the cause of the reported 1-3 s stops, as the auditor also says. Low is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only replica of MemoryService.list() (memory.ts:73-80, with the repo's zod schema) on the real ~/.coding-hub/memory, 3 rounds: all 10 projects 98-125 ms; per project 0.4-48 ms (785 notes: 41-48 ms; 336 notes: 17-22 ms; 250 notes: 13-24 ms). This matches the auditor's 13-40 ms per project and 95 ms total. remote-devices.json: last authenticated request (lastSeen, rewritten at most every 5 min by :645) was 2026-11-16 17:29Z for the linked computer and 15:35Z for the phone, file mtime Nov 16 09:29 local; so no paired device has called this server for about 36 hours.

#### A phone without Can start and stop agents can still start AI reviews, write agent memory and run commands in a running terminal

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: security. ID: `remote-server-canrun-not-boundary`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:930`
- **What the code does:** /api/coaching-review (930-935) and POST /api/memory (920-929) check only that the device is paired. /api/input (752-762) writes up to 4096 characters into any running PTY, shells included. /api/chat (705-721) passes 'answer' with allow and allowEdits. docs/reference.md:333 does say to treat a paired phone like an unlocked keyboard.
- **What goes wrong:** A phone left at the default (canRun off) types `claude` into a running Terminal channel, approves a chat's tool request, or posts handoff notes that both agents read in their next session. Each handoff also runs the Drive mirror on the main process (about 0.35 s per the known history), with no rate limit.
- **Suggested fix:** Either state in Settings that the switch covers only start, stop, add, tasks and Screen, or gate typing into shell channels, coaching reviews and memory writes behind it too. Rate-limit POST /api/memory per device.
- **Checker's notes:** The code facts are right: /api/coaching-review (remote-server.ts:930-935) and POST /api/memory (:920-929) check only that the device is paired; /api/input (:752-762) writes up to 4096 characters into any running PTY, shells included; /api/chat (:705-721) gates only table-open on canRun. POST /api/memory reaches hub.execute('memory-write') (main.ts:614), which calls drive.sync(project) synchronously (hub/service.ts:92), with no rate limit. But most of this is stated design, not a defect: docs/reference.md:333 says a paired phone can type into running channels 'the same as typing at the desktop', answer approvals, and should be treated 'like an unlocked keyboard'; :323 lists exactly what the switch covers; the comment at :921 makes the memory write deliberate. Only the coaching review (a small Haiku call, coaching/live.ts:134-142) and memory writes are not spelled out for users. The stall angle needs the person's own paired phone to post handoffs repeatedly, and each costs the already-known ~0.35 s sync. So the description is accurate but whether it is a real problem is a product/wording question; at most a low-severity clarification of the Settings text.

#### A phone's size can stay on a desktop channel after the phone has gone

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `remote-server-forced-size-outlives-phone`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:512`
- **What the code does:** phoneSize() and apply() (505-519) never check that the device has a live stream on that channel. Holds are released only by the stream's close handler (729-735), DELETE /api/size, exit, or personInput (487). desktopResize (495-498) holds back the desktop's own resizes while a hold exists.
- **What goes wrong:** The phone taps Fit to phone and is locked or loses signal. If POST /api/size is handled after the stream's close handler ran, or the dead connection is not noticed for minutes on a quiet channel, the tile stays drawn at phone width and ignores window resizes until the person types in that channel. Not reproduced.
- **Suggested fix:** In apply(), refuse or drop a hold when no stream of that device follows the session. On the 25 s heartbeat (line 272), release holds whose device has no stream left.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts: phoneSize() (505-510) and apply() (512-519) only check that the run exists; neither looks at this.streams. A forced hold is removed only by the stream's request 'close' handler (729-735), DELETE /api/size (831-833), a started/exit event (412) or personInput (487); checkDesktop (538) skips forced holds, and the 25 s heartbeat (272) only writes pings. desktopResize (495-498) returns true while a hold exists, and main.ts:845 then drops the desktop's resize. So a hold set by a POST handled when the device has no stream on that channel has no owner left to release it. What I could not establish is the trigger. The phone narrows it: it sends DELETE /api/size on visibilitychange hidden (phone/main.ts:673-677) and on leaving a channel (354), and requestSize refuses to send while hidden (337, 342), so the race needs a POST already in flight, or sent in the 2 s reconnect gap (phone/api.ts:189), followed by the phone never reconnecting. The 'dead connection unnoticed for minutes' variant depends on how long tailscaled keeps its loopback connection to Trace open after the phone vanishes, which I did not measure. Impact is cosmetic and temporary: the tile stays at phone width and ignores window resizes until a key is typed in that channel (mouse and focus reports do not count, shared/terminal-input.ts:17-19), the run restarts, or the phone reopens and leaves the channel. The comment at 502-503 says a forced size is meant to last until the person types, so only the 'phone is gone' case is a defect. Not reproduced by the reporter or by me. Line 512 is correct.
- **Checker's measurements:** None for this finding (reproducing needs a phone and would resize a live channel). On this computer phone access is on, with 1 phone and 1 linked computer paired, so the path is in use.

#### The cross-site check treats a page on any localhost port as Trace's own, and /api/pair accepts it

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: security. ID: `remote-server-origin-any-localhost-port`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:653`
- **What the code does:** allowedHost() strips the port (653) and accepts 127.0.0.1 and localhost, and handle() applies it to the Origin header too (664). Checked on the installed app: a POST to /api/pair with Origin http://localhost:5173 reached the handler, while Origin https://example.com got 403. /api/pair (679-686) does not check Content-Type.
- **What goes wrong:** A web page served on any local port, such as a dev server an agent started, sends eleven simple POSTs to /api/pair while a code is showing. The eleventh cancels the pairing (line 682). It cannot read replies, so it gets no key. Other routes still need the device key, so the impact is limited to this.
- **Suggested fix:** For Origin, compare the whole origin (scheme, host and port) with this server's own address and the tailnet https address, rather than reusing allowedHost. Require Content-Type application/json on POST routes.
- **Checker's notes:** allowedHost() (remote-server.ts:652-656) strips ':<port>' and accepts 127.0.0.1 and localhost; handle() passes new URL(origin).host to it at 663-664, so the Origin's port and scheme are ignored. /api/pair (679-686) is reached before any device-key check and body() (613-617) parses JSON whatever the Content-Type, so a no-preflight text/plain POST from a page on another loopback port is handled. Each well-formed attempt runs ++pairing.attempts (682) and the 11th sets this.pairing = null. I looked for wider reach and found none: every other route needs Authorization or Tailscale-User-Login, both of which force a preflight, and the preflight gets 401 with no Access-Control-Allow-Origin (only 'sibling' origins get CORS headers, 665-669); the page cannot read replies, and 10 tries against a 10-character code from a 31-letter alphabet is not a guessing risk. Real impact is therefore limited to cancelling a pairing during its 5 minute window, and it needs hostile script served on a localhost port; a local program could do the same with no Origin header at all. Low is right, at the bottom of low. Line 653 is correct.
- **Checker's measurements:** Against the installed app on 127.0.0.1:47821, using schema-invalid bodies that return before the attempt counter: POST /api/pair with Origin http://localhost:5173 -> 401 (handler reached); Origin https://example.com -> 403 'Cross-site request refused.'; OPTIONS /api/channels with Origin http://localhost:5173 -> 401, no Access-Control-Allow-Origin; Host evil.example -> 421.

#### An oversized or malformed request body gets 500 'The remote request failed.' instead of a 413 or 400 that says why

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `remote-server-generic-500-on-bad-body`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:615`
- **What the code does:** body() throws HubError('REMOTE_BODY') past the limit (615) and JSON.parse throws on bad JSON (616). Neither is caught in handle(), so the server's catch at line 266 answers 500 with a generic message. Checked on the installed app: a 40 KB body and a non-JSON body to /api/pair both returned 500.
- **What goes wrong:** A phone sends a chat message whose images exceed the body limit, or a handoff over 256 KB. The phone shows 'The remote request failed.' with no hint that size is the cause. For very large bodies the connection may be reset before the reply arrives (inferred from Node's stream handling, not observed).
- **Suggested fix:** Catch in body(): answer 413 with 'Request too large.' and 400 for invalid JSON, and drain or close the request deliberately.
- **Checker's notes:** The mechanism holds: body() throws HubError('REMOTE_BODY') past the limit (615) and JSON.parse throws on bad JSON (616); no route catches either (the try blocks at 712, 756, 830 start after the body is read), so the server's catch at 266 answers 500 'The remote request failed.'. The stated scenarios are narrower than claimed, though. The shipped phone app cannot exceed either limit: chat images are capped at 10 of 4.5 MB each (src/components/chat/RemoteChat.tsx:80, ChatParts.tsx:17-25; about 60 MB of base64 against CHAT_BODY of about 147 MB), and the handoff textarea has maxLength 20000 (phone/screens.tsx:102) against 256 KB. The one path I found that reaches it through the UI is a linked computer pasting a clipboard image of about 15 to 15.75 MB: the sender's IPC schema allows 21 MiB of base64 (electron/main.ts:259) while the receiver's body limit is 20,972,544 bytes (858), so the person sees the generic message instead of 'up to 15 MB'. Otherwise it affects only hand-made or buggy clients. Also, phone/api.ts:47 turns any status >= 500 on a stream connection into 'Trace is not open there', which shows how a generic 500 misleads, though that path sends no body. Severity low, and close to a nit for the phone. Line 615 is correct.
- **Checker's measurements:** Installed app: POST /api/pair with body 'not json' -> 500 'The remote request failed.'; with a 40 KB JSON body -> 500, same message. Stand-alone copy of body() and the catch on Node 24.19 (loopback, no Tailscale proxy): 40 KB and 300 KB -> 500 in 1 ms; 5 MB -> ECONNRESET after 6.0 s; 50 MB -> 500 in 20 ms. So the reply to a large body is timing-dependent, which supports the reporter's inferred reset.

#### Every push alert tells the push service the private repository's address

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: security. ID: `remote-server-vapid-sub-private-repo`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/web-push.ts:37`
- **What the code does:** vapidHeader() signs a token whose `sub` is the literal 'https://github.com/KGeary1995/Coding-Hub'. AGENTS.md describes that repository as private and names trace-releases as the public one. The token goes to Google, Apple, Mozilla or Microsoft with each alert.
- **What goes wrong:** Each alert sent from any Trace install discloses the owner's GitHub account and the private repository's name to the push service, and the link is a 404 for an operator who tries to use it as the contact address.
- **Suggested fix:** Use the public releases repository's address or a mailto: contact as `sub`.
- **Checker's notes:** electron/services/remote/web-push.ts:37 builds the VAPID token with the literal sub 'https://github.com/KGeary1995/Coding-Hub', and pushSender (63-71) sends it in the Authorization header of every push to the phone's push service. AGENTS.md:16-17 and docs/windows-releases.md:28 call that repository private and trace-releases the public one, so the link is a 404 as a contact address (RFC 8292 expects a mailto: or https: contact). The disclosure is small: the GitHub account name is already public through the update feed (electron-builder.cjs:68, shared/updates.ts:13), so the only new information the push service gets is the private repository's name, inside a signed token, not its contents. The token is base64url, not encrypted, so the push service can read it. No one else sees it. Low, closer to informational; the fix is one string. Line 37 is correct.
- **Checker's measurements:** On this computer (read-only): remote.json has enabled=true; remote-devices.json has 2 devices (1 linked computer, 1 phone), 1 with alerts on, endpoint host fcm.googleapis.com. So each alert here does send this sub to Google's push service.

### Linked computers, screen sharing and the phone app (12)

#### Auto-link hands a full computer key to any client with the same Tailscale login, with no code, prompt or notice

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: security. ID: `remote-linked-phone-autolink-no-consent`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:692`
- **What the code does:** `owner()` (637-640) trusts a request whose `Tailscale-User-Login` header equals this computer's login. `/api/auto-link` (692-699) then calls `issue('computer', ...)`. `autoLink` defaults to on (260; on here). The caller's kind is never checked (outbound `tailscalePeers` skips phones, inbound does not), no removed-machine list is consulted, and the server listens on 127.0.0.1:47821 (268), where a local process can write the header itself.
- **What goes wrong:** Any app on a device signed in to the same Tailscale account (the phone included), or another Windows account on this PC, sends one POST and gets a computer key: it can type into shells, start channels and, with Share this screen on (it is here), view and control the desktop. After Remove under Other computers it can simply ask again.
- **Suggested fix:** Ask on this computer before the first auto-link of a machine ID, or deliver the key by calling the requester back at its address from `tailscale status` instead of in the reply. Remember removed machine IDs here and refuse them. Refuse auto-link when Host is loopback. Add tests for a phone-OS caller, a loopback caller and a removed machine.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as claimed. owner() (remote-server.ts:637-640) only compares the Tailscale-User-Login header with this computer's login; /api/auto-link (692-699) then calls issue('computer', ...), and me() (400) gives a computer key canRun always. Nothing checks the caller's device type, and no removed-machine list is read on the serving side: the declined list (linked-computers.ts:119-123, 146) is only consulted by an honest requester. The server listens on 127.0.0.1 (268) and allowedHost accepts loopback, so a local process can write the header itself; the code comment at 631-636 admits this for the same user but not for another Windows account. screen-service-server.ts:89-94 admits any 'computer' key from remote-devices.json, so with Share this screen on the key also gives the desktop. Guards that do hold: a web page cannot do it (Origin check at 662-664, Funnel refused at 661). Severity lowered from high: this is a deliberate, labelled setting (OtherComputers.tsx:41-42 'Link my computers automatically'), and the attacker must already run code on a device signed in to the person's own Tailscale account or on this PC. The Settings text 'a computer you unlink stays unlinked until you link it with a code' is not enforced against a non-Trace caller. Not exercised against the live app.
- **Checker's measurements:** This computer, read-only: remote.json = {enabled:true, autoLink:true}; screen-share.json = {enabled:true}; remote-devices.json holds 1 computer and 1 phone. No request was sent to the running app.

#### Saved phone app waits on the network with no time limit when its computer is off

- **Check:** Plausible, medium (auditor said medium). Category: ux. ID: `remote-linked-phone-sw-no-timeout`
- **Where:** `phone/public/sw.js:12`
- **What the code does:** The fetch handler (9-20) always awaits `fetch(request)` and uses the saved app only when that rejects or returns 5xx; there is no time limit. The stream code already allows for a computer that is off not refusing quickly: it aborts after 6 s (phone/api.ts:155).
- **What goes wrong:** The app's own computer is asleep or off while the phone's Tailscale is connected. The page request gets neither an answer nor a refusal, so the installed app shows a blank screen until the browser's own connection timeout before the saved copy opens and reaches the other computer. Not tested on a phone; it rests on an offline tailnet computer not answering connection attempts.
- **Suggested fix:** For page loads that have a saved copy, race the network against about 3 s (AbortSignal.timeout), serve the cache on timeout and refresh it in the background.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described. phone/public/sw.js:9-20 awaits fetch(request) with no time limit and reads the saved copy only when that rejects (19) or answers 5xx (16). The same applies to every script and stylesheet request, so if connections hang, the page and then its assets each wait a full browser connection timeout, one after the other. The app's own design relies on the saved copy opening while the computer is off (sw.js:2-3, api.ts:39-43, docs/RELEASE_NOTES.md:19), and the stream code already guards against a slow non-answer with a 6 s abort (api.ts:155), which supports the premise. Not established: that a connection to an off or sleeping tailnet computer hangs rather than failing fast on the phone; that depends on Tailscale and the phone's browser and could not be tested here. If it fails fast, the saved copy opens promptly and there is no defect. Kept at medium because, if the premise holds, it defeats a feature the 1.21.3 notes describe to the person, on the phone only.

#### Any *.ts.net address from the Drive folder is trusted with a link key and pairing code

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: security. ID: `remote-linked-phone-tsnet-any-tailnet`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/linked-computers.ts:35`
- **What the code does:** `linkableAddress` (32-37) accepts every `https://*.ts.net` host, which includes other tailnets' public Funnel names. `status()` (85-89) lets the Drive folder's address for an ID override the saved link's; `access()` (157-161) gives that URL plus the link key to `openTraceScreen` (main.ts:638-640), which sends `Authorization: Bearer`. `link()` (97-100) posts the pairing code to the same address.
- **What goes wrong:** Someone able to write the Drive sync folder (a Drive-connected app, a shared folder, a stolen Google session) points computers/<id>.json at their own Funnel host. The next time that computer's Screen is opened here, its link key goes to their server, which can then control that computer. Not exercised; code path only.
- **Suggested fix:** Accept only hosts inside this computer's own tailnet (same suffix as its `tailscale status` DNS name, or present in `tailscalePeers`). Send a key only to the address saved with the link, updating it only from a tailnet peer that answers /api/hello with the same ID. Add tests with a foreign-tailnet address in the Drive record.
- **Checker's notes:** Code path verified: linkableAddress (32-37) accepts any https host ending .ts.net; in status() (85, 89) the Drive record (device-sync.ts:193-196, read from computers/<id>.json) overrides both the tailnet-found address and the saved link's URL; access() (157-161) returns that URL with the link key; main.ts:638-640 passes both to ScreenSharing, and screen-sharing.ts:123/71 writes 'Authorization: Bearer <key>' to that host. link() (97-100) posts the pairing code to the same address. Ordinary relay requests use the saved link.url (311), so only Screen and code linking follow the Drive address. What is not established is the last step of the scenario: the stolen key is only usable by someone who can reach the real computer's remote, which listens on loopback behind tailscale serve and refuses Funnel (remote-server.ts:661, screen-service-server.ts:86), so the attacker also needs to be on the person's tailnet. A writer of the Drive sync folder already controls synced shared rules and memory that agents read, which is a stronger position. Real weakness in address trust, but severity is low rather than medium.

#### Administrator screen service runs user-writable code and trusts a user-writable allow-list

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: security. ID: `remote-linked-phone-elevated-task-user-writable`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/screen-share.ts:43`
- **What the code does:** `registerTaskScript` (38-47) creates a RunLevel Highest task running `process.execPath --screen-service --storage=<root> --elevated`; `start()` runs it with `schtasks /Run`, no prompt (119). The install is per-user and unpacked (electron-builder.cjs:28 `asar: false`, :71 `perMachine: false`; seen here under AppData\Local\Programs). The service admits whoever `<root>/remote-devices.json` or screen-service-local.json names (screen-service-server.ts:89-92).
- **What goes wrong:** With 'Also control apps running as administrator' on, any program running as the person edits resources\app\dist-electron\electron\screen-service.js, or adds its own key hash to remote-devices.json, and runs the task: its code runs as administrator, or it drives administrator windows through 127.0.0.1:47822, with no Windows prompt. The option is off on this computer.
- **Suggested fix:** Start the administrator service only from a location standard users cannot write (a per-machine copy checked by signature or hash), and keep its switch and allow-list in an administrator-only folder. Until then, say in Settings what turning the option on gives up.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as claimed. registerTaskScript (screen-share.ts:38-47) registers a RunLevel Highest task for process.execPath with '--screen-service --storage=<root> --elevated'; start() (118-119) runs it with schtasks /Run without a prompt. electron-builder.cjs:28 (asar: false) and :71 (perMachine: false) put the program and its JavaScript in a folder the user can write, and nothing checks a signature or hash before the elevated run. electron/screen-service.ts:28,34 decides whether to run from screen-share.json under the user-writable storage root, and screen-service-server.ts:89-94 admits any key whose hash is in remote-devices.json there (or screen-service-local.json). So with the option on, a program running as the person gets administrator code execution or input into administrator windows with no Windows prompt (a standing UAC bypass). Severity lowered: the option is opt-in, needs one approved Windows prompt to register, and is off on this computer (no task). It would be medium for anyone who turns it on.
- **Checker's measurements:** This computer, read-only: install is at AppData\Local\Programs\AI Coding Hub\Trace with resources\app\dist-electron\electron\screen-service.js as a plain 6,953-byte file (asar off); screen-share.json has no 'elevated'; 'schtasks /Query /TN "Trace screen sharing (administrator)"' reports the task does not exist.

#### Sizes and data from a linked computer are used unchecked: one event can take gigabytes and stall the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: security. ID: `remote-linked-phone-unbounded-linked-sizes`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/linked-computers.ts:405`
- **What the code does:** `event()` (403-423) passes `cols`/`rows` from `replay`, `resize` and the channel list into `new ScreenMirror`/`resize` and on to the window and phones; `channelSchema` (21) bounds neither, and the stream buffer (343) has no cap. Measured with the repo's @xterm/headless on Node 24: 200000x2000 took 1.3 s and 4.6 GB; 2000000x400 took 4.3 s and 9.3 GB.
- **What goes wrong:** A linked computer that is compromised or buggy, or any same-login tailnet device that answers /api/hello (it is auto-linked within a minute), sends one replay event naming a huge size. Trace's main process stalls for seconds and takes gigabytes, and the same size is forwarded to the window's terminal and to phones.
- **Suggested fix:** Validate these events with the limits the server already uses (`sizeSchema`: cols 20-300, rows 6-200), cap `data` and the pending buffer at a few MB, and drop the connection on a violation. Add tests for oversized sizes and an endless event.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as claimed. event() (linked-computers.ts:403-423) only tests 'event.cols && event.rows' for truthiness before new ScreenMirror(runId, cols, rows) (407) and mirror.resize (420); channelSchema (21) gives cols/rows no range and they feed the fallback at 414; screen-mirror.ts:13-19 passes them straight to xterm with no clamp. The same values are emitted to the window (408, 422; src/runtime/linked-terminals.ts:84-85 resizes the real terminal) and to phones (423). The stream buffer at 343 grows without limit until a blank line arrives. The try/catch at 348 catches a thrown allocation error but not the time or memory. The server side does bound sizes (sizeSchema, remote-server.ts:135), so an honest Trace never sends such values: the trigger needs a compromised or faulty linked computer, or a same-login tailnet device that answers /api/hello and /api/auto-link (discover(), 139-150, links it without asking). Low is the right severity; it is not a cause of the reported 1-3 s freezes in normal use.
- **Checker's measurements:** Repo's @xterm/headless on Node 24, new Terminal({cols,rows,scrollback:1000}) plus one write: 80x24 2 ms/1 MB; 300x200 1 ms/2 MB; 20000x2000 163 ms/462 MB; 100000x400 108 ms/463 MB (about 11.5 bytes per cell, linear). That extrapolates to about 4.6 GB and 1.1-1.6 s for 200000x2000, matching the claim; I did not allocate gigabytes on the person's machine. Negative and fractional sizes did not throw.

#### An invalid linked-computers.json leaves Trace with no window, and unchecked replies can make it invalid

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `remote-linked-phone-links-file-stops-startup`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:633`
- **What the code does:** `links()` (79) reads the file with a strict schema (43-character key, at most 20 entries) and throws otherwise. `keep()` (114-118) saves the other side's reply unchecked: `body.token` is only tested for truthiness (103, 149) and nothing caps the list. `linked.start()` (main.ts:633) runs unguarded inside `app.whenReady().then`, before `createWindow()` (851).
- **What goes wrong:** A computer answering auto-link with a malformed key, or under a new ID each minute (21 entries), or a file left incomplete by a crash (`save()` renames without flushing, local-store.ts:18), makes every `links()` throw: status updates throw in a timer, and the next launch stops before the window is created until the file is removed by hand.
- **Suggested fix:** Check the returned key against the schema and cap the list before saving. Read links one entry at a time and drop bad ones (as channels already are, 386). Catch a failed `linked.start()` so linking is reported unavailable instead of stopping startup. Add a test that starts with an invalid file.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as claimed. links() (linked-computers.ts:79) uses load() with a strict schema (43-character key, max 20), and load() (local-store.ts:9-10) throws HubError when the file exists but does not parse. linked.start() at main.ts:633 runs with no try/catch inside the single app.whenReady().then callback (491-866), which has no .catch, so a throw there skips every later handle() registration and createWindow() at 851: Trace runs with no window and no message, and the second-instance handler (489) does nothing because there is no window. keep() (114-118) saves body.token after only a truthiness test (103, 149) and does not cap the list; after a bad save, notify()'s timer (320) calls status() which throws as an uncaught exception (main.ts:75 only records it). save() (local-store.ts:18) does not flush before rename. Severity lowered from medium because every trigger is rare: a real Trace always returns a 43-character key (remote-server.ts:620), 21 linked computers is not a normal state, and the file is only written on link or unlink, so a power cut must land in that moment. The primary defect is the unguarded call in main.ts:633 rather than line 79.
- **Checker's measurements:** This computer, read-only: linked-computers.json has 1 entry with a 43-character key and only the 5 schema fields, so it is valid now.

#### Typed input has no timeout: one stalled request holds every later key, then all arrive late

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `remote-linked-phone-input-queue-no-timeout`
- **Where:** `phone/api.ts:63`
- **What the code does:** `send()` (61-66) chains every key on one promise queue and `api()` (32-37) passes no timeout, so a stalled POST holds all later input and the rest go out when it settles. `type()` (phone/main.ts:624-632) shows nothing while waiting. `LinkedComputers.input` (linked-computers.ts:173) is likewise the one relay call without `AbortSignal.timeout`; the window queues behind it (src/runtime/linked-terminals.ts:49).
- **What goes wrong:** On a weak mobile connection the person taps y, sees nothing, then taps Enter and Ctrl C. The first request hangs until the browser gives up or the network returns; the queued keys then arrive together and answer whatever prompt the agent shows by then. The run ID is unchanged, so the computer accepts them.
- **Suggested fix:** Give input requests a short timeout (about 8 s). When one fails, discard what is queued behind it and say so. Show a sending state on the keys and Send. Add a test with a fetch that never settles.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as claimed. send() (phone/api.ts:61-66) chains each key on one promise and sets queue = delivery.catch(() => {}), so later keys go out after the earlier request settles, whether it succeeded or failed; api() (32-37) passes no signal or timeout. The key buttons call 'void type(key.data)' (phone/main.ts:413) and type() (624-632) shows nothing until the request settles. LinkedComputers.input (linked-computers.ts:173) is the only relay call without AbortSignal.timeout (compare 177, 184, 190, 219), it is also used for a phone typing into a linked computer's channel (301-305, remote-server.ts:757), and the window queues behind it (src/runtime/linked-terminals.ts:49). The server accepts late input for the same run (inputSchema and host.write check only sessionId and runId, remote-server.ts:752-758). So keys tapped during a network stall are delivered together later, or the first is dropped and the rest still sent, and they answer whatever prompt is showing then. Not run on a phone; how long a stalled request hangs depends on the browser and network. Severity lowered from medium: it needs a stall plus continued tapping, order is preserved, and it is unrelated to the desktop freezes; the real risk is an unintended answer to a newer agent prompt.

#### Phone's direct fallback to another computer only works for addresses in the Drive folder

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `remote-linked-phone-sibling-origins-drive-only`
- **Where:** `electron/main.ts:644`
- **What the code does:** `phone.allowedOrigins` lists only `devices.advertised()`, the Drive sync folder's addresses. That list is the page's CSP `connect-src` (remote-server.ts:676) and the other computer's cross-origin allowance (663-669). Keys for other computers are given to the phone for every linked computer (`/api/computer-key`, 790-795), including ones linked over the tailnet without Drive sync.
- **What goes wrong:** Two computers linked by auto-link with sync between computers off or on different Drive folders: the phone stores a key for the second, but when the first is off its requests are blocked by the saved page's CSP or refused as cross-site, and the app only says the first can't be reached. A page loaded before the first Drive sync after launch has the same gap.
- **Suggested fix:** Build the allowed origins from linked computers' addresses (links and tailnet-found ones) as well as Drive's, and have the phone reload its page when the list it was served with changes. Not run with two computers; this computer has Drive sync on.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as claimed. phone.allowedOrigins (main.ts:644) is built only from devices.advertised(), which device-sync.ts:188-200 fills solely from the Drive sync folder's computers/*.json. That list is the served page's CSP connect-src (remote-server.ts:676) and the only cross-origin allowance on the other computer (663-669; also the screen upgrade at 307); a request from another origin gets 403 'Cross-site request refused' (664), including the preflight that the Authorization header forces. Yet /api/computers (785-789) lists every connected linked computer and /api/computer-key (790-795) hands the phone a key for each, including ones linked over the tailnet without a shared Drive folder (linked-computers.ts:59-60, 134-150 exist for exactly that case). The phone's follow() (phone/api.ts:153) then tries those siblings directly and fails, and only the home computer's problem is reported (184). So the direct fallback silently does not work for computers linked without the same Drive folder. Not run with two computers. Low is right: a degraded fallback in one configuration, with no data loss; whether this person is affected was not checked beyond the fact that Drive sync is configured here.

#### Opening a linked computer's stream or tab serializes every channel's screen back to back on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `remote-linked-phone-stream-open-serialize`
- **Where:** `electron/services/remote/remote-server.ts:742`
- **What the code does:** `/api/stream?all=1` (741-748) snapshots every channel's mirror; `ScreenMirror.snapshot` (screen-mirror.ts:21-23) runs xterm's synchronous `serialize()` over up to 1,000 scrollback lines in Trace's main process, one channel after another. `LinkedComputers.watch` (linked-computers.ts:254-258) does the same when a linked computer's tab opens.
- **What goes wrong:** Each time the linked Mac's Trace connects or reconnects (waking, a network change), or its tab is opened here, the main process is busy for the sum of all channels' serializations; a phone opening a channel costs one. A short hitch, not the reported 1-3 s stall.
- **Blocking time:** Trace main process. Measured outside Trace with the repo's @xterm/headless on Node 24: 6-36 ms per channel with a full scrollback (120x40 to 300x80). Estimated 0.05-0.3 s for eight channels, once per connect.
- **Suggested fix:** Serialize one channel per turn (setImmediate between them), or keep the mirrors in a utility process.
