- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. remote-server.ts:741-748 calls mirror.snapshot for every entry of this.replay when a linked computer opens /api/stream?all=1 (phones are refused at 724; a phone costs one mirror, 743). screen-mirror.ts:21-23 runs SerializeAddon.serialize({scrollback:1000}) inside xterm's write callback, and RemoteServer is built in Trace's main process (main.ts:566). linked-computers.ts:254-258 does the same for a linked computer's mirrors on 'linked-watch' (main.ts:321), which LinkedComputerView.tsx:123 sends on tab open, reconnect and each new run. xterm 6.0.0 schedules each terminal's callback with its own setTimeout, but they all land in one timers pass, so the loop is held for the sum (measured). Reachable here: remote.json has enabled:true, one linked computer and one phone are paired. Severity low is right: once per connect or tab open, tens to low hundreds of ms, not a periodic 1-3 s stall. Note the mirror count is capped at 32 (REPLAY_CHANNELS, line 155), not 8, and mirrors stay after a run exits until the channel leaves the workspace, so the sum can be larger than the eight-channel estimate. On this computer remote-devices.json shows the linked computer's last request at 2026-11-16T17:29Z, which suggests this path has not run since then and so does not explain freezes seen today (a stream held open since then would not update that time, so this is an indication, not proof).
- **Checker's measurements:** Node 24 with the repo's @xterm/headless 6.0.0 and addon-serialize 0.14.0, 8 mirrors with full 1,000-line scrollback, snapshot done exactly as ScreenMirror does, plus JSON.stringify of each replay. Plain text: 120x40 50 ms, 200x60 57 ms, 300x80 97 ms total. Heavily coloured text: 120x40 81 ms, 200x60 140 ms, 300x80 227 ms total. Longest event-loop gap equalled the total each time (48-225 ms), so nothing else ran in between. Per channel 2.6-22 ms serialize; payload 1-15 MB for eight. One 200x60 coloured channel: 21 ms.

#### Screen capture reads back and compares the whole desktop for every captured frame

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `remote-linked-phone-capture-full-readback`
- **Where:** `electron/screen-capture-preload.ts:57`
- **What the code does:** For each new frame the capture window draws every display, reads the whole picture with `getImageData` into a new array (57; up to about 18 MB for two displays, screen-layout.ts:33-34) and compares it pixel by pixel in 64-pixel tiles (59-61). Captures run at up to 30 a second (26) and the session asks again 15 ms after an unchanged frame (rfb-server.ts:21, 253).
- **What goes wrong:** While a phone or linked computer has this computer's Screen open, the screen service keeps a renderer busy reading back and comparing the full desktop many times a second, competing with Trace and the CLIs for CPU on the computer being viewed. Not measured: no viewer was connected and starting one was out of bounds.
- **Suggested fix:** Compare a downscaled copy first, reuse one buffer, and limit comparisons to about 10-15 a second while the picture keeps changing. Measure with a viewer connected before and after.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described: screen-capture-preload.ts:56-61 draws every display, takes a new full getImageData copy and compares it in 64-pixel tiles; layoutDisplays caps several displays at 1920*1200*2 pixels (screen-layout.ts:33-34), about 18 MB. Two points weaken it. First, it only happens when a new captured frame has arrived (line 54, `fresh`); the 15 ms polls after an unchanged frame (rfb-server.ts:21, 253) skip the read-back, so the finding's wording about polling overstates the idle cost. Second, this runs in the hidden window of the separate screen-service process (screen-service.ts:31, 48; screen-capture.ts:31), only while a viewer is connected, so it cannot block Trace's main process or window; the only effect is CPU competition. The part I could measure is small. Not established: the cost of drawImage from a VideoFrame into a software canvas, the JPEG encoding, and how many frames Chromium delivers for a still or changing desktop; no viewer was connected and starting one was out of bounds. Screen sharing is on here (screen-share.json enabled:true), so the path is reachable. Not a cause of the reported 1-3 s freeze.
- **Checker's measurements:** This computer has two 2560x1440 displays, so the picture would be 3840x1080 (16.6 MB). Node 24 stand-in for lines 57-61 at 3840x1200 (17.6 MB): buffer copy 3.4 ms, tile comparison of an unchanged picture (worst case, every pixel read) 4.7 ms; about 8 ms per frame, at most about 25% of one core at 30 frames a second. At 1920x1080: 1.3 ms + 2.5 ms. drawImage read-back and JPEG encoding not measured.

#### Phone app does not reconnect when it comes back to the front

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: ux. ID: `remote-linked-phone-no-reconnect-on-resume`
- **Where:** `phone/main.ts:673`
- **What the code does:** The only `visibilitychange` handler (673-677) releases or re-requests the channel's size; nothing restarts the stream, and there is no `online` or `pageshow` handler. `follow()` (phone/api.ts:164-170) notices a dead connection only when `reader.read()` fails or after 60 s of silence, a timer that does not run while the page is suspended.
- **What goes wrong:** The phone is locked for a few minutes on a channel, then unlocked. The header still says live and the screen is the old one for up to a minute, while the size request sent on return has already made the computer redraw the CLI at the phone's size. Keys typed meanwhile reach the CLI but their effect is not shown.
- **Suggested fix:** On becoming visible and on `online`, restart the stream when nothing has arrived for a few seconds (keep the time of the last byte), then ask for the size. Not tested on a phone: some browsers report the dead connection at once, which already recovers in 2 s.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described. phone/main.ts:673-677 is the only visibilitychange handler in phone/ (grep found no pageshow, online or focus handler) and it only releases or re-requests the size; nothing restarts the stream. follow() (phone/api.ts:164-171) leaves a connection only when reader.read() ends or rejects, or when the 60 s silence timer aborts it; the server pings every 25 s (remote-server.ts:272). So if the phone's browser keeps a dead connection open without reporting an error after a lock, the header stays 'live' (main.ts:129, 134) and the old screen stays until the timer fires, while the size request on return (675) can already have resized the CLI. What I could not establish is the trigger: whether iOS Safari or Android Chrome actually leave the stream silently dead after a lock, or reject the read at once (which recovers in 2 s, api.ts:189), and whether the suspended timer fires at once on resume. The auditor says the same. Phone-only, self-healing within a minute, input still delivered: low rather than medium until seen on a phone.

#### Start, Stop and Restart failures show nothing inside a channel

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `remote-linked-phone-control-errors-hidden`
- **Where:** `phone/main.ts:179`
- **What the code does:** `alertText()` (179) writes into `#phone-alert`, which only `showList()` adds (197). Start, Stop and Restart (`channelControls`, 144-154) are also placed in a channel's header (388, 421), where that element does not exist, so the message their `catch` passes on is dropped.
- **What goes wrong:** Inside a channel the person taps Restart while the computer refuses (the permission was just turned off, or the window answers 409). The button becomes available again and nothing is said, so it looks as if the tap did nothing.
- **Suggested fix:** Add the same live region to the channel and chat views, or show the message in the channel's existing `#send-error` line.
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed. alertText (phone/main.ts:179) writes only if #phone-alert exists; that element is created only in showList (197, appended after app.replaceChildren). showChannel replaces all of app's children (388 for chats, 421 for terminals) with a header that includes channelControls(channel), so inside a channel the element is gone and the message from press()'s catch (149) is dropped; the button is simply enabled again. The server does return messages worth showing: 403 RUN_REFUSED, 400, and 409 with the HubError text when host.control fails (remote-server.ts:868-876), for example a channel that cannot start. One narrowing: the 'permission just turned off' example mostly corrects itself, because the 'me' event redraws the channel without the buttons (main.ts:637); the launch or stop failures (409) are the real silent case. Phone-only, no data loss: low.

### Repository, workbench, storage, updates, coaching (14)

#### Backups will stop working when usage.db passes 16 MiB, about five weeks away at this computer's rate

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `services-misc-backup-usage-cap`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/backups.ts:99`
- **What the code does:** Line 99 throws BACKUP_SIZE 'One backup file exceeds 16 MiB.' for telemetry/usage.db; telemetry keeps 366 days (hub/telemetry.ts:115). usage.db here is 8.4 MB after 37 days (2026-10-11 to 2026-11-17). The real Backups class on a scratch copy made 10.7 MB without logs and 54.9 MB with logs, against the 64 MiB cap (line 92).
- **What goes wrong:** Around late December usage.db exceeds 16 MiB. Every Create backup then fails, with or without logs, with a message that names neither the file nor a remedy. With logs it fails sooner, once logs grow about 12 MB. The date assumes usage keeps growing at the same rate.
- **Suggested fix:** Remove the per-file cap for the databases (write entries as a stream instead of one JSON string), or back up a trimmed usage export. Name the file in the error. Add a test with a database over 16 MiB.
- **Checker's notes:** backups.ts:99 copies telemetry/usage.db with VACUUM INTO and throws BACKUP_SIZE 'One backup file exceeds 16 MiB.' when the copy is over 16,777,216 bytes, with or without logs; line 91 does the same for storage.db. The message names no file. Nothing shrinks usage.db: telemetry.ts:115 only deletes runs older than 366 days and line 207 adds a row per usage event. On a copy of this computer's usage.db (with its WAL) the VACUUM INTO result is 8.11 MB after 38 days of use, growing 0.21 MB a day on average and about 0.33 MB a day over the last three weeks, so the cap is reached in roughly 26-40 days (mid to late December) if use continues at this rate. That projection is an extrapolation, not a certainty. With logs the total today is about 55 MB against the 64 MiB cap at line 92/99 (logs 44.5 MB, usage 8.1 MB, memory 1.9 MB, coaching 0.6 MB, storage.db 0.2 MB). Note: this computer has no ~/.coding-hub/backups folder, so the person has never made a backup; the defect is real but latent for them.
- **Checker's measurements:** usage.db copy: 21,290 usage rows in 244 runs, 2026-10-11 to 2026-11-18 UTC; VACUUM INTO output 8,114,176 bytes (31 ms). Cap 16,777,216 bytes. Folder sizes: logs 44.5 MB (102 files), memory 1.93 MB (1,858 notes), coaching 0.64 MB.

#### A submodule's own Git filter still runs when Trace only reads a repository

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: security. ID: `services-misc-submodule-filter`
- **Where:** `electron/services/repository/git-safety.ts:14`
- **What the code does:** readOnlyGitOptions (lines 14-19) blanks only filter drivers in the opened repository's local config. git.ts:68 runs status with --ignore-submodules=none; delivery.ts:50, workbench/repository-state.ts:19 and worktrees.ts:80 also look into submodules. In a scratch repository (Git 2.55) a clean filter set in the submodule's config ran during Trace's exact status command; the same filter in the top repository did not.
- **What goes wrong:** The person opens a folder from a zip or shared drive holding an initialized submodule with filter.x.clean in .git/modules/<name>/config and a .gitattributes. Showing Changes, a task watch or a delivery review runs that program with the person's permissions, which git-safety.ts says it prevents.
- **Suggested fix:** Add -c diff.ignoreSubmodules=dirty to noHookGitOptions and use --ignore-submodules=dirty in git.ts:68. In the scratch test both stopped the submodule filter from running. Add a test with a submodule filter.
- **Checker's notes:** Reproduced on this computer (Git 2.55.0.windows.3) in a scratch repository. git-safety.ts:14-19 lists filter drivers only from the opened repository's local/worktree config, so a filter.x.clean set in .git/modules/sub/config is not blanked. With Trace's exact options (-c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.hooksPath=NUL) the submodule's clean filter ran for: the status at git.ts:68 (--ignore-submodules=none), the diff at git.ts:101, the status at worktrees.ts:80, plain status --porcelain (delivery.ts:50 form) and diff --stat HEAD. The same filter set in the top repository's config was listed as 'local' and did not run when blanked, so the gap is specific to submodules. It triggers when Git must re-hash a submodule file (changed stat data), which is the case for every file after extracting a zip. Fix check: --ignore-submodules=dirty on the status stopped it; -c diff.ignoreSubmodules=dirty stopped it for diff and for status without an explicit flag, but NOT while git.ts:68 still passes --ignore-submodules=none, so both changes are needed as the auditor says. Passing -c filter.x.clean= by name also reached the submodule. Likelihood is low for someone working on their own repositories (it needs a crafted folder with an initialized submodule), but the effect is running a program on merely viewing a folder, which this module exists to prevent; medium kept.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch repo with a submodule whose own config defines filter.x.clean writing a marker file: marker written under Trace's status command (git.ts:68 form) in 2 of 2 fresh runs, and under 4 other read commands; not written with --ignore-submodules=dirty, nor with -c diff.ignoreSubmodules=dirty when no explicit =none flag is passed.

#### Command menu search walks the project and memory synchronously on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `services-misc-search-sync`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/workflows.ts:80`
- **What the code does:** search() (lines 80-101) uses readdirSync, statSync and readFileSync for up to 3,000 entries and 8 MB, then every memory note. src/components/CommandMenu.tsx:74 calls it 300 ms after each typing pause (scope "all", 3+ characters). A read-only replica on this computer's project and memory took 536 ms cold and 212 ms warm; memory alone 101-160 ms.
- **What goes wrong:** The person opens the command menu and types a query with pauses. Each pause blocks the main process for 0.2-0.5 s: channel output, keystrokes to terminals, phone and linked-computer requests all wait.
- **Blocking time:** 0.2-0.5 s per search on the main process. Measured with a replica of the walk on this computer's data (536 ms cold, 212 ms warm).
- **Suggested fix:** Walk with fs.promises and yield between folders, or move the search to a worker thread. Keep memory notes in an in-memory index. Drop a search that a newer query replaced.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: workflows.ts:80-101 search() uses lstatSync/readdirSync/statSync/readFileSync with caps of 3,000 entries and 8 MB, and is reached with no guard from CommandMenu.tsx:74 (300 ms debounce, scope All needs 3+ characters, Files/Memory 2+; command-search.ts:25) through main.ts:694-728 on the main process. A read-only replica on this computer blocks about 0.17-0.2 s per search warm (I did not measure cold; the auditor's 536 ms cold is believable). It only runs while the person is typing in the command menu (or the Workflow search tab), so it cannot be the 'random' 1-3 s stall; severity lowered to low. Extra fact found while measuring: the saved workspace root here is ~\Claude\Projects, and in scope All the project walk uses up both caps (3,004 entries, 8.39 MB) before memory is reached, so scope All returns no memory hits at all on this computer (memoryMs 0, limited true). Only the '#' memory scope searches notes. Not covered by the finding: WorkflowCenter.tsx:33 with includeOutput reads up to 100 log tails of 1 MB each through readLog + stripVTControlCharacters (workflows.ts:100), which is heavier but only on an explicit click; not measured.
- **Checker's measurements:** Replica of search() on real data, warm: scope All with rootDirectory ~\Claude\Projects = 164-195 ms (5 queries; 3,004 entries visited, 366 files / 8.39 MB read, memory never reached). Memory-only scope = 92-98 ms (1,868 entries, 1,858 notes, 1.93 MB).

#### Each 200 ms of channel output makes a fsynced SQLite commit on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `services-misc-log-fsync`
- **Where:** `electron/services/storage/workspace-storage.ts:105`
- **What the code does:** Line 30 sets synchronous=FULL. flush() (85-106), run by a 200 ms timer per channel with output (line 80), does writeSync and then UPDATE runs SET recordedBytes (line 105), a commit that fsyncs the WAL. On a copy of this storage.db: 1.4 ms mean, 4.5 ms max per flush with FULL; 0.03 ms with NORMAL.
- **What goes wrong:** Eight channels streaming output make up to 40 fsyncs a second on the main thread (about 55 ms per second on an idle disk). When builds or Drive sync load the disk, one slow fsync blocks every channel. Not confirmed as the reported 1-3 s stall: I did not measure under disk load.
- **Blocking time:** Measured 1.1-1.4 ms typical and 4.5 ms max per flush on an idle disk, up to 5 flushes a second per channel, main process. Under disk load: not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Stop committing on every flush: write recordedBytes and logTruncated in endRun, history() and readLog (the constructor already re-reads log sizes for interrupted runs, line 40). Or set synchronous=NORMAL.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: workspace-storage.ts:30 sets synchronous=FULL; append() (75-83) arms a 200 ms timer per run with pending output (or flushes at 128 KB), and flush() (85-106) does writeSync then an auto-committed UPDATE at line 105, on the main process (called from main.ts:134 and :154). Flushing stops once a log hits the 5 MB cap (line 77). My measurement on a copy of this computer's storage.db matches the auditor's: about 1.3 ms per flush with FULL against 0.02 ms with NORMAL, worst case 11.9 ms in 3,000 flushes (the WAL checkpoint). That is 5 flushes a second per channel with output, so roughly 13 ms per second for two busy channels and about 53 ms per second for eight. This is steady overhead, not a stall: nothing above 12 ms was seen on an idle disk. The link to the reported 1-3 s freeze is unproven; I did not measure under disk load (kept the disk quiet for the running timing probe), so a slow fsync under load remains a hypothesis. The suggested fix (commit recordedBytes at endRun/history/readLog, or synchronous=NORMAL) is sound; the constructor already repairs recordedBytes for interrupted runs (line 40).
- **Checker's measurements:** Copy of storage.db (229 KB + 4.1 MB WAL), 3,000 flushes of 2 KB + UPDATE each: FULL mean 1.32-1.33 ms, p50 1.09 ms, p99 2.4-2.9 ms, max 11.9 ms, 1 of 3,000 over 5 ms, none over 20 ms. NORMAL mean 0.023 ms, p99 0.057 ms, max 7.6 ms.

#### Git and GitHub commands that time out are only half stopped, leaving partial work behind

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `services-misc-timeout-orphans`
- **Where:** `electron/services/repository/worktrees.ts:14`
- **What the code does:** execFile timeouts end only the direct child: worktrees.ts:14 (30 s; the record is saved only after `worktree add` returns, lines 57-58), project-setup.ts:15 (120 s for `gh repo clone`), workbench/delivery.ts:28 (120 s for commit, push, `gh pr create`). None uses stop-process.ts (AGENTS.md rule 8). Not reproduced; based on how Node ends a timed-out child on Windows.
- **What goes wrong:** A large repository takes over 30 s to check out: Trace reports a generic Git failure, yet a partial worktree folder and a coding-hub/<id> branch remain that Trace never lists or removes. A slow clone continues under gh's git child after "Setup could not finish"; a long commit hook keeps running after "failed or timed out".
- **Suggested fix:** Use longer limits with a Cancel button, and end the whole process tree on timeout. After a failed `worktree add`, remove the partial worktree and its branch, or save the record before running Git.
- **Checker's notes:** Mechanism reproduced on this computer. Node's execFile timeout ended only the direct git process: a git command whose work runs in a child (stand-in for a hook, gh's git clone, or a checkout) reported killed/SIGTERM at 1.5 s, and the child finished its work 6 s after start. For worktrees.ts:14 specifically, a `git worktree add -b coding-hub/<id>` on a 4,000-file scratch repository that was timed out at 250 ms and at 600 ms still ended with all 4,000 files checked out, the worktree registered in `git worktree list` and the branch created. Trace saves the record only after the command succeeds (worktrees.ts:57-58), so that worktree and branch would never appear in Trace, and pruneBranches (line 24) keeps the branch because a worktree uses it. One correction: in my test the leftover worktree was complete, not partial. project-setup.ts:15 (120 s, gh repo clone) and delivery.ts:28/91 (120 s) use the same execFile timeout; project-setup.ts:44 does tell the person the folder was kept, and after a timed-out clone the `config --unset core.hooksPath` at line 42 never runs, so hooks stay off in that clone. Severity lowered: it needs a checkout over 30 s or a clone/commit/push over 120 s, nothing is lost, and this computer has no ~/.coding-hub/worktrees folder (the feature has not been used here). The real 30 s / 120 s paths were not run.
- **Checker's measurements:** execFile('git', alias running a 6 s child, timeout 1500): callback at 1,514 ms with killed=true; child's marker file absent then, present 8 s later. `git worktree add` on 4,000 files: untimed 1,363 ms; timed out at 250 ms (566 files present) and 600 ms (1,568 files present), both had 4,000 files, a listed worktree and the branch 5 s later.

#### Restoring a backup silently drops phone pairings, linked computers and this computer's identity

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: data-loss. ID: `services-misc-restore-drops-links`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/backups.ts:15`
- **What the code does:** The backup list (lines 15-16) omits machine.json, device-sync.json, remote.json, remote-devices.json, remote-push.json, linked-computers.json and screen-sharing.json, all kept in the active storage folder (hub/device-sync.ts:91-96, remote/remote-server.ts:219-263). After a restore the active folder is restores/<uuid> (line 120). The confirmation (main.ts:559) mentions only credentials. Read from code; I did not run a restore and restart.
- **What goes wrong:** The person restores a backup and restarts. Phone access is off, each phone and linked computer must be paired again, push alerts stop, and device-sync.ts:94 writes a new machine identity, so synced computers see a new computer. Nothing says so, and the window offers no way back to the previous data.
- **Suggested fix:** Copy these files from the current storage into the restored copy, say in the dialog what is not restored, and offer a way to return to the previous data.
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed by reading; I did not run a restore. backups.ts:15-16 allows only the listed files plus memory/, designs/, coaching/ and optionally logs/. A restore writes only those entries into restores/<uuid> (lines 111-120), and resolveStorageRoot (line 18, main.ts:71) makes that folder storageRoot after restart. Every service below is built on storageRoot: RemoteServer (main.ts:566; remote.json defaults to enabled:false at remote-server.ts:257, remote-devices.json at :254, remote-push.json at :219), LinkedComputers (main.ts:627), ScreenSharing (main.ts:638), and DeviceSync, which writes a new machine.json when none exists (device-sync.ts:91-94). This computer has all of those files in ~/.coding-hub today. The dialog at main.ts:559 and the panel text (RuntimeBackupPanel.tsx:16) mention only credentials and named accounts, and no code other than backups.ts touches storage-location.json, so there is no in-app way back. Corrections to severity and category: nothing is deleted (the old folder stays intact, as the dialog says), and the new identity is a known, handled case (device-sync.ts:205-207 removes duplicate runs for that reason). So this is a silent loss of pairings and a missing warning and return path, not data loss; it follows an explicit, rare action, and this computer has no backups or restores folder.

#### Mac script update quits Trace later without re-checking, and assumes /Applications/Trace.app

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `services-misc-mac-script-quit`
- **Where:** `electron/services/updates/mac-script-update.ts:71`
- **What the code does:** main.ts:301 checks only manager.hasSessions, once, before the script starts. The script downloads and stages first, then line 71 runs `quit app "Trace"` unconditionally. Line 59 fixes APP=/Applications/Trace.app; on a failed move line 74 runs `open -a "$APP"` and exits. Read only: there is no Mac here to run it.
- **What goes wrong:** A channel started during the download is stopped mid-task when the script quits Trace; verification, project checks and sign-ins are never checked (rule 9). If that Mac runs Trace from another folder, Trace quits, the move fails, `open` finds nothing, and Trace stays closed with nobody there.
- **Suggested fix:** Have the script ask Trace on its loopback port whether it is idle just before quitting, using the full hasSessions list. Pass the running app's own path into the script and reopen that path on failure.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described; I could not run it (no Mac). main.ts:301 checks only manager.hasSessions, once, before startMacUpdate; unlike the self-updating path (main.ts:754 and 761) it does not check accounts, project setup, project checks, verification, backups, delivery, workflows or worktrees, and it does not set updateRestart, so new channels can start while the script downloads and stages. mac-script-update.ts:71 then runs `quit app "Trace"` unconditionally; before-quit (main.ts:869-890) stops running channels with manager.dispose() and cancels verification and project checks. Partial guard that exists: before-quit refuses while delivery, workflows or a worktree operation is running (line 872), and the script gives up after 90 s leaving Trace as it was (line 73). Line 59 fixes APP=/Applications/Trace.app: if Trace runs from elsewhere, the quit at line 71 still closes it, `mv "$APP"` fails at line 74 and `open -a "$APP"` has nothing to open, so Trace stays closed. Not established: whether the person's Mac still uses this script path (it applies only to copies not signed with the Developer ID), where Trace is installed there, and how likely a channel start is during the download window. Both triggers are narrow, so low.

#### Command menu "all" search never reaches memory notes when the project uses up the search budget

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `services-misc-search-skips-memory`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/workflows.ts:88`
- **What the code does:** The project scan and the memory scan share `visited` (3,000) and `bytes` (8 MB) (lines 81, 88, 91, 96-98). In the replica run on this computer's project the project scan ended at visited 3,004 and 8.00 MB after 365 files, so the memory scan returned at once. CommandMenu.tsx:74 ignores `limited`.
- **What goes wrong:** The person searches the command menu in its default scope for words from a handoff note. No memory hit appears and nothing says the search was cut short. Only the first 365 project files were read as well.
- **Suggested fix:** Give memory its own budget and search it first (it is small). Show in the menu when a search was limited.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: one `visited` (3,000) and one `bytes` (8 MiB) counter are shared by the project scan (line 97) and the memory scan (line 98); scan() returns at once when either is used up (line 88). CommandMenu.tsx:74 passes rootDirectory for every scope but 'memory' and uses only result.hits (WorkflowCenter does show `limited`). I ran the real Workflows class (bundled to scratch, read-only) against this computer's data. The saved workspace root is ~\Claude\Projects (from a copy of storage.db), and with that root the project scan ends at visited 3,004 and 8,388,588 bytes after 364 files; the memory scan reads 0 of 1,858 notes. Query "handoff": 98 memory hits in memory scope, 0 memory hits (13 file hits) in the default scope. With the root set to the Coding-Hub folder alone, memory is still reached (443 entries visited), so the fault depends on project size; it holds for this person's actual root. Severity stays low: the memory scope is a working way round (the comment at CommandMenu.tsx:67-68 shows the author knew), but the default scope gives no sign it was cut short. Side note: the search is fully synchronous on the main process, about 0.17 s per search here.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real class on live data (read-only). Root ~\Claude\Projects: project scan stops at visited 3,004, 8,388,588 bytes, 364 files read, 0 of 1,858 memory notes read. "handoff": memory scope 98 memory hits; default scope 0 memory hits, limited=true. "Tailscale": 30 vs 0. "DriveMirror": 3 vs 0. Main-process block per search: 161-180 ms (Projects root), 116-136 ms (Coding-Hub root), 89-113 ms (memory only), warm cache.

#### Task check freshness re-hashes every changed file synchronously, twice every 30 s on the Tasks screen

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `services-misc-freshness-sync`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/repository-state.ts:45`
- **What the code does:** repositoryState (lines 40-49) reads and hashes every changed or untracked file with readFileSync, up to 5,000 files and 256 MB. Verification.freshness (verification.ts:50-56) calls it per result folder. src/components/TasksPanel.tsx:59 requests it whenever `watch` or `tasks` change, and src/state/task-store.ts:230 replaces both every 30 s.
- **What goes wrong:** A task has a check attached and the Tasks screen is open in a project with large uncommitted or untracked files. The main process stalls for the read-and-hash time twice every 30 s. Not active on this computer today: it has no tasks.json or verification.json.
- **Blocking time:** Estimated, not measured: grows with changed bytes, up to 256 MB per folder (roughly 0.5-2 s), twice per 30 s, main process.
- **Suggested fix:** Hash off the main thread (streams or a Git child process), reuse a result while the size-and-time marks from changedFiles are unchanged, and ask for freshness only when the watch result really changed.
- **Checker's notes:** Code path holds. repositoryState (lines 43-46) stats, reads (readFileSync) and hashes every changed or untracked file in a synchronous loop after the async git status, up to 5,000 files and 256 MB. Verification.freshness (verification.ts:50-56) calls it once per distinct result folder, for every stored result with `tested`. It runs in the main process (main.ts:733). TasksPanel.tsx:59 re-requests it when `watch` or `tasks` change and any task has a verificationId; task-store.ts:230 runs refreshTaskWatch and loadTasks every 30 s, and each sets a new object (lines 27, 38-39), so the effect fires twice. TasksPanel is mounted only while the Tasks screen is open (FeaturePanel.tsx:33). The auditor's 0.5-2 s estimate is too high for a warm cache: I measured about 0.37 s per call near the limits, so about two 0.4 s stalls per 30 s in the worst case, and milliseconds for an ordinary working tree. Cold cache or a slow disk would be slower; not measured. Not active on this computer: ~/.coding-hub has no tasks.json or verification.json, so it is not the cause of the reported freeze.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real repositoryState on a scratch Git repository with 2,510 untracked files (213 MB in 510 files plus 2,000 small files): total 480-508 ms per call, of which the longest event-loop block was 364-385 ms (three runs, warm cache). Plain readFileSync+sha256 on 42.4 MB of logs: 39 ms (about 1,100 MB/s). ~/.coding-hub has no tasks.json or verification.json.

#### Git delivery hides why a commit, push or pull request failed

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `services-misc-delivery-error-hidden`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/delivery.ts:29`
- **What the code does:** command() (lines 27-30) catches every failure and throws one fixed DELIVERY_FAILED sentence; the failed process's stderr and stdout are discarded. The same path runs `git commit` with hooks, `git push` and `gh pr create` (lines 79-91).
- **What goes wrong:** A pre-commit hook rejects the commit, a push is refused as non-fast-forward, or a pull request already exists. The person sees only "The command failed or timed out. Check local Git/GitHub authentication, hooks, branch protection, and remote access" and must repeat it in a terminal to learn why.
- **Suggested fix:** Put the last 2 KB or so of stderr in the HubError message, with the URL-credential redaction already used at line 91.
- **Checker's notes:** Line 29 catches every execFile failure and throws a HubError with one of two fixed sentences (ENOENT, or the generic "The command failed or timed out…"). The rejected promisified execFile error carries stdout and stderr, and both are dropped. The same command() runs the confirmed `git commit` (hooks run), `git push` and `gh pr create` at line 91, with nothing else surfacing their output on failure; only successful output is returned (lines 91-94). So a hook rejection, a non-fast-forward push or "a pull request already exists" all show the same generic sentence. No guard elsewhere. It is a usability gap, not data loss, and nothing is executed wrongly; low is right. Any fix must keep the credential redaction used at line 91.

#### File browser's .git block is bypassed by a Windows short name or stream syntax

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: security. ID: `services-misc-git-shortname`
- **Where:** `electron/services/repository/filesystem.ts:15`
- **What the code does:** validateRelativePath (lines 12-17) compares segments to '.git' only, and scopedPath (lines 21-33) does not re-check the resolved path. Against a scratch folder, RepositoryFiles.read returned .git/config for 'GIT~1/config' and for '.git::$INDEX_ALLOCATION/config', and list('GIT~1') listed the folder.
- **What goes wrong:** A compromised or buggy renderer (rule 1 treats it as untrusted) asks for GIT~1/config in an opened project and reads Git metadata the browser promises to hide, including credentials embedded in remote URLs.
- **Suggested fix:** After realpath, reject the request when any segment of path.relative(root, resolved) equals '.git' in any case. On Windows also reject segments containing ':'.
- **Checker's notes:** Reproduced with the real RepositoryFiles class on a scratch folder holding .git/config. validateRelativePath (line 15) only compares each segment to '.git' (case-insensitive), and scopedPath (lines 30-33) checks containment after realpath but not for a .git segment. repositoryPathSchema does not restrict the characters. '.git/config', '.GIT/config' and 'src/../.git/config' are blocked; 'GIT~1/config', '.git::$INDEX_ALLOCATION/config' and '.git:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION/config' return the file, and list('GIT~1') lists the folder. open() is not affected (line 87 checks after realpath). Severity stays low, arguably lower: the only callers are the renderer IPC handlers (main.ts:775-776) and Git diff preview; the phone API does not use them. A renderer able to send these requests can already start a terminal channel and read the same file, so this is a defence-in-depth gap rather than a new exposure. It also needs 8.3 short names enabled on the volume (they were on this C: scratch folder) or NTFS stream syntax.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real class, scratch project: BLOCK .git/config, .GIT/config, src/../.git/config. READ GIT~1/config, .git::$INDEX_ALLOCATION/config, .git:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION/config (returned the remote URL line with the fake embedded token). LIST GIT~1 and .git::$INDEX_ALLOCATION returned 'config'. Also read src/a.txt::$DATA.

#### Creating and restoring a backup runs entirely on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `services-misc-backup-sync`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/backups.ts:82`
- **What the code does:** backup-create (lines 77-102) runs VACUUM INTO, reads, hashes and base64s every file, then JSON.stringify, gzipSync and a full re-read to verify (manifest, 41-47), all synchronous. Restore (107-118) gunzips, writes and re-hashes before its dialog. The real class on a scratch copy of this storage: create 0.54 s (1.56 s with logs), restore preparation 2.2-2.4 s.
- **What goes wrong:** The person clicks Create backup or Restore. The main process answers nothing for 0.5-2.4 s, more as usage.db and logs grow, so phone and linked-computer requests and window requests stall. No channel can be running then (readyForBackup).
- **Blocking time:** Measured on a copy of this computer's data: 0.54 s create, 1.56 s create with logs, 2.2-2.4 s restore preparation, main process.
- **Suggested fix:** Do the work in a worker thread, or with async file reads and a zlib stream.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: after `await callbacks.ready()`, backup-create (lines 78-101) runs readdirSync, VACUUM INTO, readFileSync, sha256, base64, JSON.stringify, gzipSync, writeFileSync and then manifest() (gunzipSync, JSON.parse, zod, re-hash) with no yield. Restore and import (lines 107-118) do manifest(), write every file, re-read and re-hash, and rebase the databases before the confirmation dialog. Export also runs manifest() first. I ran the real class on a scratch copy of this computer's data with an event-loop probe: the whole create is one block, and the restore preparation is one block. Numbers are a little lower than the auditor's but the same order. Low is right: it happens only when the person clicks the button (which is then disabled), no channel can be running (readyForBackup, main.ts:555), and it is not the random freeze. Phone and linked-computer requests do stall for that time. It grows with usage.db, memory and logs.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real Backups class on a copy (storage.db+WAL, usage.db+WAL, 1,858 memory notes, coaching, 102 logs). Create without logs: 403 and 418 ms, event loop blocked the whole time; 1,869 files, 10.75 MB, 3.46 MB gzip. Create with logs: 1,316 ms blocked; 1,971 files, 55.3 MB, 11.1 MB gzip. Restore up to the confirmation dialog: 1,432-1,530 ms (no logs), 1,743 ms (with logs); declined-restore cleanup adds about 0.12 s.

#### Backups cannot be deleted in Trace, and the 50-backup limit is a dead end

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `services-misc-backup-no-delete`
- **Where:** `electron/services/workbench/backups.ts:78`
- **What the code does:** The request union (line 75) has list, create, export, restore and import only. Line 78 refuses a 51st backup with "Export and manage older backups before adding more". src/components/RuntimeBackupPanel.tsx:10 offers the same actions. Each confirmed restore also leaves a full copy under restores/ (line 111) that nothing removes.
- **What goes wrong:** After 50 backups Create backup always fails, and the only way on is deleting files and editing backups/index.json by hand. Backups of this storage are 3.5-11 MB each, and old restore copies pile up.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a backup-delete action (file and index entry) with a button, and remove restore copies that are neither active nor the previous one.
- **Checker's notes:** The request union at line 75 has only backup-list, -create, -export, -restore and -import; a search of electron/, src/, shared/ and phone/ finds no backup-delete and no other code that removes a .hub-backup.gz or an index.json entry. Line 78 refuses a 51st backup and tells the person to "export and manage older backups", but export only copies the file (line 109). RuntimeBackupPanel.tsx offers the same five actions. The 'restores' folder is referenced only at backups.ts:18 and :111; a declined or failed restore is removed (lines 119, 121), a confirmed one stays for good, including earlier ones that are no longer active. Deleting a backup file by hand is not enough: the count comes from index.json (line 40). Impact is real but distant on this computer: ~/.coding-hub has no backups or restores folder today, so 50 manual backups are a long way off. Low.
- **Checker's measurements:** Backup size on a copy of this data: 3.46 MB without logs, 11.1 MB with logs (matches the claim). Live ~/.coding-hub: no backups/ and no restores/ folder.

#### A run's saved output keeps the first 5 MiB and drops everything after

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: improvement. ID: `services-misc-log-head-only`
- **Where:** `electron/services/storage/workspace-storage.ts:98`
- **What the code does:** flush() (lines 94-99) sets log.truncated at maxLogBytes, and append() (line 77) then discards all later output. readLog returns the file's last 1 MiB (133-140), and workflows.ts:45 shows its last 2,000 characters as the failure diagnostic. Five logs on this computer are at the 5 MiB cap.
- **What goes wrong:** A long session fails after its log reached 5 MiB. Session history, Recent failures and output search show megabytes 4-5 of the session, not what happened before the exit, so the saved diagnostic can be hours old.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the start and the end, for example the first 1 MiB plus a rolling last 4 MiB, so the end of a run is always saved.
- **Checker's notes:** flush() cuts the buffer at maxLogBytes (5 MiB, line 23) and sets log.truncated (lines 94-99); append() returns at once for a truncated log (line 77), so everything later is dropped. readLog returns the last 1 MiB of the file (lines 133-140) and workflows.ts:45 takes its last 2,000 characters as the failure diagnostic. So for a capped run the 'end' shown is megabyte 4-5 of the recording, not the end of the run. Real data confirms the effect: five logs are exactly 5,242,880 bytes; the four finished ones all have exit code 1 and stopped recording 2 to 34 hours before the run ended. One softening point: SessionHistory.tsx:46 does tell the person "Recording reached the per-run limit; later terminal output was not saved", so it is disclosed there; the Recent failures diagnostic is still stale. It is a design limit rather than a malfunction, and nothing else depends on the log. Low, as an improvement.
- **Checker's measurements:** Copy of storage.db plus live log file times: run 52bed21a lasted 27.3 h, log full after 5.3 h (22.0 h unrecorded); 3a084d81 37.6 h, full after 22.6 h (15.0 h unrecorded); 2e3133a3 36.2 h, full after 2.0 h (34.3 h unrecorded); 52a5461b 4.2 h, full after 2.1 h (2.1 h unrecorded); 96850d3f still running, already full. All four finished ones: exit code 1, logTruncated=1, 5,242,880 bytes.

### Window state and runtime (14)

#### Every streamed chat token re-renders and re-parses the whole thread, so a long chat or Round Table stalls the window while an agent replies

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: freeze. ID: `renderer-logic-chat-delta-rerender`
- **Where:** `src/state/chat-store.ts:67`
- **What the code does:** Each `delta` replaces the thread (copy of all items, findIndex from the start). ChatView.tsx:136-139 then renders every Item again: ChatParts.tsx:87 Item is not memoized and gets a new `answer` closure, so each message's Markdown is parsed again. The host sends one event per token chunk (claude-stream.ts:115). Threads are never trimmed and survive resumes (chat-store.ts:28-29).
- **What goes wrong:** A hierarchy table has run for hours (three merged threads). An agent streams a reply at tens of deltas a second; each delta costs more than the gap between them, so clicks and typing in every channel wait until the reply ends, then the window comes back. 74 of the last 102 recorded runs here were chat runs.
- **Blocking time:** Renderer process. Measured lower bound (server render of Markdown.tsx alone, Node 24): 29 ms per delta at 200 messages (213 KB), 82 ms at 600, 203 ms at 1500. With tens of deltas per second this blocks for the length of each streamed reply, estimated 1-3 s. Not measured in the live app.
- **Suggested fix:** Memoize Item (stable answer/view callbacks, per-item props) so only the streaming message renders; batch deltas per animation frame in applyChatEvent; look up the assistant item from the end; cap or virtualize long threads. Add a render-count test for a 500-item thread.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as cited: chat-store.ts:67 replaces the thread on every delta, ChatView.tsx:136-139 renders every Item again, Item (ChatParts.tsx:87) and Markdown (Markdown.tsx:8) are not memoized (no React.memo anywhere in src/), and neither claude-stream.ts:115 nor main.ts:152-157 batches deltas. Cost is linear in thread length. But the auditor's estimate (29 ms per delta at 200 messages) is about ten times too high for real threads: it assumed every message is 1 KB of Markdown, while real 200-message histories here hold 29-98 KB of text and mostly tool cards. I ran the real App, stores and ChatView in headless Chrome with real histories from this computer: a 200-item thread costs 2.5 ms per token, so typical chats are unaffected. The page only falls behind at about 2,500-3,000 items. That size is reachable here: one chat run last week lasted 31.6 h with 1,900 tool calls in one thread, which is roughly 80-100% of the renderer while a reply streams. The effect is sluggishness (5-10 frames a second, the reply drawn late) in every channel for the length of each reply in a very long session, not a random 1-3 s stop, so it does not by itself explain the reported symptom. Severity medium, not high. Also note finding renderer-logic-noop-session-churn re-renders the same threads on each session event.
- **Checker's measurements:** Headless Chrome, production React, real App + real chat histories (chatHistory output of local transcripts). Per streamed token (store update + render + forced layout): 2.5 ms at 201 items (p95 4.9), 7.4 ms at 569, 26 ms at 1,555 (p95 33), 38 ms at 3,109 (p95 53). 90 tokens at 30/s (ideal 2,937 ms): 1,555 items took 3,079 ms with 2,307 ms of script, worst frame gap 179 ms; 3,109 items took 4,977 ms with 4,243 ms of script, 33 frames over 100 ms, worst gap 302 ms. Real data: 74 of 102 recorded runs are chat runs; tool calls per chat run median 0, p90 150, max 1,900 (31.6 h run), 3 runs over 500; chat run length median 0.13 h, p90 12.2 h.

#### Another computer's chat threads are deleted whenever this computer's sessions array changes

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `renderer-logic-linked-chat-wiped`
- **Where:** `src/runtime/terminals.ts:261`
- **What the code does:** releaseRemovedTerminals calls forgetChatsExcept(local session ids) on every `sessions` change (use-native-host.ts:135). Linked computers' threads live in the same store under `computerId:sessionId` keys (RemoteChat.tsx:30, use-native-host.ts:95), which are never in that set, so chat-store.ts:34-38 removes them. Their history is replayed only when the view mounts or the run changes (LinkedComputerView.tsx:43).
- **What goes wrong:** The person watches a linked computer's chat channel while a local channel works. The next local event (any status change, or the no-op changes on each tool call and usage update) empties the linked thread; the following remote event starts a blank thread, so the conversation shows only what arrived since, until the tab is left and opened again.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep linked threads out of the purge: only drop keys that are local session ids no longer present (for example keys without a computer prefix), and drop linked keys when their computer or channel leaves useLinkedStore. Add a test with one local and one linked thread.
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed by reading and by running it. use-native-host.ts:135 calls releaseRemovedTerminals with the local session ids on every change of the sessions array; terminals.ts:261 passes them to forgetChatsExcept, and chat-store.ts:34-38 removes every thread whose key is not one of them. Linked computers' threads are stored under `computerId:sessionId` (RemoteChat.tsx:30, use-native-host.ts:95), so they are always removed. Their history is requested only on mount or when the remote run changes (LinkedComputerView.tsx:43), and receiveChat (RemoteChat.tsx:19) starts an empty thread for the next event. Because applyEvent replaces the sessions array even for events that change nothing (workspace-store.ts:199), any local Claude tool call or usage update triggers it. Reachable here: this computer has one linked computer and local agents run most of the time. No guard found. It loses only this window's view of the other computer's conversation (the conversation itself is intact there, and leaving and reopening the tab reloads it), so medium is right. It only affects linked chat channels, not linked terminals.
- **Checker's measurements:** In headless Chrome with the real App mounted: a linked thread seeded with 4 items through receiveReplay still had 4 items after 200 ms idle; after one local `answered` event that changed nothing the thread was deleted; the next remote delta produced a new thread with 1 item. ~/.coding-hub/linked-computers.json lists 1 linked computer.

#### Closing a Round Table's channel with the tile's X (or from a phone) discards its review findings without saving them

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: data-loss. ID: `renderer-logic-close-drops-findings`
- **Where:** `src/runtime/use-native-host.ts:193`
- **What the code does:** closeChannel stops the lead and its seats, then calls removeSession; remoteControl 'close' does the same at line 252. Neither calls archiveFindings. A stopped lead makes the host close the table without `closed` (chat/round-table.ts:209-211), so forgetTable never runs. removeSession (workspace-store.ts:230-245) drops the session with its roundTable.findings. Only ChatView.tsx:107 saves findings before closing.
- **What goes wrong:** A table has unverified findings. The person presses Close on the tile (SessionTile.tsx:96) instead of the table's own X. The channel and its hidden seats go, and the review record and open items are never written to shared memory, contrary to keep-findings.test.ts ('findings outlive a closed Round Table').
- **Suggested fix:** In closeChannel and the remote 'close' branch, call archiveFindings(id) before removeSession and stop with its message when it fails, as ChatView.closeTable does. Also clear another lead's record only after its findings are archived (workspace-store.ts:237).
- **Checker's notes:** Confirmed by reading and by running it. closeChannel (use-native-host.ts:188-195) stops the lead and its seats and calls removeSession at line 193; the remote `close` branch does the same at line 252. Neither calls archiveFindings. removeSession (workspace-store.ts:230-245) drops the lead together with its roundTable.findings, and savedWorkspace no longer contains it. A stopped lead makes the host close the table without `closed` (electron/services/chat/round-table.ts:209-211, close(..., false) at :201-207), so forgetTable (use-native-host.ts:76) never runs. Only ChatView.tsx:107 and round-table.ts retire() archive first. The host has no saving of its own: the only finding writes are in src/runtime/round-table.ts:77-80. The app even tells the person to close the table to save a full record (review-chain.ts:479, round-table.ts:306), and SessionTile.tsx:93-97 is the ordinary way to close a channel. The agents' messages remain in the CLI transcripts and run log, but the review record and open items never reach shared memory. Medium stands.
- **Checker's measurements:** In headless Chrome with the real code and a recording stand-in for the host: closeChannel on a table lead with 3 findings (1 open, 1 fixed, 1 verified) returned true, removed the channel and made 0 hub calls; archiveFindings on an identical lead made memory-key plus 3 memory-write calls (the record and 2 open items).

#### No-op session events replace the sessions array, re-rendering the whole window on every Claude tool call and usage update

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: performance. ID: `renderer-logic-noop-session-churn`
- **Where:** `src/state/workspace-store.ts:199`
- **What the code does:** applyEvent returns a new `sessions` array for every non-data event, changed or not. The host sends `answered` on every Claude PostToolUse hook (attention.ts:114, pty-manager.ts:129). use-native-host.ts:63 also maps `sessions` on every usage update (telemetry polls each run every 750 ms). App.tsx:34 and SessionGrid, SessionTile, StatusBar, Topbar, Toasts subscribe to the whole store; no component uses React.memo.
- **What goes wrong:** Two to eight Claude channels work at once, each making several tool calls a second. Every call re-renders every tile and the shell, runs savedWorkspace plus JSON.stringify (use-native-host.ts:115-118), restoreTables and releaseRemovedTerminals. Typing and scrolling get jerky in proportion to agent activity. Render cost per pass was not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Return the same state from applyEvent when no session object changed; in the onUsage handler call setState only when a resumeId really differs; select fields with useShallow in App and the shell components; skip the save subscriber when sessions, tiles, layout, root and focus are the same references.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described: workspace-store.ts:199 returns a new sessions array for every non-data event, changed or not; `answered` is sent on each Claude PostToolUse hook (attention.ts:114, pty-manager.ts:129) and on chat permission-done (pty-manager.ts:283); use-native-host.ts:63 maps sessions on every usage update that carries a conversation id; App.tsx:34, SessionGrid, SessionTile, StatusBar, Topbar and Toasts subscribe to the whole store, and nothing is memoized, so each such event renders the whole window including every chat thread, and runs savedWorkspace + JSON.stringify (use-native-host.ts:115-118), restoreTables and releaseRemovedTerminals. What does not hold is the impact: the scenario assumes several tool calls a second per channel, and telemetry publishes usage only when it changed (telemetry.ts:180), not every 750 ms. On this computer's transcripts tool calls run at a median of 4 per active minute for all conversations together (max 34 a minute), and one no-op event costs 10-29 ms, so this is well under 2% of the renderer and not visible jerkiness. Worth fixing because it is cheap, it multiplies the chat re-render cost, and it is what triggers the linked-thread purge (renderer-logic-linked-chat-wiped), but low on its own.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real App in headless Chrome: one no-op `answered` event costs 2.9 ms (1 chat x 200 items + 1 terminal), 10.2 ms (4 chats x 200 + 4 terminals), 28.9 ms (4 chats x 569 + 4 terminals, p95 44), 24.4 ms (1 chat x 1,555); a setState that keeps the same state object costs 0 ms. Real event rate from 68 conversations of the last 14 days (13,862 tool calls): median 4 tool calls per active minute across all conversations, p90 9, p99 17, max 34; busiest 10 s of any conversation: 10 calls.

#### Long tasks in terminal channels never reach Ready for review: the transcript read keeps only the last 200 messages of the last 8 MB

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `renderer-logic-transcript-window`
- **Where:** `src/state/task-store.ts:87`
- **What the code does:** lastReply and taskTurnDone read `channel-transcript`, which returns `messages.slice(-200)` from the final 8 MB of the CLI log (electron/services/chat/transcript.ts:5-7, 75). Every tool call is its own assistant message. replyToTask returns null when the task's prompt is not among them (shared/task-signal.ts:28-29), so taskSignal sees an empty reply.
- **What goes wrong:** A task sent to a Claude Code or Codex terminal takes more than about 200 tool calls, or reads more than 8 MB of files. The agent ends with TASK COMPLETE, but the prompt has left the window: the task is marked unconfirmed or quiet and stays in Working. Short tasks move on; exactly the long ones do not.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the host a task-reply read that scans the transcript backwards for the send mark (`Task <sendId>:`) and returns only the last assistant text after it, without the 200-message cap or tool results. Add a test with more than 200 messages after the prompt.
- **Checker's notes:** The code is as described. task-store.ts:75-87 and :125 read `channel-transcript` for terminal channels; main.ts:330-335 answers it with chatHistory, which reads only the last 8 MB of the CLI transcript (transcript.ts:5, 15-22) and returns messages.slice(-200) (transcript.ts:75). When the task's prompt is outside that window replyToTask returns null (shared/task-signal.ts:28-29), taskSignal('') is null, and the task is set to unconfirmed (task-store.ts:135) or quiet (task-store.ts:223) and stays in Working. One correction: for Claude an assistant message is one API response (consecutive lines with the same message id are merged, transcript.ts:54-55), so parallel tool calls count once; for Codex each call is its own message (transcript.ts:69). Chat channels are not affected (they read the window's own thread). On this computer's real transcripts the window is exceeded rarely, and more often by the 8 MB limit than by the 200 messages. The result is a wrong label and a task the person moves by hand, with nothing lost, so I rate it low rather than medium; it does hit exactly the longest tasks.
- **Checker's measurements:** 100 local Claude transcripts, 1,603 person-prompt turns: assistant messages per turn median 4, p90 23, p99 98, max 378; 2 turns exceed 200 messages and 7 turns exceed 8 MB of transcript between the prompt and the next prompt (largest 34 MB), so about 0.5% of turns; none of the turns over 200 messages are in the last 14 days. 19 of 100 transcripts are over 8 MB (largest 138 MB). chatHistory on the 138 MB file returned 200 messages in 25 ms.

#### Closing a table writes one memory note per open finding in a row, each running a Drive sync; a failure part way duplicates notes on retry

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `renderer-logic-keepfindings-writes`
- **Where:** `src/runtime/round-table.ts:77`
- **What the code does:** keepFindings awaits one `memory-write` for the record and one per unverified finding (lines 77-80; up to 50 findings by schema). The host runs `this.drive.sync(project)` inside every memory-write (electron/services/hub/service.ts:92). If a later write throws, archiveFindings (line 91) keeps all findings and the next attempt writes every note again; notes are immutable.
- **What goes wrong:** The person closes a table with ten unverified findings: eleven Drive syncs run back to back on the main process and the window stops answering for seconds. If Drive fails on the sixth write, Needs you retries and the record plus the first five open items are stored twice.
- **Blocking time:** Main process. About 0.35 s per note on this computer (the Drive sync cost per write already measured for 1.21.3), so roughly 2 s for 5 open findings and up to 18 s at the 50-finding cap. Estimated from that figure; not measured here.
- **Suggested fix:** Add one host action that writes the record and its open items together and syncs once. Remember which finding ids were already written (or mark them on the saved record) so a retry writes only the rest.
- **Checker's notes:** The repeated sync is as described: keepFindings (src/runtime/round-table.ts:77-80) awaits one memory-write for the record and one per unverified finding, and each memory-write runs DriveMirror.sync synchronously on the main process (service.ts:92), which lists and stats every note on both sides (drive.ts:80, memory.ts:35-42) before uploading the new one. This is the per-write sync already known from 1.21.3, multiplied by 1 + open findings. The cost depends on the project: 0.41 s per write in this computer's largest project, under 60 ms in most. The duplicate-on-retry part is wrong as written: a Drive failure cannot fail the write, because service.ts:92 catches it. A partial failure needs the local write or the IPC call itself to fail (disk error, MEMORY_LIMIT at memory.ts:69); then archiveFindings (round-table.ts:89-93) does keep all findings and a retry writes everything again. keep-findings.test.ts simulates 'Drive folder is offline' as a thrown write, which the real host does not do. It happens once, on a deliberate close of a table with open findings, so low. A table's record content could also exceed the 60,000-character limit (service.ts:33) only near 50 long findings; not established.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only timing of what one write's sync reads (listings and file sizes on both sides, archive listings, the Drive folder listing for import), median of 5, per memory project: 409 ms for the 785-note project (383 ms of it on G:\), 165 ms (336 notes), 143 ms (304), 127 ms (250), 58 ms (105), 8-21 ms for the five projects with 7-31 notes. So closing a table with 5 open findings blocks the main process for about 2.5 s in the largest project (6 writes), about 0.35 s in a 105-note project; 10 open findings: about 4.5 s and 0.6 s. The upload itself is not included.

#### Task automation, activity sampling and output acknowledgements run on timers in a window Chromium may throttle when hidden

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `renderer-logic-background-throttle`
- **Where:** `src/state/activity-store.ts:32`
- **What the code does:** The main window sets no `backgroundThrottling: false` (electron/main.ts:418-423). tickActivity shifts one sample per timer call, not per elapsed second (lines 14-26). The task tick is a 5 s interval (task-store.ts:233); acknowledgements wait on a 50 ms timer and xterm's parse callback (terminals.ts:213, 239). The host pauses a CLI at 1 MB unacknowledged for up to 5 s (pty-manager.ts:393-395).
- **What goes wrong:** Trace is minimized, covered or the screen is locked while the person follows from a phone. Throttled timers stretch the 45-sample quiet check from 45 s to as much as 45 minutes and delay marking tasks stopped or complete; heavy output could pause CLIs 5 s at a time. No acknowledgement stall is recorded in host-health.json's last 100 events, so that part is unconfirmed here.
- **Suggested fix:** Set backgroundThrottling: false on the main window, or move the task tick and quiet detection to the host. Make tickActivity advance by elapsed whole seconds. Add a test that ticks once after a 60 s gap.
- **Checker's notes:** The code facts hold: the main window sets no backgroundThrottling (main.ts:418-423; the only use in the repository is the hidden screen-capture window, screen-capture.ts:31, and there is no command-line switch or powerSaveBlocker); tickActivity shifts one sample per call, not per elapsed second (activity-store.ts:14-26, timer at :32); the task tick is a 5 s interval (task-store.ts:233); acknowledgements use a 50 ms timeout and xterm's parse callback (terminals.ts:213, 239); the host pauses a CLI over 1 MB unacknowledged for up to 5 s (pty-manager.ts:69, 393-395). What I could not establish is the trigger: whether Electron 44 applies Chromium's once-a-minute throttling of chained timers to this window after five minutes minimized or covered. That cannot be read from the repository and I could not test it without touching the running app. Ordinary hidden-page throttling (once a second) does not break a 1 s or 5 s interval, and the acknowledgement timeout is not a chained timer. If the stronger throttling applies, quiet detection (45 zero samples) and stopped/failed marks are delayed while the window is hidden, for terminal channels with tasks only; nothing is lost and it corrects itself when the window is shown. It is unrelated to the reported 1-3 s freezes.
- **Checker's measurements:** ~/.coding-hub/host-health.json, last 100 events (2026-11-14 to 2026-11-18): 0 output-acknowledgement-stalled events (42 cli-started, 42 cli-exited, 6 host-start, 6 host-stop, 3 auto-memory-notice, 1 child-process-gone). No timer measurement was possible in the live app.

#### The status bar's usage poll makes the host re-read every CLI log of the last 60 days after each start, 16 MB per synchronous pass

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `renderer-logic-dayusage-startup`
- **Where:** `src/state/day-usage-store.ts:18`
- **What the code does:** refreshDayUsage asks again 500 ms after any incomplete answer; StatusBar.tsx:70 also calls it every 15 s. The host reads synchronously with a 16 MB budget per call and keeps offsets only in memory (electron/services/hub/day-usage.ts:15, 25, 34, 78-92), and lists and stats every transcript each call (lines 63-76). This computer has 340 logs, 1,440 MB, inside the 60 days.
- **What goes wrong:** After every launch about 90 passes follow each other half a second apart; each blocks the main process while it reads and parses 16 MB. For roughly the first minute terminals echo unevenly. Afterwards each 15 s poll costs one enumeration.
- **Blocking time:** Main process. Measured with a read-only replica on the real log folders (Node 24, other auditors running): 33-79 ms per 16 MB pass for Claude logs plus 21-37 ms to list and stat, about 90 passes per launch; steady state about 30 ms every 15 s. Codex passes were not timed separately.
- **Suggested fix:** Persist per-file offsets and day totals between launches, or do the read off the main thread (worker, or async reads with yields). Until then space the follow-up passes further apart and lower the budget.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. src/state/day-usage-store.ts:18 re-asks 500 ms after an incomplete answer; StatusBar.tsx:70 polls every 15 s and :71 again 2 s after each usage change. electron/services/hub/day-usage.ts keeps offsets only in a Map (line 25), reads synchronously with a 16 MB budget (15, 34, 78-92) and lists and stats every candidate each call (63-76). main.ts:704 runs it in the main process; main.ts:860 (every 60 s) and :606 (phone) call it too. Two corrections: (1) each 15 s interval call that gets an incomplete answer starts its own 500 ms chain, so passes speed up and catch-up takes roughly 30-35 s, not a minute; (2) a single block is 22-111 ms, so this causes uneven echo after launch but cannot be the reported 1-3 s stall. Not established: cost with a cold file cache after a reboot (my run was warm), which could be higher on a slow disk.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only replica of the reader on the real folders (Node 24, warm cache): 340 logs, 1,440.2 MB inside 60 days (~/.claude 195 files 1,202 MB; ~/.codex 127 files 111 MB; two profiles 18 files 127 MB). 91 passes to catch up, 278,347 lines parsed. Per pass: min 22 ms, median 51 ms, p90 85 ms, max 111 ms; Codex-heavy passes 58-93 ms. Total blocking 5.07 s. List and stat: median 13 ms, max 29 ms. Steady state: 23-29 ms per call.

#### Round Table findings are attributed to the wrong task once tasks can wait at the table

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `renderer-logic-evidence-misattributed`
- **Where:** `src/state/task-evidence.ts:10`
- **What the code does:** findingsForTask cuts a task's findings at the next sentAt on the same channel. Since 1.22.0 a task sent while the table is busy waits in nextTasks (electron/services/chat/review-chain.ts:279-280) with its sentAt already set, and a dropped one returns to To do still carrying sentAt (workbench/tasks.ts:55 keeps every field but outcome).
- **What goes wrong:** Task A runs at a hierarchy table; task B is sent and waits. The reviewer's findings on A that arrive after B was sent disappear from A's card in Tasks and show under B, which has not started. If B is dropped back to To do it keeps claiming them.
- **Suggested fix:** Bound a task's findings by when the table actually started the next task (the chain's task carrying its send mark), not by sentAt; clear sentAt and sendId when a task is returned to queued. Extend task-evidence.test.ts with a waiting task.
- **Checker's notes:** src/state/task-evidence.ts:10 bounds a task's findings by the next sentAt on the same channel, whatever that task's status. task-send sets sentAt before the prompt reaches the table (electron/services/workbench/tasks.ts:61), and a task sent to a busy hierarchy table only waits in nextTasks (review-chain.ts:278-280) while the findings record carries on across tasks. So findings on A reported after B.sentAt leave A and show under B. A dropped task goes back to queued through task-status, which strips only outcome and outcomeNote (tasks.ts:53, not 55), so it keeps sentAt and keeps claiming them. Impact is display only: the sole caller is the collapsed 'Everything about this task' block (TasksPanel.tsx:33, 42); no task is moved and nothing is saved from it. The existing test covers only two tasks sent in sequence.

#### Terminal output can set the clipboard for 3 s after any key press, including the Enter that ran the command

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: security. ID: `renderer-logic-osc52-keydown`
- **Where:** `src/runtime/terminals.ts:57`
- **What the code does:** attachCopy marks `touched` on every mouseup and keydown in the terminal (lines 55-56) and the OSC 52 handler copies the decoded text when that was under 3 s ago (line 58). It is attached to every local channel, plain shells included (line 156), and to linked computers' terminals (linked-terminals.ts:69).
- **What goes wrong:** The person runs `cat` on a downloaded file, or an agent prints untrusted file or web content, within 3 s of a key press. An embedded OSC 52 sequence replaces the clipboard with a command of the attacker's choice, which the person later pastes into a shell.
- **Suggested fix:** Honour OSC 52 only after a mouseup (the full-screen selection it exists for) or an explicit copy key, not any keydown; limit it to Claude Code and Codex channels; show a short 'copied from channel' toast so a silent change is visible.
- **Checker's notes:** src/runtime/terminals.ts:55-58: every mouseup and keydown on the terminal element sets `touched`, and the OSC 52 handler writes the decoded text to the clipboard when that was under 3 s ago. It is attached to every local terminal, plain shells included (line 156), and to linked computers' terminals (linked-terminals.ts:69). The Enter that runs `cat file` is a keydown, so output arriving right after it qualifies; the host only caps the length (main.ts:823, COPY_LIMIT). Clipboard read requests are refused (copy-text.ts:19). Reachable, but it is write-only and needs the person to paste later without looking, and it is deliberate design (comment at lines 39-45). Several mainstream terminals accept OSC 52 writes with no gesture check at all, so this is a hardening gap, not an exposure beyond the norm. Low is right.

#### A hierarchy table that needs the person rewrites its task's status on every table event when the note is empty or over 500 characters

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `renderer-logic-needs-you-rewrite`
- **Where:** `src/state/task-store.ts:163`
- **What the code does:** The guard compares `task.outcomeNote !== note`, but what is stored is `note.slice(0, 500) || 'The Round Table needs you.'`. For an empty note or one longer than 500 characters the two never match, so each `table` event issues `task-status` and a full loadTasks again.
- **What goes wrong:** The chain stops at needs-you with a long planner note. Every later table event (a finding update, a waiting message, a seat going idle) rewrites tasks.json on the host and reloads the board, for as long as the table waits.
- **Suggested fix:** Compute the stored form once (`const stored = note.slice(0, 500) || 'The Round Table needs you.'`) and compare against that. Add a case to task-store.test.ts with an empty and a 600-character note.
- **Checker's notes:** src/state/task-store.ts:163 compares task.outcomeNote with the raw note but stores `note.slice(0, 500) || 'The Round Table needs you.'`, so a note over 500 characters or an empty one never matches and each table event issues task-status plus loadTasks. Reachability is narrower than stated. The long case is real: review-chain.ts:507 builds the needs-you note from the planner's last CHANGES NEEDED line, which is uncapped (verdictOf, 73-77). The empty case I could not reach: every site that sets needs-you also sets a note (350, 451, 479, 504, 507). Table events while the chain rests at needs-you are few (a waiting message, a dropped task, a late seat `done` at 435), and the write does not trigger another table event, so there is no loop. Cost is a handful of redundant tasks.json writes; nothing visible goes wrong.

#### Restoring a run into a full workspace can replace a hidden Round Table seat or a lead that still holds findings

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `renderer-logic-restore-replaces-seat`
- **Where:** `src/state/workspace-store.ts:214`
- **What the code does:** restoreRun picks `state.sessions.find(...)`: the first session that is not running or starting, from all sessions, not from channelsOf. That can be a stopped hidden partner or planner, or a stopped lead whose roundTable carries findings or waiting tasks. The session is swapped in place at line 217 with no cleanup of the table that pointed at it.
- **What goes wrong:** Eight agents are in use and a table's reviewer has stopped. Session history's Restore replaces the reviewer nobody can see: the lead's table now reports it lost its planner or reviewer, while no visible panel was replaced as the dialog promised. Replacing a stopped lead drops its unsaved findings.
- **Suggested fix:** Choose the slot from channelsOf(sessions) and skip leads with a roundTable record (or archive and retire the table first); when only seats are idle, return false so the dialog's message shows.
- **Checker's notes:** src/state/workspace-store.ts:214 takes the first non-running session from all sessions, not channelsOf, and line 217 swaps it in place with no table cleanup. Both callers promise 'the first idle panel/tile is replaced' (SessionHistory.tsx:46, WorkflowCenter.tsx:29). The most reachable case is not a lone stopped reviewer but a full workspace right after a restart: hydrate sets every session idle (line 173), so Restore replaces sessions[0]. If that is a table's lead, its roundTable record (findings, chain, waiting tasks) is dropped without archiveFindings and its seats turn into visible tiles (seatedIds depends on the lead's record). Seats are appended after their lead (round-table.ts:45, 212), so a hidden seat is chosen only when every earlier session is running; then the lead points at a missing seat and reopenHierarchy reports it lost (round-table.ts:231). Needs all eight slots in use plus a rare action, so low.

#### A waiting Round Table message's images (up to 6 MB of base64) are serialized on every workspace store change

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `renderer-logic-waiting-images-stringify`
- **Where:** `src/runtime/round-table.ts:133`
- **What the code does:** rememberTable copies `table.waitingImages` into the lead's saved record and compares records with two JSON.stringify calls per table event (line 136). The save subscriber stringifies the whole saved workspace on every store notification (use-native-host.ts:115-118), and each real change writes it to storage. The host keeps up to 6,000,000 characters of image data in the table state (chat/round-table.ts:29, 94).
- **What goes wrong:** The person sends screenshots to a busy table, so the message waits. Until it goes, every store notification (including the no-op ones on each tool call) stringifies and compares about 6 MB, and each real change saves 6 MB to storage.db. Estimated tens of milliseconds each; not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep waiting images out of the compared and saved JSON (store them once under a content hash, or in their own row), and skip savedWorkspace when the store's saved fields are the same references as last time.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: rememberTable copies waitingImages into the saved record and compares with two JSON.stringify calls (src/runtime/round-table.ts:133, 136); the save subscriber stringifies the whole saved workspace on every store notification (use-native-host.ts:115-118), and zustand 5 notifies even when a setter returns {}. The schema allows up to 6,000,000 characters (shared/schemas.ts:40; chat/round-table.ts:29, 94; review-chain.ts:121), and each real change is written synchronously as one row in the main process (workspace-storage.ts:64, via main.ts:788). But the impact is smaller than the finding's 'tens of milliseconds' estimate, and it applies only while images wait at a busy table. It is waste, not a perceptible stall, and not a cause of the reported freezes.
- **Checker's measurements:** Node 24, synthetic workspace holding 6,000,427 characters (three images of 2 MB base64): JSON.stringify plus compare per store notification median 5.3 ms, max 6.0 ms; rememberTable's two stringifies per table event median 10.0 ms, max 11.0 ms; structuredClone (one IPC direction) median 3.4 ms; host stringify plus SQLite write of the row (scratch database, WAL) median 10.1 ms, max 32.6 ms. Zod validation and real IPC not timed.

#### addChatSession turns an existing channel into a chat when the workspace is already full

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `renderer-logic-addchat-full`
- **Where:** `src/state/workspace-store.ts:221`
- **What the code does:** addChatSession calls addSession, which does nothing at eight sessions (line 223), then sets `chat: true` on `sessions.at(-1)` whenever its engine matches, without checking that a session was added or that it is stopped. startTaskInWorktree checks the limit before two awaits (task-worktree.ts:32-39), so a channel added meanwhile (a phone's + Channel or Resume) fills the workspace first.
- **What goes wrong:** Seven sessions exist; 'start in a worktree' is pressed and, while Git creates the worktree, the phone adds a channel. The last channel, possibly a running Claude terminal, becomes a chat: routeTerminalEvent (terminals.ts:221-225) stops drawing its output and only acknowledges it, so its terminal goes dead until restart.
