- **Suggested fix:** In addChatSession compare the session ids before and after addSession and change only a newly added one; re-check the limit in startTaskInWorktree after the awaits. Add a test at MAX_CHANNELS.
- **Checker's notes:** src/state/workspace-store.ts:221: addSession is a no-op at eight sessions (line 223), yet addChatSession then sets chat: true on sessions.at(-1) when its engine matches, with no check that a session was added or of its status. Three of the four callers check the limit synchronously just before (round-table.ts:176, 193; use-native-host.ts:230). Only startTaskInWorktree has a gap: limit check at task-worktree.ts:32, then two awaits (33, 36) before line 39. One correction to the scenario: every add path appends, so the session flipped is the one added during the gap, which is normally still idle; it silently becomes a chat instead of a terminal, and the worktree task fails with 'no channel could be added' (line 41). The dead-terminal outcome (terminals.ts:221-225) needs that channel to be started too within the same one-to-few-second window; I could not establish that. Narrow race, low.

### Window components (14)

#### Close on a tile stops a running agent and removes the channel at once, with no question and no undo

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: ux. ID: `renderer-ui-close-no-confirm`
- **Where:** `src/components/SessionTile.tsx:150`
- **What the code does:** SessionTile.tsx:150 Close calls close() (line 93), then closeChannel (use-native-host.ts:188-195): it stops the CLI and removes the session. A Round Table lead takes its hidden reviewer and planner too (workspace-store.ts:234) and the chat thread is dropped (terminals.ts:261). The same action on a linked computer asks 'Stop and close?' first (LinkedComputerView.tsx:98-102).
- **What goes wrong:** The person aims for Maximize or Duplicate in the hover row and hits Close on a channel mid-task. The agent is killed; its scrollback or chat thread and its settings are gone; a hierarchy table loses three agents. Nothing asks first. The conversation can only be found again under Session history, native CLI conversations.
- **Suggested fix:** Ask before closing a running channel (the inline 'Stop and close? / Keep' pattern LinkedTile already has) and show a short 'Channel closed · Undo' notice that restores the session with its resumeId.
- **Checker's notes:** SessionTile.tsx:150 calls close() (lines 93-97) directly; closeChannel (use-native-host.ts:188-195) stops the run and its hidden seats, waits for exit, then removeSession (workspace-store.ts:230-236) drops the session and its stopped seats. releaseRemovedTerminals (terminals.ts:258-262) disposes the terminal and forgets the chat thread. There is no confirmation and no undo anywhere in src (searched for undo/confirm/restore; only permissions.ts matches, unrelated). The button is disabled only for the last channel, while starting, or while stopping, and it sits next to Maximize and Duplicate in the hover row. The linked-computer tile does ask first (screens/LinkedComputerView.tsx:98-101; the auditor's path lacked the screens/ folder). Extra loss the auditor did not state: closeChannel does not call archiveFindings, which ChatView's closeTable does (ChatView.tsx:107), so a Round Table's findings and chain state held on session.roundTable go with the tile. Mitigation: files on disk are untouched, the recorded output stays in Session history and the CLI conversation can be resumed from there. Needs a misclick, but the result is a killed agent with no way back in place.

#### Notices and strips inside a tile resize the running CLI's terminal each time they appear or go

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: freeze. ID: `renderer-ui-tile-strips-resize-pty`
- **Where:** `src/components/SessionTile.tsx:162`
- **What the code does:** SessionTile.tsx:161-171 puts the attention bar, error bar, budget notice, TaskStrip, LiveTipStrip and PastedImages in the tile's flex column beside .terminal-frame (flex: 1, styles.css:42). TerminalViewport.tsx:11-13 calls fit() on every ResizeObserver callback, undebounced; terminals.ts:157 sends each new size to the PTY. tile.css:69-70 and :192 show the detail row and transcript were made overlays to avoid this.
- **What goes wrong:** Claude Code asks for approval: the ~34 px attention bar appears, the terminal loses one or two rows and the CLI is resized while drawing its prompt, then again when answered. Same for a coaching tip, task strip or pasted image. Each resize makes the CLI redraw. Dragging the window edge sends a resize per frame to all eight PTYs.
- **Blocking time:** Not measured. One CLI redraw per resize in that channel; its length depends on the CLI and conversation size. A candidate for the reported pauses, not confirmed.
- **Suggested fix:** Draw the attention, error and tip bars over the terminal (as the detail row and transcript overlay are) or reserve their height; debounce fit() by about 100 ms and skip sendSize when cols and rows are unchanged.
- **Checker's notes:** Mechanism holds. SessionTile.tsx:161-171 puts the coaching/attention/error/budget bars and Task/Tip/PastedImages strips in the tile's flex column; .terminal-frame is flex:1 (styles.css:42), so each one changes the terminal's height. TerminalViewport.tsx:11-13 fits on every ResizeObserver callback with no debounce, terminals.ts:157 sends the new size, pty-manager.ts:432-440 resizes the PTY at once. tile.css:69-70 and :192 show the author meant to avoid exactly this for the detail row and transcript. Attention is raised only for permission_prompt and elicitation_dialog (attention.ts:40), so it is two resizes per approval prompt, not per turn. Two parts are overstated: (1) 'skip sendSize when cols and rows are unchanged' is already the case: FitAddon 0.11 fit() resizes only when cols/rows differ and xterm 6 resize() returns early on equal sizes, so onResize does not fire; (2) dragging a window edge therefore sends a resize per cell-count change, not per frame. What is missing: any measurement of how long Claude Code or Codex takes to redraw after a rows-only resize in a long session, and so any link to the reported 1-3 s pauses. Certain impact is a visible reflow/redraw of that channel at each approval; freeze contribution is unproven.
- **Checker's measurements:** No timing taken (would need a running CLI). Geometry only: terminal font 13 px x line-height 1.45 = 18.85 px per row on this computer (preferences.json terminalFontSize 13); the attention bar is about 34 px (workbench.css:278: 6+6 padding, 2 px borders, 14 px text), so 1-2 rows are lost each time it shows.

#### A chat thread has no size limit and is re-rendered in full for every streamed token, keystroke and unrelated store update

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: performance. ID: `renderer-ui-chat-rerender-per-delta`
- **Where:** `src/components/chat/ChatView.tsx:136`
- **What the code does:** chat-store.ts:67 makes a new items array per delta; threads are never capped (chat-store.ts:27-30 keeps items across restarts). ChatView.tsx:136-139 maps every item to an un-memoised Item (ChatParts.tsx:87) that re-parses Markdown and re-stringifies tool inputs (ChatParts.tsx:61). Deltas are one IPC message each (claude-stream.ts:115). Scratch benchmark (server render, Node 24): 13-18 ms per pass at 1,000 items, 24-31 ms at 2,000.
- **What goes wrong:** A hierarchy Round Table runs for hours and its merged thread passes 1,000 items. Each streamed token, each key typed in the message box (setText re-renders ChatView) and each store update from other channels re-renders the whole thread. At 20-50 deltas a second the window's one renderer thread is mostly busy, so every channel's terminal and typing lag. Real DOM cost not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Wrap Item and Markdown in React.memo with stable callbacks, apply deltas once per animation frame, and render only the last few hundred items with a 'Show earlier' control.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described: applyChatEvent copies the items array for every event (chat-store.ts:56, :67), nothing caps a thread, ChatView.tsx:136-139 maps every item to Item, which is not memoised (ChatParts.tsx:87) and gets new answerFor closures each render, and Markdown re-parses each text block. Each delta is its own IPC message (claude-stream.ts:115, main.ts:155), and the message box's text state lives in ChatView (line 50), so each keystroke re-renders the thread too. Corrections: other channels' deltas do NOT re-render this ChatView (its selector returns its own thread object, ChatView.tsx:26); it re-renders with its parent SessionTile on any workspace-store change (SessionTile.tsx:60 subscribes to the whole store) and on usage changes. Reaching 1,000 items is harder than implied: the store is in memory only and a resumed chat loads at most 200 earlier messages (transcript.ts:7 MESSAGES = 200), so it takes about 800 new messages in one app session. At typical sizes (under 500 items) a pass is under 4 ms. Missing: a real DOM measurement and evidence that threads here reach that size. This is growing lag in long chats, not the 1-3 s stall.
- **Checker's measurements:** Server-render benchmark of the real Item/Markdown code (bundled to scratch, Node 24, production React, machine busy with other auditors): 200 items 2.0 ms per pass, 500 items 3.7 ms, 1,000 items 7.9 ms, 2,000 items 12.9 ms (medians of 12). Auditor reported 13-18 ms and 24-31 ms with heavier items. From a copy of storage.db: 74 of 102 recorded runs were chat runs, so chat channels are used here; the saved workspace now holds 2 Claude terminal channels.

#### Alt+1-8 is captured everywhere, so on a Mac Option+digit characters (# on UK, [ ] | { on German keyboards) cannot be typed

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `renderer-ui-alt-digit-mac`
- **Where:** `src/components/AccessibilityControls.tsx:13`
- **What the code does:** AccessibilityControls.tsx:13-21 listens on window in the capture phase; for altKey + Digit1-8 without Ctrl/Meta it calls preventDefault and stopPropagation and focuses that channel, whatever has focus. On macOS Option is altKey and Option+digit types characters. There is no platform or focused-field check (view-shortcuts.ts:17 has one for the G shortcuts). Not run on a Mac.
- **What goes wrong:** On a Mac with a UK keyboard Option+3 is '#'; on a German one Option+5/6/7/8 are '[', ']', '|', '{'. With that many channels open the key jumps to another channel instead of typing, in terminals, chat boxes and every form. Windows AltGr is unaffected because it also sets ctrlKey.
- **Suggested fix:** On macOS use Ctrl+1-8 for channel focus, or let the event through when event.key is a printable character other than the digit; add a unit test for a Mac Option+3 keydown.
- **Checker's notes:** AccessibilityControls.tsx:11-25 registers a capture-phase keydown listener on window; for altKey without ctrlKey/metaKey and code Digit1-8 it calls preventDefault and stopPropagation and focuses that channel when one exists at that index (line 15). It is mounted for the whole app (App.tsx:30). There is no platform check and no focused-field check, unlike view-shortcuts.ts:17. On macOS Option sets altKey and Option+digit produces a character through the keydown's default action, so cancelling the keydown at window capture stops it reaching xterm, the chat box and every input. No other code handles this (searched altKey/Digit across src and electron). The digit is taken only when that many channels are open (Option+3 needs three channels). Windows AltGr sets ctrlKey as well, so it is unaffected, as the auditor says. Verified by reading only; Chromium's macOS key events are standard behaviour but I did not run it. Severity for this person is low: the layout here is US, where Option+digit gives rarely typed symbols. For anyone on a UK or German Mac keyboard it is medium ('#', '[', ']', '|', '{' cannot be typed).
- **Checker's measurements:** This computer's input layout is en-US (Get-WinUserLanguageList: 0409:00000409). Not run on a Mac.

#### Opening a channel's Settings reads every memory note of every project on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `renderer-ui-settings-reads-all-notes`
- **Where:** `src/components/EngineControls.tsx:28`
- **What the code does:** EngineControls.tsx:28 calls hub 'memory-projects' each time a tile's Settings opens. service.ts:89 runs MemoryService.projects() (memory.ts:23-30), which list()s each project: statSync, readFileSync, JSON.parse and zod parse per note, synchronously. Replayed read-only on this computer's notes: 1,858 notes, 98-133 ms per call (warm cache). 'memory-list' of the largest project (785 notes): 38-45 ms, 0.9 MB reply.
- **What goes wrong:** The person clicks Settings on a tile and the main process stops for about 0.1 s (more with a cold disk cache, and growing with the notes), so all channels' output and typing pause. Opening Shared memory, its Refresh and each save there block again for the selected project's notes.
- **Blocking time:** Main process: 98-133 ms per Settings open here, measured in Node 24 against the real notes with a warm cache; 38-45 ms per Shared memory list of the largest project.
- **Suggested fix:** Answer 'memory-projects' from folder listings (count and newest file time) without reading notes, or cache counts per project by folder mtime; read notes with fs.promises.
- **Checker's notes:** EngineControls.tsx:28 calls hub 'memory-projects' in an effect when MemoryProjectControl mounts, which is each time Settings is opened on a Claude or Codex tile (SessionTile.tsx:153-154; not for terminal channels). service.ts:89 returns this.memory.projects(), and memory.ts:23-30 calls list() per project folder, which does statSync, readFileSync, JSON.parse and a zod parse for every note (memory.ts:73-80), all synchronous on the main process. It is the only caller of 'memory-projects'. Shared memory's 'memory-list' (HubPanels.tsx:83, :98) blocks the same way for one project. The data is larger than the task's figure (1,858 notes in 10 folders, not about 1,200 in 7). Real impact: a 0.15-0.17 s main-process pause that the person triggers by clicking Settings, so it is not the random 1-3 s stall; it grows with the notes and would be worse on a cold cache. Low is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** Ran the real MemoryService code (bundled to scratch, constructor bypassed, read-only) against ~/.coding-hub/memory: projects() took 167.6, 148.8, 162.2, 148.4, 172.5, 161.5 ms per call; 10 projects, 1,858 notes, largest 785. list() of the largest project: 58.2, 47.0, 54.9, 50.7, 46.8 ms, reply 927,870 bytes. Warm cache; machine busy with other auditors, so a little above the auditor's 98-133 ms.

#### The status bar's 15-second usage poll walks and parses the CLIs' logs synchronously on the main process

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: freeze. ID: `renderer-ui-usage-today-poll`
- **Where:** `src/components/shell/StatusBar.tsx:70`
- **What the code does:** StatusBar.tsx:70-71 calls 'usage-today' every 15 s and 2 s after each usage event; day-usage-store.ts:18 repeats after 500 ms until caught up. main.ts:704 runs DayUsage.totals() synchronously (day-usage.ts:31-48). Measured here: the folder walk takes 18-22 ms (199 readdir, 125 of them failing, 680 stat); the 340 logs total 1,439 MB; each 16 MB pass takes 35-68 ms.
- **What goes wrong:** After every start the status bar drives about 90 passes, each holding the main process 35-68 ms, half a second apart, for roughly the first minute; afterwards about 20 ms every 15 s and after each usage report. These are small hitches in terminal output, not seconds, but they add to other main-process work.
- **Blocking time:** Main process: about 20 ms every 15 s (measured); about 90 passes of 35-68 ms after each start (12 passes measured in Node 24, the count extrapolated from 1,439 MB / 16 MB).
- **Suggested fix:** Read the logs with fs.promises or in a worker thread, lower READ_BUDGET to a few MB, and persist file offsets and day totals so a restart does not read 1.4 GB again.
- **Checker's notes:** StatusBar.tsx:70 polls 'usage-today' at mount and every 15 s, and line 71 again 2 s after the usage array last changed; day-usage-store.ts:18 repeats 500 ms later while the reply is not complete. main.ts:704 runs dayUsage.totals() synchronously: days() walks every project folder with readdirSync/statSync (day-usage.ts:63-76), stats each candidate again in read() (line 80), and reads and JSON-parses up to 16 MB per pass (lines 15, 78-92). File offsets live only in memory (line 25), so every start reads all 1.44 GB again. The same totals() also runs on the 60 s usage-alert timer (main.ts:860) and for a phone's usage request (main.ts:606), which the auditor did not list. My numbers agree with the auditor's: 54-72 ms per pass for roughly the first 50 s after each start (about 12% of the main thread in that window), then about 22-28 ms per poll. These are small hitches, far from 1-3 s on their own, but they stack with other main-process work. Low is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** Ran the real DayUsage code (bundled to scratch, read-only) on ~/.claude, ~/.codex and the two profile folders: 4 sources, 340 candidate logs, 1,440 MB. Folder walk plus one stat per log (what a caught-up pass costs): 28.1, 22.0, 21.6, 25.2, 21.8, 21.9 ms. First eight 16 MB passes of totals(): 72, 65, 68, 54, 67, 68, 64, 61 ms, none complete. Passes to catch up after a start: 91 (1,440 MB / 16 MB), 500 ms apart, so about 50 s.

#### Status bar cells do not shrink, so with several channels the alerts and 'Needs you' are pushed off the right edge

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: ux. ID: `renderer-ui-statusbar-overflow`
- **Where:** `src/shell.css:57`
- **What the code does:** shell.css:57 gives every status cell flex: none; only project, alert and save status may shrink (line 60) and .app-shell clips overflow (line 3). StatusBar.tsx:78-100 renders a cell per channel (with tokens), two plan-limit cells, Σ today, Needs you, alerts and save status. From the 12 px mono font I estimate about 186 px per channel cell and about 750 px for the fixed right-hand cells.
- **What goes wrong:** Four channels with usage at 1440 px (or 1920 px at 125% scale) need about 1,490 px: the alert text shrinks to nothing and the last cells are cut. With eight channels (about 2,240 px) a 1920 px window loses 'Needs you' and the alerts, the cells that say a channel is waiting. Estimated from the CSS, not seen in the running app.
- **Suggested fix:** Drop the token count, then the state word, from channel cells by container query (as the top bar does at lines 83-92), and place Needs you and alerts where they cannot be clipped.
- **Checker's notes:** The CSS is as described: every .tr-sb-cell is flex:none (shell.css:57), only project, alert and save status can shrink (lines 60-61), the bar does not wrap or scroll and .app-shell clips (line 3); the only narrow-window rule for the bar hides the host cell at 1366 px or less (line 84). State words are short (IDLE, RUN, HOLD: channel-state.ts:9-17), and my arithmetic lands on the auditor's figures. Four channels with usage need about 1,650 px at a 1440 px window: host and save status are cut and the alert text shrinks to nothing; 'Needs you' survives at four channels and is lost at eight on a 1920 px window (about 2,400 px needed). On this computer the trigger is weak: a maximized window is 2,560 px, which fits eight channels with about 160 px to spare, and the workspace currently has two channels (fits down to about 1,280 px). It bites with a non-maximized window, interface scale 110-125%, or the smaller Mac screen. The waiting state is also shown on the tile, its border and a desktop notification, so nothing is silently missed. Missing: an observation in the running app.
- **Checker's measurements:** Not seen running; widths computed from the CSS. Font is IBM Plex Mono 12 px (tokens.css:75, bundled in main.tsx:12-14), uppercase with .06em spacing (flight-deck.css:59): 7.92 px per character. Channel cell with tokens about 187 px ('CH1', glyph, RUN, '48.2K TOK'). Fixed right-hand cells about 720 px (Claude limits 211, Codex limits 203, sum-today about 210, 'no alerts' 96), plus the host cell 132 px above 1366 px. Display here: 2560x1440 at 96 DPI; saved workspace has 2 channels.

#### Choosing another Claude account in a channel's Settings silently discards its saved conversation link

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: data-loss. ID: `renderer-ui-profile-change-drops-conversation`
- **Where:** `src/components/EngineControls.tsx:61`
- **What the code does:** EngineControls.tsx:59-62: the account select rebuilds the config without resumeId ('cannot resume under another'). Nothing is shown, and choosing the original profile again does not restore it. The only hint is the fixed line 'Stop this session before changing its account' (line 64).
- **What goes wrong:** A stopped channel holds a long conversation. The person opens Settings, picks the wrong account, then picks the right one again. The link to the conversation is gone, so Start begins a new conversation without saying so; the old one has to be found under Session history, native CLI conversations.
- **Suggested fix:** Remember the dropped resumeId per profile while the panel is open and restore it when that profile is chosen again; state under the list that the next start will be a new conversation.
- **Checker's notes:** src/components/EngineControls.tsx:59-62 rebuilds the config without accountProfileId and resumeId on every change of the account select, and nothing restores resumeId when the first profile is chosen again. The select only works on a stopped channel (SessionTile.tsx:153 disables the fieldset while running; workspace-store.ts:247 refuses the update), so the scenario is exactly a stopped channel. After that, startSession passes the config with no resumeId, so a new conversation starts. Extra detail: on Windows, arrow keys on the focused select fire onChange per step, so just moving through the list drops the link. Hints are only indirect (the Conversation button at SessionTile.tsx:145 disappears). The old conversation stays in the CLI's own history, so nothing is deleted: low is right, and 'data-loss' overstates it.

#### Adding or closing a channel throws away the person's tile arrangement and switches to the grid layout

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `renderer-ui-layout-reset-on-add`
- **Where:** `src/state/workspace-store.ts:228`
- **What the code does:** addSession (workspace-store.ts:228) sets tiles: arrangeTiles(sessions, 'grid') and layoutMode: 'grid'; removeSession (line 242) re-arranges every tile; restoreRun (218), NativeHistory.tsx:20, remoteResume (use-native-host.ts:224) and CoachingPanel.tsx:30 do the same. SessionGrid.tsx:43 otherwise keeps dragged sizes and positions.
- **What goes wrong:** The person has chosen columns and resized two tiles. They add a Terminal channel (or a phone adds one, or a Round Table closes its seats): every tile snaps to the default grid, the mode becomes grid, every terminal is resized, and the old arrangement cannot be brought back.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep existing tiles and place only the new one in the first free slot; keep the current layout mode; re-arrange only when the person presses an arrangement button.
- **Checker's notes:** src/state/workspace-store.ts:228 (addSession) sets tiles: arrangeTiles(sessions,'grid') and layoutMode:'grid'; :242 (removeSession) lays every tile out again in the current mode, dropping dragged sizes; :218 restoreRun, NativeHistory.tsx:20, CoachingPanel.tsx:30 and use-native-host.ts:224 (remoteResume) do the same as addSession. Callers: Topbar.tsx:61, Duplicate (SessionTile.tsx:148), a phone or linked computer (use-native-host.ts:232), + Channel Round Table (round-table.ts:177, :194), task worktree (task-worktree.ts:39). One sub-claim is wrong: closing a Round Table's hidden seats goes through retire(), which uses fitTiles (round-table.ts:113) and keeps the tiles; opening a table on an existing chat (round-table.ts:45) does not touch tiles either. The rest holds; there is no undo. A UX shortfall, not a fault.

#### Every Claude tool call re-renders all tiles, bars and chat threads through whole-store subscriptions

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: performance. ID: `renderer-ui-whole-store-rerenders`
- **Where:** `src/state/workspace-store.ts:199`
- **What the code does:** applyEvent (workspace-store.ts:199-209) builds a new sessions array for every non-data event, changed or not. Claude Code's PostToolUse hook reaches Trace after each tool call (attention.ts:71, :114) and becomes an 'answered' event (pty-manager.ts:129). SessionTile.tsx:60, Topbar.tsx:49, StatusBar.tsx:61, Toasts.tsx:15, CommandMenu.tsx:38 and App.tsx:34 subscribe to the whole store; use-native-host.ts:115-118 re-serialises the workspace each time.
- **What goes wrong:** Eight busy Claude channels make several tool calls a second. Each one re-renders every tile, the top and status bars, toasts and, through SessionTile, each chat channel's whole thread, and stringifies the saved workspace. Not measured in the app; it is steady renderer work that makes the chat finding worse.
- **Suggested fix:** Return the same state from applyEvent when no session changed, subscribe with selectors instead of the whole store, and wrap SessionTile in React.memo.
- **Checker's notes:** Mechanism holds end to end. workspace-store.ts:199 maps sessions into a new array for every non-data event, so zustand 5.0.15 notifies every subscriber even when nothing changed. Claude runs, chat and terminal, get the PostToolUse http hook (main.ts:164-169, attention.ts:114); attention.ts:71 calls onResolved; pty-manager.ts:129 sends 'answered'; use-native-host.ts:88 applies it. Whole-store subscribers at SessionTile.tsx:60, SessionGrid.tsx:7, Topbar.tsx:49, StatusBar.tsx:61, Toasts.tsx:15, CommandMenu.tsx:38, App.tsx:34 re-render; src/ has no React.memo, so each SessionTile re-renders its ChatView and every Item, and Markdown parses every reply again. use-native-host.ts:115-118 re-serialises the workspace each time; :63 adds another full notify per usage update that carries a conversationId. My measurement shows the cost per event is a few milliseconds, so this is steady background work, not a cause of the reported 1-3 s stalls. The rate of tool calls in real use was not measured.
- **Checker's measurements:** Synthetic thread rendered with the repo's own Item/Markdown components (bundled to scratch, React 18.3.1 server renderer, Node 24, production mode): 50 items / 26 KB of reply text = 1.3 ms per full render; 200 items / 106 KB = 4.3 ms; 600 items / 317 KB = 12.0 ms. Browser reconciliation was not measured; the frequency of PostToolUse events in the running app was not measured.

#### Links in chat replies cannot be opened or copied

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `renderer-ui-chat-links-dead`
- **Where:** `src/components/chat/Markdown.tsx:23`
- **What the code does:** Markdown.tsx:23 renders a Markdown link as a span with class chat-link and the address only in its title tooltip: no click handler, no copy action, and bare URLs are not recognised. (The rendering is safe: replies become React elements, and src/ has no dangerouslySetInnerHTML.)
- **What goes wrong:** An agent answers in a chat channel with a pull request or documentation link. The person can neither open it nor copy its address: they hover, read the tooltip and retype it, or ask the agent to print the raw URL. In a terminal channel the same link is at least selectable.
- **Suggested fix:** On click show the address with 'Open in browser' and 'Copy', opened through a host call that accepts only http and https URLs (as flow-open-pr validates its URL); never navigate the window.
- **Checker's notes:** src/components/chat/Markdown.tsx:23 renders [text](url) as <span className="chat-link" title={url}> with no handler; the inline pattern at :14 has no case for bare URLs. tile.css:147 gives it cursor: help, and the file comment (:6) says links show their address on hover and do not navigate, so this is a deliberate safety choice with no way to act on the link. main.ts:425-426 deny window.open and navigation, and no host call opens an arbitrary http(s) URL (only flow-open-pr at main.ts:711, GitHub pull requests). Slight overstatement: a bare URL in a reply is ordinary selectable text (no user-select: none on chat text), so it can be copied; only the address behind a Markdown link is unreachable except by reading the tooltip.

#### Removing a task, a prompt example or all coached prompts happens on one click with no confirmation

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `renderer-ui-remove-no-confirm`

- **What the code does:** TasksPanel.tsx:112 Remove calls removeTask (task-store.ts:33, 'task-remove') directly. CoachingPanel.tsx:40 (trash icon on an example) and LiveCoachingCard.tsx:32 ('Clear coached prompts') also act at once. Other removals confirm inline: HubPanels.tsx:41 (MCP server), WorkflowCenter.tsx:42 (template), WorktreeManager.tsx:8.

CoachingPanel.tsx:40 (trash icon calls remove(item.id), defined at :21) and LiveCoachingCard.tsx:32 (clearLiveCoaching) also act on one click. #### Typing in Shared memory's project key field sends a list request per keystroke and leaves each partial key in the 30-second Drive sync

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `renderer-ui-memory-key-keystrokes`
- **Where:** `src/components/HubPanels.tsx:99`
- **What the code does:** HubPanels.tsx:99: the 'Shared project key' input calls setProject on every keystroke, and the effect at line 98 calls 'memory-list' for each partial key. service.ts:87 first does this.projects.add(request.project); tick() (service.ts:64) then calls drive.syncLater for every key in that set every 30 s until Trace restarts. The input's pattern is not enforced, so invalid text returns the raw validation message.
- **What goes wrong:** Typing 'ophthalmicacademy-da6198e1c7' sends 28 list requests to the main process and leaves 28 junk keys in the Drive sync, each listing a local and a Drive folder that do not exist, every 30 seconds. Typing a space shows a raw validation error in the panel.
- **Suggested fix:** Hold the typed key in local state and apply it on Enter, blur or a 400 ms pause once it matches the pattern; do not add a key with no notes to the sync set from 'memory-list'.
- **Checker's notes:** src/components/HubPanels.tsx:99: the key input calls setProject per keystroke (the pattern attribute is not enforced outside a form submit); the effect at :98 sends 'memory-list' for each value. electron/services/hub/service.ts:87 adds each to this.projects before listing; tick() at :64 calls drive.syncLater for every key every 30 s; nothing removes a key until restart. An invalid value (a space, or an empty field after deleting) fails projectSchema and service.ts:80 returns zod's raw message, which hub-store.ts:10 throws and the panel shows. For a key with no folder, syncLater still does synchronous work on the main process: mirror() and sync() each read preferences.json and stat/realpath the Drive folder (drive.ts:24-28, :68-74). The work is in chunks of about 4 ms separated by awaits, so it adds load but no visible freeze. Only reached by typing in a rarely used field.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only emulation of syncLater for keys with no folder, against this computer's real ~/.coding-hub/memory and Drive folder (same stat/lstat/realpath/readdir calls, a stat in place of mkdirSync, zod parsing left out): 28 junk keys cost 220-263 ms of synchronous time per 30 s tick over 5 rounds, 7.9-9.4 ms per key, in two chunks of about 4 ms per key.

#### No error boundary: one render error in any tile or screen blanks the whole window

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: improvement. ID: `renderer-ui-no-error-boundary`
- **Where:** `src/main.tsx:29`
- **What the code does:** main.tsx:29-31 renders App with no error boundary and src/ has none (no componentDidCatch or getDerivedStateFromError), so React 18 unmounts the root on an uncaught render error. One path: ChatParts.tsx:95-97 sends every block that is not 'text' or 'thinking' to ToolCard, whose toolSummary reads input[key] (line 42) and JSON.stringify(block.input).slice (line 61). I did not check whether the host validates block types from a linked computer.
- **What goes wrong:** A linked computer on a newer Trace sends a chat block of a type this version does not know, or a tool block without input. ToolCard throws while rendering and the entire window goes blank, all channels' tiles included, while their CLIs keep running. Only reloading or restarting Trace brings the interface back.
- **Suggested fix:** Add an error boundary around each tile and each screen that shows 'This view failed · Reload view' and leaves the rest running; make Item skip block types it does not know.
- **Checker's notes:** src/main.tsx:29-31 renders <App /> with no boundary, and src/ has no componentDidCatch, getDerivedStateFromError or ErrorBoundary (searched), so React 18 unmounts the whole root on any render error. I checked what the auditor could not: chat events from a linked computer are validated only as { type: string } with passthrough (electron/services/remote/linked-computers.ts:24-26, used at :393 and :398), so block shapes reach the renderer unchecked (RemoteChat.tsx:17-27, then Item). ChatParts.tsx:95-97 sends any block that is not text or thinking to ToolCard; with no input, toolSummary throws at :42/:52 (Object.values(undefined)). Blocks made on this computer are always well formed (claude-stream.ts:123-125, codex-stream.ts:181-197, transcript.ts:51, :69), and no released version sends another block type, so the trigger is latent: a future version on a linked computer, or any other render bug. Low today; the missing boundary is real.

### Build, release, tests and docs (14)

#### Nothing records or tests main-process stalls, so each freeze report starts from zero

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: test-gap. ID: `build-tests-docs-no-stall-detection`
- **Where:** `electron/services/storage/host-health.ts:29`
- **What the code does:** HostHealth.record (lines 30-34) stores lifecycle events only. A search of electron/, src/ and shared/ finds no monitorEventLoopDelay and no window 'unresponsive' handler. No test sets a time or read budget for the 30 s hub tick, the first Drive comparison after start, or launch-time sync at this computer's data sizes (about 1,200 notes, 8 MB usage.db).
- **What goes wrong:** The host blocks for 1-3 s, as reported. Nothing is written anywhere, so the cause has to be found by attaching a profiler, as for 1.21.3. A change that brings back a full note re-read per tick passes CI and the release candidate.
- **Suggested fix:** In main.ts run perf_hooks.monitorEventLoopDelay; when a 10 s window's max exceeds about 250 ms, record a host-health event naming the IPC channel or timer that was running. Add a vitest that runs the hub tick and Drive sync over about 1,200 fixture notes and asserts file reads and elapsed time.
- **Checker's notes:** Main claim holds. HostHealth.record (host-health.ts:29-33, not 30-34) is only called for lifecycle events (main.ts:75-80, 445, 453), and nothing in the host or renderer measures event-loop delay or handles a window 'unresponsive' event. So a 1-3 s block leaves no trace; the 100-event cap, mostly filled by cli-started/cli-exited, also means the file holds only about four days. One sub-claim is overstated: hub.test.ts:345-361 does assert that a repeated DriveMirror.sync and syncLater call neither MemoryService.note nor MemoryService.list, including the same-content-different-bytes case, so the exact 1.21.3 regression (full re-read per tick) would fail CI. What is still missing: any elapsed-time or read budget at real sizes, any test of HubService.tick (service.test.ts has no tick or timer test), and any cover for devices.sync or the launch-time drive.sync at service.ts:49 and :92. Severity lowered from high: this is a diagnosability gap, not a defect that itself causes the freeze, though it is the reason the reported freeze cannot be attributed from recorded data.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read-only summary of ~\.coding-hub\host-health.json: 100 events (the cap) covering 2026-11-14 to 2026-11-18 only: cli-started 42, cli-exited 42, host-start 6, host-stop 6, auto-memory-notice 3, child-process-gone 1. No stall or delay event type exists. Grep of electron/, src/, shared/, phone/ for monitorEventLoopDelay, eventLoopUtilization, 'unresponsive', did-become-responsive, PerformanceObserver, longtask: 0 matches.

#### Release publishes a Windows installer that was never started, and does not check the release candidate

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said high). Category: build-release. ID: `build-tests-docs-release-gate`
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:97`
- **What the code does:** The windows job runs npm ci, npm test and dist:win (lines 33-41); dist:win only verifies files statically (verify-windows-package.cjs). publish needs only [windows, mac] (96-97). No step reads the windows-release.yml result. The Mac job runs packaged-smoke, but packaged-smoke.cjs:14 writes extensionless shebang fixtures, which Windows discovery never picks.
- **What goes wrong:** A tag is pushed on a commit where the release candidate was not run, failed, or ran on another commit. Setup.exe is published and offered to every Windows install. A published version is never replaced, so the repair is another release.
- **Suggested fix:** In publish (permissions: actions: read) fail unless `gh run list --workflow windows-release.yml --commit "$GITHUB_SHA" --status success` returns a run. Give packaged-smoke.cjs the .cmd fixture shims desktop-smoke uses and run it on the staged win-unpacked\Trace.exe in the windows job.
- **Checker's notes:** Read release.yml in full. The windows job (lines 21-50) runs only npm ci, npm test and dist:win; dist:win is build + scripts/build-windows.cjs, which runs verify-windows-package.cjs (static checks: PE headers, files present, sha512 of the installer; its own PASS line says 'Windows execution still requires a native Windows check'). publish (95-97) needs only [windows, mac] and no step queries windows-release.yml. The published Setup.exe is a fresh build that is never started; the release candidate tests a different build of the same commit, and only if someone ran it. packaged-smoke.cjs:14 writes extensionless shebang fixtures and discovery.ts:149-150 only accepts .exe/.com/.cmd/.bat on Windows, so that smoke cannot run on Windows as written. Severity lowered from high: the gap is known and documented as a manual step (AGENTS.md releasing step 2 'Release doesn't check this', windows-releases.md:39, release.yml:3), and it was left manual on purpose because of Windows runner cost. It remains a real risk for a one-person release process: docs/RELEASE_NOTES.md for 1.21.3 itself lists 'the installed app on Windows' as not tried. I did not query GitHub to see whether the candidate passed on 6b0e9b9 (no project data sent outside).

#### Published builds are unsigned, so updates are trusted on the feed alone, and the workflow says nothing

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: security. ID: `build-tests-docs-unsigned-updates`
- **Where:** `electron-builder.cjs:25`
- **What the code does:** The installed 1.22.0 Trace.exe reports Get-AuthenticodeSignature Status NotSigned, and its resources/app-update.yml has no publisherName. electron-builder.cjs:25,37,40 sign only when CSC_LINK is set. release.yml:36-41 passes an empty secret with no warning (the Mac step warns, line 74). README.md:19-20 says both platforms are unsigned.
- **What goes wrong:** Whoever holds RELEASES_TOKEN or the GitHub account can publish a release that installs accept on a sha512 from the same feed; a linked computer can start the install with nobody present (update-service.ts:89). Later, one signed release followed by an unsigned one leaves installs refusing every update.
- **Suggested fix:** Sign Windows releases. In release.yml emit a warning when the certificate secret is empty, fail when vars.WINDOWS_PUBLISHER_NAME is set but the certificate is missing, and run verify-windows-signatures.cjs when signing. Do the same for a missing MAC_CERTIFICATE once a signed Mac release exists.
- **Checker's notes:** Facts hold. electron-builder.cjs:25 and :40 sign only when CSC_LINK is set, :37 adds publisherName only with WINDOWS_PUBLISHER_NAME; release.yml:35-41 passes the secrets with no warning when empty, while the Mac step warns at :74. Also true and not stated by the auditor: release.yml runs dist:win, never dist:win:signed, so verify-windows-signatures.cjs (build-windows.cjs:28, only with --signed) would not run in Release even with a certificate. remoteInstall exists at update-service.ts:89 (called from main.ts:296) and needs an already linked computer and no running channels. Severity lowered: this is a deliberate, documented state (README.md:19 'the installer isn't code-signed yet'; windows-releases.md:22-24 says hashes alone do not authenticate a publisher), and the stated attacker already controls the GitHub account or the release token; with the certificate held as a secret in the same repository, signing would add little against that attacker. The 'signed then unsigned' lock-out is a real property of electron-updater but hypothetical here, since no signed Windows release exists and an expired certificate would fail the build (forceCodeSigning) rather than publish unsigned; only deleting the secret would trigger it.
- **Checker's measurements:** Installed ~\AppData\Local\Programs\AI Coding Hub\Trace\Trace.exe: Get-AuthenticodeSignature Status NotSigned, ProductVersion 1.22.0.0, no signer. 'Uninstall Trace.exe' also NotSigned. resources\app-update.yml holds owner, repo, provider, releaseType, updaterCacheDirName only: no publisherName.

#### Memory server, real PTY and the Windows screen service are never tested before release

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: test-gap. ID: `build-tests-docs-ci-coverage`
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:34`
- **What the code does:** ci.yml runs on Linux only (lines 14-38). test:memory is in no workflow; test:pty runs only in release.yml:64 on the Mac. windows-release.yml:55-65 omits the remote, screen and screen-service smokes, and screen-service-smoke.cjs:11 skips everywhere but Windows, so it runs nowhere. electron/main.ts (892 lines), phone/main.ts (691) and src/runtime/terminals.ts have no unit tests.
- **What goes wrong:** A change to memory-server.ts, ConPTY handling or the Windows screen service passes CI and the release candidate. The person finds it after updating: memory tools fail in every channel, or Share this screen stops working.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `npm run test:memory` and `npm run test:pty` to ci.yml (neither needs a display). Add screen-service-smoke and remote-smoke to windows-release.yml. Move prepareLaunch's argument building out of main.ts into a module with unit tests.
- **Checker's notes:** Mostly holds, with one overstated part. True: ci.yml is Linux only; test:memory appears in no workflow and no vitest file imports memory-server.ts, so the stdio MCP wrapper (96 lines) is covered only by a script nobody's workflow runs; screen-service-smoke.cjs:11 exits on anything but Windows and no workflow runs it, so it runs nowhere automatically; windows-release.yml:55-65 has no remote or screen smoke; there is no main.test.ts, phone/main test or terminals test. Overstated: 'real PTY never tested before release'. release.yml:64 runs test:pty on the Mac job that publish depends on, and on Windows the release candidate's desktop-smoke and windows-install-smoke start fixture CLIs in real ConPTYs (install smoke check 'installed-preload-pty-usage-history-rules-memory'), when that candidate is run. Memory storage logic (memory.ts, auto-memory.ts, DriveMirror) is covered by vitest. The gaps are also documented as deliberate in docs/development.md:68 ('seven smokes (not remote)') and :72 ('test:pty and test:memory don't run in CI ... Run them yourself'). Severity lowered to low: a latent test gap with partial cover, cheap to close (test:memory needs no display).
- **Checker's measurements:** wc -l: electron/main.ts 892, phone/main.ts 691, src/runtime/terminals.ts 262, electron/services/hub/memory-server.ts 96. Grep of .github/workflows for memory, pty, screen, remote: only ci.yml:34 (remote, screen smokes on Linux), release.yml:64 (test:pty on the Mac) and release.yml:85 (packaged-smoke). No test file imports memory-server.ts.

#### Smoke scripts' cleanup can replace the real failure with EBUSY, or fail a passed run

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: test-gap. ID: `build-tests-docs-smoke-cleanup-masks-failure`
- **Where:** `scripts/desktop-smoke.cjs:533`
- **What the code does:** The catch rethrows (line 530), then finally awaits rm(root, { recursive, force }) with no retries (533). Same shape in remote-smoke.cjs:277, enterprise-smoke.cjs:123, productivity-smoke.cjs:89, profiles-smoke.cjs:104, workbench-smoke.cjs:115, design-smoke.cjs:73, models-stability-smoke.cjs:55. Only paste-smoke.cjs:91 and windows-install-smoke.cjs:117 pass maxRetries.
- **What goes wrong:** On Windows a child process still holds the temp folder when the app closes. rm throws EBUSY, which replaces the assertion error (it is never printed) or turns a PASS into exit 1. docs/troubleshooting.md:67 describes this EBUSY after a timeout.
- **Suggested fix:** Add one helper in scripts/lib (rm with maxRetries: 10, retryDelay: 500, failure logged and not thrown) and use it in every smoke script. Print the caught error before cleanup starts.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described. desktop-smoke.cjs:503-530 prints visible state and probes but never prints the caught error, rethrows at :530, and finally (:531-534) awaits rm with no retries and no catch; the outer .catch at :535 then prints only whichever error survived. The same unguarded rm is at remote-smoke.cjs:277, enterprise-smoke.cjs:123, productivity-smoke.cjs:89, profiles-smoke.cjs:104, workbench-smoke.cjs:115, design-smoke.cjs:73, models-stability-smoke.cjs:55, plus packaged-smoke.cjs:32, memory-smoke.cjs:55 and pty-smoke.cjs:54 which the auditor did not list. Several of these also call app.close() without a catch, which can mask the same way. paste-smoke.cjs:91 and windows-install-smoke.cjs:117 retry; screen-smoke, screen-service-smoke and gpu-crash-smoke swallow the rm error. The mechanism is reproduced above; what I could not establish is how often a child still holds the folder after app.close() in a real run. The known desktop-smoke failure at line 277 on this computer was reported with its assertion line, so masking is not what happened there. Severity lowered to low: developer and CI tooling only, no effect on the installed app.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch test on this computer (Node 24): with a child process whose working folder is the temp folder, fs.promises.rm(dir,{recursive:true,force:true}) failed with EBUSY in 1 ms; with maxRetries:10, retryDelay:500 it succeeded after 5,021 ms, once the child had exited. A throw inside finally replaced the original error: only 'EBUSY' surfaced, 'ASSERTION' was lost.

#### Five unguarded symbolic-link tests keep npm test red on this computer

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: test-gap. ID: `build-tests-docs-symlink-tests`
- **Where:** `electron/services/hub/hub.test.ts:39`
- **What the code does:** fs.symlinkSync runs with no platform or privilege guard at hub.test.ts:39, rules.test.ts:37 and :50, claude-profiles.test.ts:38 and pty-manager.test.ts:163. Other tests use skipIf (repository.test.ts:51). docs/troubleshooting.md:63 tells people to expect about four EPERM failures.
- **What goes wrong:** npm test exits non-zero on every run here, so its exit status cannot gate anything and a real fifth failure sits among four expected ones. The link-refusal assertions after each symlink call never run on this computer.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a canSymlink probe (try one link in a temp folder) and use it.skipIf(!canSymlink). Move assertions that need no link into their own tests so they still run.
- **Checker's notes:** Holds, but the count is four, not five. Unguarded symlinkSync calls: hub.test.ts:39, rules.test.ts:37, rules.test.ts:50, pty-manager.test.ts:163. The fifth citation is wrong: claude-profiles.test.ts:38 sits inside `if (process.platform !== 'win32')` at line 37, so it does not run on Windows (hub.test.ts:333 likewise returns early on win32). Four matches what AGENTS.md and docs/troubleshooting.md:63 already state ('about four'), so this is a known, documented condition, not a new defect. The real costs are as the auditor says: npm test cannot exit 0 on this computer, and assertions after the failing call never run here, for example rules.test.ts:37's checks that a broken shared-rules.json is preserved and that oversized payloads are refused, which need no link. pty-manager.test.ts:163 also leaks its temp folder because the link is created before the try. Severity lowered to low: tests only, they pass on Linux CI and on the elevated Windows runner.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch test on this computer: fs.symlinkSync failed with EPERM for a file target, a directory target and a missing target (3 of 3), so Developer Mode is off and the session is not elevated.

#### Docs contradict the code in several places and say nothing about freezes

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: docs. ID: `build-tests-docs-docs-drift`
- **Where:** `docs/architecture.md:152`
- **What the code does:** architecture.md:152 says Macs only open the downloads page (update-service.ts:160 enables signed Macs); :184 calls the phone app framework-free (phone/chat.tsx and screens.tsx import React); :126 lists 2 memory tools (the server has 6); :45, :55, :63 line numbers are 160-250 off; :161 says Tailwind is unused (src/styles.css:1-3). windows-releases.md:60 says no zips without a certificate (electron-builder.cjs:47). development.md:67 omits the screen smoke.
- **What goes wrong:** A new session trusts the docs: it treats Mac self-update as absent, removes Tailwind and loses the base reset, or looks for a freeze entry and finds none (troubleshooting.md and README.md have no entry for the app not responding).
- **Suggested fix:** Correct those lines. Add a troubleshooting entry for 'Trace stops responding for a few seconds' with the 1.21.3 cause, what was left, and how to measure. Document gpu-crash-smoke, design-tour, the real-*-check scripts, test:screen and test:screen-service in development.md.
- **Checker's notes:** Checked each item. Confirmed: architecture.md:152 says Mac only offers the downloads page, while update-service.ts:160-161 enables a Developer ID Mac build (README.md:29 and AGENTS.md agree with the code); architecture.md:11 and :184 call the phone app framework-free, while phone/chat.tsx:2 and phone/screens.tsx:1-2 import React and react-dom; :126 lists read_memory and write_handoff only; the line numbers are stale as measured (the page does warn they are for orientation); windows-releases.md:60 says no zips without MAC_CERTIFICATE, while electron-builder.cjs:47 always builds zips and release.yml:65-67 says so; development.md:67 omits the screen smoke that ci.yml:34 runs, and development.md has no row for test:screen, test:screen-service, gpu-crash-smoke, design-tour or the real-*-check scripts; troubleshooting.md has no entry for the app not responding (the only freeze text is in RELEASE_NOTES.md:15-17). Arguable: architecture.md:161 'Tailwind is installed but effectively unused' is roughly true for utility classes, but src/styles.css:1-3 does pull in Tailwind's base reset, which the page does not say. The failure scenarios are speculative, so real impact is low; it does fall short of the repository's own rule that docs stay current enough for a new session.
- **Checker's measurements:** Line references in docs/architecture.md against electron/main.ts: requestSingleInstanceLock doc 328, actual 490 (off 162); whenReady doc 329, actual 491; before-quit doc 619, actual 869 (off 250); assertSender doc 229, actual 389 (off 160); IPC_CHANNELS doc shared/ipc.ts:108, actual 131. memory-server.ts:29-34 defines 6 tools; architecture.md:126 names 2.

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: test-gap. ID: `build-tests-docs-desktop-smoke-277`
- **Where:** `scripts/desktop-smoke.cjs:280`
- **What the code does:** At b18255b line 277 was the wait for the preview to contain coding-hub-memory after clicking the first .extension-row (now lines 279-280). config.ts:92 lists ~/.agents before project rows. This computer has 25 AWS skills in ~/.agents/skills since 13 Nov. Running the built inventory read-only with the real home gave 31 rows, the first .agents\skills\amazon-bedrock. 6b0e9b9 redirects USERPROFILE (line 52).

HEAD line 52 redirects USERPROFILE on win32 only; HOME is untouched, so a Mac or Linux developer computer with ~/.agents/skills still fails (clean CI runners do not). #### Tests and check scripts leave hundreds of folders and about 280 MB in the temp folder

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: resource-leak. ID: `build-tests-docs-test-leftovers`
- **Where:** `vitest.setup.ts:10`
- **What the code does:** %TEMP% here holds 273 Trace test entries: 184 hub-within-* (pty-manager.test.ts:163 calls symlinkSync before its try), trace-test-backups with 13,653 files, 65 MB (vitest.setup.ts:10, never cleared), 10 hub-screen-service-* at 110 MB (screen-service-smoke.cjs:109 swallows the rm failure), 44 trace-update.* (mac-script-update.ts:67 exits without removing $WORK), 20 trace-chain-/trace-real-* at 104 MB (real-hierarchy-check.cjs:86 and real-round-table-check.cjs:72 never remove root).
- **What goes wrong:** Every npm test run adds files. The real-check folders keep Trace storage and logs from sessions on the person's own logins indefinitely. On a Mac, a failed update from another computer leaves the downloaded disk image in /tmp.
- **Suggested fix:** Move symlinkSync inside the try. Give each vitest run its own backup folder and remove it in a global teardown. Wait for the service to exit before rm in screen-service-smoke. Remove root in the real-check scripts. Add a trap in the update script that removes $WORK on failure.
- **Checker's notes:** Each cause holds. pty-manager.test.ts:163 calls fs.symlinkSync before the try on line 164, so the EPERM on Windows without Developer Mode skips the finally. vitest.setup.ts:10 sets one shared backup root that nothing removes; file-backups.ts:33 trims to 20 per original file, but each test uses a new mkdtemp path so each gets its own folder. screen-service-smoke.cjs:108-109 kills the service and removes root at once with the error swallowed. mac-script-update.ts:67 (and 62-75) exit without removing $WORK; in the Git Bash test /tmp maps to %TEMP%. real-hierarchy-check.cjs:14 and real-round-table-check.cjs:10 create root and never remove it (no rm in either file); those folders hold storage.db, logs, memory, telemetry. Counts differ slightly from the report (37 trace-update, not 44; 9 of the 22 chain/real entries are screenshot folders). Developer-computer disk clutter only; on a Mac /tmp is cleared at restart.
- **Checker's measurements:** %TEMP% now: 185 hub-within-* (under 1 MB), trace-test-backups 13,786 files / 66 MB, 10 hub-screen-service-* / 110 MB, 37 trace-update.* / about 1 MB, 13 trace-chain-* + 9 trace-real-* / 104 MB. Total about 281 MB in 246 top-level entries.

#### 57 of 96 test files are never type-checked

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: test-gap. ID: `build-tests-docs-host-tests-untyped`
- **Where:** `tsconfig.electron.json:14`
- **What the code does:** tsconfig.electron.json:14 excludes **/*.test.ts. tsconfig.json:17 includes only src, shared, phone and the Vite configs. typecheck (package.json:13) runs those two projects, and Vitest strips types without checking. So every electron/**/*.test.ts, scripts/lib/wait.test.ts and vitest.setup.ts are unchecked.
- **What goes wrong:** A service's signature changes and a host test keeps calling the old shape. It still compiles, and passes or fails for the wrong reason, while typecheck stays green. I did not run tsc over the tests, so whether errors exist today is unknown.
- **Suggested fix:** Add tsconfig.test.json (noEmit, include electron/**/*.test.ts, scripts/lib/*.test.ts, vitest.setup.ts) and run it as a third step of `npm run typecheck`.
- **Checker's notes:** tsconfig.electron.json:13-14 includes electron and shared and excludes **/*.test.ts. tsconfig.json:17 includes only src, shared, phone and the two Vite configs. package.json:13 typecheck runs just those two projects, and vite.config.ts:23 sets no Vitest typecheck. So electron tests, scripts/lib/wait.test.ts and vitest.setup.ts are never type-checked; shared tests are (root project has no exclude). The count is 59 of 96, not 57. Whether any type error exists today is unknown: I did not run tsc over the tests because of the no-typecheck limit, so the practical cost is unproven.
- **Checker's measurements:** git ls-files test files: 96 total = 58 electron + 1 scripts + 10 shared + 26 src + 1 phone. Unchecked: 59 (plus vitest.setup.ts).

#### Three known flaky checks have identifiable causes

- **Where:** `electron/services/updates/mac-script-update.test.ts:20`
- **What the code does:** Line 20 runs `bash -n` on a Windows path with whatever bash PATH gives; in PowerShell here that is WindowsApps\bash.exe (WSL). Lines 51-56 choose Git Bash only for the second test. rules.test.ts:56 does 100 read-and-save rounds with no timeout (default 5 s; notes record 5030 ms). remote-smoke.cjs:101-102 clicks buttons in a row that shows only on hover (tile.css:71-72).

The remote smoke fails with '.tile-header intercepts pointer events'. Not run by me; the first is derived from PATH, the last is the least certain.
- **Suggested fix:** Use the resolved Git Bash for line 20 too, or skip without it. Give the rules test { timeout: 30_000 } or fewer rounds. In tileControl focus the button (focus-within keeps the row open) and press Enter instead of hovering and clicking.

(1) Holds: mac-script-update.test.ts:20 runs execFileSync('bash', ['-n', <Windows path>]); from PowerShell the only bash on PATH is the WindowsApps alias (WSL, Ubuntu installed), which cannot open a C:\ path. Memory notes record '1 mac-script bash -n' failing in vitest here. Deterministic from PowerShell, passes from Git Bash; I did not run it (it would start WSL). (2) Wrong at HEAD: rules.test.ts:61 already has `}, 30_000)` with a comment about the 5 s timeout, added in 6b0e9b9. A note records one '.tile-header intercepts pointer events' failure followed by two passes, but what removed the hover (real pointer over the visible window, re-render after Stop) is not established.
