#### Windows and release jobs have no time limit; two workflows use unpinned actions

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: build-release. ID: `build-tests-docs-workflow-hardening`
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/windows-release.yml:47`
- **What the code does:** windows-release.yml (jobs from line 47), release.yml and signed-release.yml set no timeout-minutes; only ci.yml:16 does (30), so the 360-minute default applies. ci.yml:21-22 and windows-release.yml:49, 50, 80, 92 use actions/*@v4 tags, while release.yml and signed-release.yml pin commit SHAs.
- **What goes wrong:** A smoke test hangs on a Windows runner, which bills double: up to 6 hours, 720 billed minutes, for one run. The spending limit then stops every workflow (the case in troubleshooting.md:71).
- **Suggested fix:** Add timeout-minutes: 45 to the Windows and signed jobs and 60 to the release jobs. Pin the actions in ci.yml and windows-release.yml to the SHAs release.yml already uses.
- **Checker's notes:** grep over .github/workflows finds timeout-minutes only at ci.yml:16 (30). windows-release.yml (windows job runs-on at line 47), release.yml (windows line 22, mac line 52, publish line 98) and signed-release.yml (line 11) have none, so GitHub's 360-minute default applies. ci.yml:21-22 and windows-release.yml:49, 50, 80, 92 use @v4 tags, while release.yml and signed-release.yml pin commit SHAs; windows-release.yml reads the signing certificate secrets at lines 68-69 and 75-76, so the unpinned actions there run in a job that holds secrets. The auditor understated one part: release.yml's macos-latest job is also uncapped, and macOS minutes bill at a higher multiplier than Windows. A hang needs a step that never returns; the smoke scripts mostly have 20-30 s Playwright timeouts, so this is a cost guard, not an observed failure.

#### dist:mac deletes whatever folder TRACE_MAC_OUTPUT names, with no check

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: data-loss. ID: `build-tests-docs-mac-output-rm`
- **Where:** `scripts/build-mac.cjs:59`
- **What the code does:** Line 58 resolves TRACE_MAC_OUTPUT against the repository root with no validation. Line 59 runs fs.rmSync(output, { recursive: true, force: true }) before the build.
- **What goes wrong:** On a Mac, `TRACE_MAC_OUTPUT=. npm run dist:mac` deletes the working tree including .git; `..` deletes the parent folder; an absolute path deletes that folder. Uncommitted work is gone before electron-builder starts.
- **Suggested fix:** Refuse an output that is the repository root, an ancestor of it, or outside the repository's release/ folder, unless the folder is empty or holds a marker file the script wrote.
- **Checker's notes:** scripts/build-mac.cjs:58 resolves TRACE_MAC_OUTPUT against the repository root with no check, and line 59 runs fs.rmSync(output, { recursive: true, force: true }) before electron-builder (line 61). '.' resolves to the repository root, '..' to its parent, and an absolute path is taken as is. Guards: line 55 stops the script anywhere but macOS, and the variable is unset by default (release/mac); git grep finds it only in this script and one line of docs/windows-releases.md, and no workflow sets it. So it takes a person on a Mac typing an unusual value. Real but self-inflicted; bottom of low.

#### Electron is two patch trains behind in its major; dev tooling carries 22 advisories

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: build-release. ID: `build-tests-docs-dependencies`
- **Where:** `package.json:71`
- **What the code does:** Both commands ran. `npm audit --omit=dev`: 0 vulnerabilities. `npm audit`: 22 (5 moderate, 17 high), all in build and test tooling (picomatch and tinyglobby through vite, vitest, tailwindcss, electron-builder; follow-redirects; joi). `npm outdated`: electron 44.9.3 installed, 44.11.4 wanted, 45.3.4 latest; electron-updater 6.8.9, wanted 6.8.11. The installed app ships 44.9.3.
- **What goes wrong:** The shipped Chromium lacks the fixes in 44.10 and 44.11 while it renders agent output and the noVNC screen view. The dev advisory count grew from 3 in the October audit (S08) to 22; none ship in the app.
- **Suggested fix:** `npm update electron electron-updater` within the current ranges, rerun the smoke tests and the release candidate, then `npm audit fix` for the non-breaking tooling fixes.
- **Checker's notes:** Version facts hold. package-lock.json and node_modules/electron both give 44.9.3 (package.json:71 allows ^44.9.3), and electron-updater is locked at 6.8.9. The public registry lists 44.11.4 as the newest 44.x and 45.3.4 as latest; electron-updater now has 6.8.12 (the report said 6.8.11). 44.9.3 is about four weeks old, so this is routine upkeep. Not verified: the npm audit counts (0 production, 22 dev), because running npm audit sends the project's dependency tree to the registry, which the limits forbid; and whether 44.10/44.11 contain fixes that matter here (release notes not read). No evidence links this to the 1-3 s stalls. Process note: no handoff was written, since the audit limits forbid writes under ~/.coding-hub.
- **Checker's measurements:** npm view (public package metadata only): electron dist-tags 44-x-y = 44.11.4, latest = 45.3.4; publish dates 44.9.3 2026-10-21, 44.10.0 2026-10-27, 44.11.0 2026-11-10, 44.11.4 2026-11-18 UTC. electron-updater 6.8.x newest 6.8.12.

## Second round (files the first round did not read)

### Second round: shared contracts (12)

#### Hierarchy table: the builder is never told why a finding was reopened

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `SC-01`
- **Where:** `shared/findings.ts:86`
- **What the code does:** findingsFor() adds status and reason only when status !== 'open'. apply() stores the REOPENED reason in finding.check and sets status 'open'. review-chain.ts:225 fixPrompt sends the builder only findingsFor(...,'driver'). Run on the real code after FIXED then REOPENED 'still leaks in the retry path': the line is "F1 (low): t".
- **What goes wrong:** Codex answers "REOPENED F1: still leaks in the retry path". The builder's next fix prompt lists F1 with its original text only. It cannot see Codex's reply (seats are separate chats), so it repeats or guesses the fix, and the last review round is spent.
- **Suggested fix:** In findingsFor, for an open finding with a non-empty check, add it: `, reopened: ${finding.check}` (and the builder's earlier response). Add a test in shared/findings.test.ts for a reopened finding's driver line.
- **Checker's notes:** findings.ts:78 stores the REOPENED reason in finding.check and sets status 'open'; line 86 adds status and reason only when status !== 'open' and never reads check. review-chain.ts:224-227 fixPrompt sends the builder only that list plus the planner's note; turn() (158) sends nothing else. With REVIEW_ROUNDS = 2 the fix after a reopening goes straight to the final review (486-487), so it is never re-checked by Codex. Partial mitigation: finalPrompt (230) gives the planner the reason. The two-seat table relays the reviewer's whole answer, so only the hierarchy is affected.
- **Checker's measurements:** Bundled the real shared/findings.ts with esbuild into the scratch folder. apply(F1,'FIXED','added a guard') then apply(F1,'REOPENED','still leaks in the retry path') gives status open, check 'still leaks in the retry path'. findingsFor([F1],'driver') returns the heading line and "F1 (low): t": no reason, no mark that it was reopened.

#### Pasting more than 4,096 characters into a linked computer's terminal sends nothing, with no message

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: data-loss. ID: `SC-02`
- **Where:** `src/runtime/linked-terminals.ts:49`
- **What the code does:** onData sends the whole string as one 'linked-input' and ends with .catch(() => {}). electron/main.ts:252 allows data max(4096), as does remote-server.ts:151. xterm delivers a paste as one data event (node_modules/@xterm/xterm/src/browser/Clipboard.ts:54). Local terminals split input into 16 KB pieces and report errors (src/runtime/terminals.ts:181-187).
- **What goes wrong:** On a linked computer's tab, paste a 6,000-character log into a Claude Code channel. The request fails validation (INVALID_REQUEST), the error is swallowed, and nothing reaches the CLI. Not run end to end; read from the code.
- **Suggested fix:** Split typed data into pieces of at most 4,096 UTF-16 units (not inside a surrogate pair) in the queue, as writeTerminal does, and show a failure through useLinkedStore addError instead of swallowing it.
- **Checker's notes:** linked-terminals.ts:44-50 sends each xterm data event as one linked-input and ends with .catch(() => {}). main.ts:252 limits data to 4096 and linkedRequest (308) parses it, so handle() answers INVALID_REQUEST (404); workbench() throws and the catch discards it. xterm's paste() (Clipboard.ts:51-55) makes one triggerDataEvent, adding 12 characters when bracketed paste is on. The element's paste listener (63-68) handles images only; LinkedComputerView has no other text path. Nothing is sent, so no data is destroyed: it is a silent failure rather than data loss. Not run in the app.
- **Checker's measurements:** The schema line from main.ts:252 re-typed with the repo's zod: data of 4096 characters passes; 4097 and 6000 fail with 'data: Too big: expected string to have <=4096 characters'. Local terminals split at 16 KB and report errors (terminals.ts:181-190), read in the file. End-to-end paste on a linked tab was not run (Trace must not be driven).

#### Hierarchy table's Models fields save any text as a seat's model, after which the workspace cannot be saved

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `SC-03`
- **Where:** `src/components/chat/ChatParts.tsx:194`
- **What the code does:** setModel passes value.trim() to onModel; ChatView.tsx:119 and :122 call updateConfig with it. No modelValue() check and no maxLength, unlike EngineControls.tsx:54. Run on the real schema: a workspace whose model is "Opus 5.5" fails savedWorkspaceSchema with "Use a model identifier, or leave blank for the CLI default."; modelValue("Opus 5.5") is null.
- **What goes wrong:** The seats are shown as "Fable 5.2" and "Opus 5.5". The person types "Opus 5.5" into Builder under Models. Every later workspace save is refused (workspace-storage.ts:63) with a banner that names no field, and the seat cannot start, until the text is corrected.
- **Suggested fix:** In setModel, use modelValue(value) from shared/schemas.ts and ignore or flag a null result (show the reason beside the field); add maxLength={MAX_MODEL_LENGTH} to the three inputs.
- **Checker's notes:** ChatParts.tsx:194 passes value.trim() to onModel on blur with no modelValue check; ChatView.tsx:119 and :122 call updateConfig, which stores it for a stopped session (workspace-store.ts:246). Each save then fails at workspace-storage.ts:63 and App.tsx:42 shows the message. Inputs are disabled while a seat runs (192), so this needs the channel stopped and text with a space or other disallowed character. Severity lowered: the person must type invalid text, and the banner does say 'Use a model identifier', though not which seat. The close-time flush fails too (first-round finding main-ipc-flush-invalid-waits).
- **Checker's measurements:** Real shared/schemas.ts bundled: modelValue('Opus 5.5') is null. savedWorkspaceSchema on a one-channel workspace passes with model 'claude-opus-5-5' and fails with 'Opus 5.5': 'Use a model identifier, or leave blank for the CLI default.' A 200-character model fails: 'Too big: expected string to have <=160 characters'. startSessionSchema fails with 'config.model: Use a model identifier, or leave blank for the CLI default.'

#### An answer line naming several findings records only the first

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said medium). Category: bug. ID: `SC-04`
- **Where:** `shared/findings.ts:47`
- **What the code does:** ANSWER matches one F<n> per line and the rest becomes the note. Run on the real code: parseAnswers('FIXED F1, F2, F3: escaped all three','driver') gives only F1 with note "F2, F3: escaped all three"; 'VERIFIED F1, F2 and F3' gives only F1; 'FIXED F1-F3: done' gives only F1. parseChallengeAnswers('ACCEPTED C1, C2') gives only C1.
- **What goes wrong:** The builder writes "FIXED F1, F2, F3: same root cause". F2 and F3 stay open, are not offered to the reviewer for checking, and come back in another fix step; the final review record shows fixed work as open. The same loss happens to grouped VERIFIED lines.
- **Suggested fix:** After the verb, read every F<n> (or C<n>) up to the first colon or dash, including ranges, and apply the verb and note to each. Add these lines to shared/findings.test.ts.
- **Checker's notes:** The parser behaves as claimed: ANSWER (line 47) and RESPONSE (93) take one id per line and the rest becomes the note. Callers: review-chain.ts:528 and :464, round-table.ts:302. Unanswered findings stay open and come back in the next fix prompt, or reach the final review as open. The trigger is not established: both prompts tell the agent to answer each finding on its own line (findings.ts:88-89, round-table.ts:110-111), and I have no transcript of an agent grouping ids. The cost is one spent round or a wrong status in the record, hence low.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real parseAnswers: 'FIXED F1, F2, F3: escaped all three' gives only F1 with note 'F2, F3: escaped all three'; 'VERIFIED F1, F2 and F3' gives only F1 (note 'F2 and F3'); 'FIXED F1-F3: done' gives only F1 (note 'F3: done'); 'FIXED F1 and F2: done' gives only F1. parseChallengeAnswers('ACCEPTED C1, C2') gives only C1 with reason 'C2'. Two separate lines give both ids.

#### A chat event of a type this version does not know erases the thread

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `SC-05`
- **Where:** `src/state/chat-store.ts:62`
- **What the code does:** applyChatEvent's switch has no default, so an unknown type returns undefined and update() stores undefined as the thread. linked-computers.ts:24 passes any event type through on purpose ("it may be newer"). Run on the real store: after {type:'some-newer-event'} the thread with one assistant message became undefined, and a later event for that run was ignored.
- **What goes wrong:** A linked computer runs a newer Trace that adds a chat event type (three were added since 26 October). On this computer's tab for it, and on a phone, each such event empties the conversation shown so far. No current version pair was confirmed to do this.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `default: return current;` to the switch, and a test that an unknown event type leaves the thread unchanged.
- **Checker's notes:** The switch at chat-store.ts:62-99 has no default, so update() (18-20) stores undefined for the thread. linked-computers.ts:24 passes any event type through on purpose, and RemoteChat.tsx:17-21 feeds those events to applyChatEvent for linked tabs and the phone. All 13 types in shared/chat.ts are handled today, so it needs a future type plus mixed versions; types were added on 26 October, 7 November and 8 November (git log). One correction: through receiveChat a later event is not ignored; the thread starts again empty and shows only what follows.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real chat-store bundled: after one assistant message, applyChatEvent with {type:'some-newer-event'} leaves threads.s undefined (key present); a later direct notice event is ignored (still undefined). Through receiveChat (copied from RemoteChat.tsx:15-21) the unknown event also leaves the thread undefined, and the next notice event gives a thread whose items are ['notice'] only: the earlier assistant message is gone.

#### Plan-limit warnings repeat when Claude Code words the same reset time differently

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `SC-06`
- **Where:** `electron/services/alerts.ts:30`
- **What the code does:** The once-only key contains window.resets as the CLI wrote it. This computer's usage-alerts.json holds "claude:Work:Week:Nov 4, 10:59am:80" and "claude:Work:Week:Nov 4, 11am:80": one weekly window, warned twice. Reproduced with usageAlerts: the second wording returns the same alert again. A window with no reset text gets a key that never changes.
- **What goes wrong:** /usage reports the weekly reset as "Nov 4, 10:59am" on one check and "Nov 4, 11am" on the next. The 80% notification (and the 100% one) is shown again for the same window. With no reset text, a later window's warning would not be shown at all (trigger not confirmed).
- **Suggested fix:** Key Claude windows by something stable: the reset wording rounded (drop minutes, or parse to the nearest hour), or the account, label and the day the warning was given. Add a test with the two wordings.
- **Checker's notes:** alerts.ts:30 builds the once-only key from window.resets, which for Claude is the CLI's own wording (plan-limits.ts:28-30); main.ts:855-862 keeps the keys in usage-alerts.json. The file on this computer holds both wordings for one account, label and date, so the 80% warning was issued twice for that window. The repo's own test text shows the same drift ('7:59am' and '8am', plan-limits.test.ts:10-11). The no-reset half holds in the function, but I could not confirm that Claude Code ever omits the reset text.
- **Checker's measurements:** Read (not written) ~\.coding-hub\usage-alerts.json: it contains 'claude:Work:Week:Nov 4, 10:59am:80' and 'claude:Work:Week:Nov 4, 11am:80'. Real usageAlerts at 85%: 'Nov 4, 10:59am' returns one alert, the same wording again returns none, 'Nov 4, 11am' returns '85% of the Claude (Work) week limit used' again. With resets null the key is 'claude:Work:Week::100' and a later day's call returns no alert.

#### Finding and answer lines in common Markdown forms are not read, and are dropped without a word

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `SC-07`
- **Where:** `shared/findings.ts:46`
- **What the code does:** The patterns allow only an optional '-' or '*' bullet. Run on the real code, each returns []: '1. FINDING high src/a.ts:12 — leaks', '### FINDING high …', 'FINDING critical …', 'FINDING **high** …', '1. FIXED F1: …', '**FIXED** **F1**: …', '`FIXED F1`: done', '> FIXED F1: done', '### VERIFIED F1'.
- **What goes wrong:** Codex numbers its findings ("1. FINDING high …"). In a discussion table nothing is recorded. In a hierarchy the review stops with "ended without findings"; if only some lines are in these forms, those findings are lost and the review counts as complete.
- **Suggested fix:** Allow a leading list number, heading marks, '>' and backticks, and emphasis around the id and severity. Tell the person in a notice when a line starts with FINDING, FIXED, DECLINED, VERIFIED or REOPENED but could not be read.
- **Checker's notes:** Lines 46-47 allow only an optional '-' or '*' before the keyword. Callers give no notice for unread lines: round-table.ts:297-306 reports only a full record; review-chain.ts:477 stops only when nothing at all was read (so that case is not silent, but the note is misleading), and with a mix the unread findings are lost. Not established: how often the agents write these forms; no real reviewer answer was checked. The prompt (round-table.ts:111, review-chain.ts:222) asks for the plain form.
- **Checker's measurements:** Bundled shared/findings.ts with esbuild and ran it. parseFindings returned [] for '1. FINDING high src/a.ts:12 — leaks', '### FINDING high …', 'FINDING critical …', 'FINDING **high** …', '> FINDING high …', '`FINDING high …`'. parseAnswers returned [] for '1. FIXED F1: done', '**FIXED** **F1**: done', '`FIXED F1`: done', '> FIXED F1: done', '### VERIFIED F1'. Extra: '**FINDING high** src/a.ts:12 — leaks' gives evidence '' and text '** src/a.ts:12 — leaks'.

#### Finding severity is matched without a word boundary, and a dash after it hides the evidence

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `SC-08`
- **Where:** `shared/findings.ts:46`
- **What the code does:** SEVERITY is followed by \s* with no boundary, and only [:,-] may follow it. Run on the real code: 'FINDING: Higher-order component leaks listeners' gives severity high, text "er-order component leaks listeners"; 'FINDING: Lowercase header names…' gives low, "ercase header names…"; 'FINDING high — src/a.ts:12 — leaks the token' gives evidence '' and text "— src/a.ts:12 — leaks the token".
- **What goes wrong:** A reviewer leaves out the severity and starts with "Higher", "Highlight" or "Lower": the finding is recorded with an invented severity and cut text. A reviewer writing an em dash after the severity gets a finding with no file reference.
- **Suggested fix:** Require a boundary after the severity (\b) and accept [:,—–-]* after it, as the CHALLENGE pattern at line 92 already does. Add both cases to shared/findings.test.ts.
- **Checker's notes:** SEVERITY at line 45 is followed by \s* with no boundary, and line 46 accepts only [:,-] after it, while split() at line 50 needs a path before the dash. Both outputs reproduce. Impact is small: the em dash case still records the finding with its whole text (only the evidence field is empty), and the 'Higher' case needs a reviewer who left out the severity, against the prompt. CHALLENGE (line 92) has the same missing boundary. No real reviewer answer was checked.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real code: 'FINDING: Higher-order component leaks listeners' gives {severity:'high', evidence:'', text:'er-order component leaks listeners'}; 'FINDING: Lowercase header names…' gives low, 'ercase header names…'; 'FINDING Mediumship' gives medium, 'ship'; 'FINDING high — src/a.ts:12 — leaks the token' gives evidence '', text '— src/a.ts:12 — leaks the token'; with a hyphen instead, evidence 'src/a.ts:12', text 'leaks'. 'CHALLENGE Higher risk than stated' gives high, 'er risk than stated'.

#### A plan limit whose reset time has passed shows "resets in 1 min"

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `SC-10`
- **Where:** `src/state/plan-limits-store.ts:21`
- **What the code does:** resetText returns `in ${Math.max(1, minutes)} min` for every minutes < 60, negative ones included, and treats any string Date.parse accepts as a time. The same lines run in Node: a reset of 2026-11-15T23:12:54Z seen on 18 November gives "in 1 min"; 'Nov 4' (V8 reads it as 2001) gives "in 1 min". alerts.ts:30 instead tests /^\d{4}-/.
- **What goes wrong:** Codex reports a window whose resetsAt is already past (an idle account), or the last reading is older than the reset: Usage and the status bar tooltip say "resets in 1 min" for as long as that value stays. Whether Codex returns past times was not confirmed.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat only ISO strings (/^\d{4}-/) as times, as alerts.ts does, and return null (or "reset due") when the time is not in the future.
- **Checker's notes:** Line 21 returns 'in 1 min' for zero and negative minutes. Established by the code: readings are cached five minutes (plan-limits.ts:77, StatusBar.tsx:36), so a window whose reset passed since the last check shows 'resets in 1 min' until the next check. Not established: that Codex returns past times for an idle account, or that Claude prints a date without a time (the fixture forms all have 'at <time>' and are shown as written). So the lasting case is unproven; the short one is real and cosmetic.
- **Checker's measurements:** Lines 17-24 retyped and run in Node 24 with now = 2026-11-18T12:00Z: resets '2026-11-15T23:12:54.000Z' gives 'in 1 min'; 'Nov 4', 'Nov 21', 'Dec 1' parse as year 2001 and give 'in 1 min'. 'Oct 31 at 7:59am', 'Nov 18 at 3pm', '3pm', '11:59pm' give NaN and come back as written.

#### Codex's rate-limit reply is used unchecked: a non-numeric reset time throws in the main process

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `SC-11`
- **Where:** `shared/plan-limits.ts:39`
- **What the code does:** codexWindow calls new Date(resetsAt * 1000).toISOString() on whatever the app server sent (electron/services/hub/plan-limits.ts:46-47 only casts the JSON). Run on the real code: resetsAt '2026-11-18T18:26:48Z' throws "RangeError: Invalid time value"; a missing usedPercent comes back undefined; two windows of 300 and 60 minutes are both labelled "Session".
- **What goes wrong:** A Codex update sends resetsAt as a string or renames usedPercent. The throw happens inside the child's stdout handler, outside any try, on every 5-minute check; or the status bar shows NaN%. No such Codex version was seen: the trigger is unconfirmed.
- **Suggested fix:** Parse rateLimits with a small zod schema (finite numbers, usedPercent 0 to 1000) before codexWindow, and report 'Codex did not report usage limits.' when it fails. Give equal labels a suffix.

The 30-second timer at line 34 still settles the read. But the trigger is a Codex reply shape that nobody has seen: the test fixture and the type show numeric seconds, and I did not query the installed Codex. This is hardening against a possible future change, not a defect that occurs today.

A numeric string ('1793536297') does not throw.

#### A full review record can give a new finding the id of the one it just dropped

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `SC-12`
- **Where:** `shared/findings.ts:37`
- **What the code does:** roomForFinding removes the first verified finding; nextFindingId is one past the highest id left. Run on the real code with F1 to F50 where only F50 is verified: roomForFinding returns true, 49 remain ending at F49, and nextFindingId returns "F50" again. The comment at line 40 says ids never repeat.
- **What goes wrong:** With 50 findings recorded and the newest one the only verified one, the reviewer's next finding becomes a second F50. The agents' conversations hold both, so a later "VERIFIED F50" or a memory note about F50 can refer to either.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the highest number ever given with the record (or compute the next id before removing the verified finding and pass it on), so a dropped id is not given out again.
- **Checker's notes:** Line 37 removes the first verified finding and line 39 counts from the highest id left; both callers (round-table.ts:298-299, review-chain.ts:524-525) call them in that order with no guard, so the comment at line 40 is wrong for this case. It needs a narrow state: a full record of 50 where the only verified finding is the highest-numbered one. The record itself stays consistent (the old F50 is gone, so lookups by id find only the new one); the confusion is limited to the agents' conversations.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real code, F1 to F50 with only F50 verified: roomForFinding returns true, 49 remain, last id F49, nextFindingId returns 'F50'. With only F10 verified: next is F51. With F49 and F50 verified: F49 is dropped, next is F51.

### Second round: shared pure logic (12)

Lines 71-72 replace the shared rules and save the verification profiles, then line 76 throws; the window shows INVALID_REQUEST and no workspace is applied.
- **Suggested fix:** Remap roundTable.partner and roundTable.planner through the same `ids` map (or drop roundTable, chain, findings and waiting when the template is saved), and run savedWorkspaceSchema.parse before rules.save and verification-save. Add a productivity.test.ts case with a Round Table template (none exists: 0 mentions of roundTable there).

Lines 71-72 run first, even with applyRules false; each retry adds another copy of every check (verification.ts:68 new id).

Lead + hidden reviewer, and hierarchy (reviewer + planner): save accepted, apply THREW ZodError [': Each channel must have exactly one grid tile.'], after writes ['rules.save:TEMPLATE RULES','verification-save:Check']. Table with findings only (partner gone): apply OK.

#### FINDING and CHALLENGE lines in a numbered list, quote or heading are not read, and are dropped silently when the reply also holds an answer line

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `shared-logic-findings-numbered-dropped`
- **Where:** `shared/findings.ts:46`
- **What the code does:** FINDING (line 46), ANSWER (47) and CHALLENGE (92) allow only an optional '-' or '*' bullet. Scratch run: parseFindings('1. FINDING high a.ts:1 — x') => []; same for '> FINDING' and '### FINDING'. A reply 'VERIFIED F1\n1. FINDING high src/a.ts:12 — leaks\n2. FINDING medium …' gives findings [] and answers [VERIFIED F1].
- **What goes wrong:** In review round 2 the reviewer verifies F1 and numbers two new findings. isReview (review-chain.ts:68) is true because of the VERIFIED line, so the chain goes on with no new findings recorded. Not observed here: 0 numbered forms against 127 plain FINDING lines in 20 Codex sessions on this computer.
- **Suggested fix:** Accept an optional list or quote marker in all four patterns, for example `^\s*(?:(?:[-*>]|\d+[.)]|#{1,6})\s*)?`, and add test cases for '1. FINDING', '> FINDING' and a reply that mixes VERIFIED lines with numbered findings.
- **Checker's notes:** The patterns are as described and the mixed case does pass review-chain.ts:477 with nothing recorded (record(), line 523). But the trigger is a reviewer ignoring the fixed form the prompt gives (review-chain.ts:222), not seen in any local session. A numbered-only reply is caught (chain stops), the planner's final review still follows, and the text stays in the chat. In a plain Round Table (round-table.ts:297) there is no gate at all.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch run of the real functions: '1. FINDING high a.ts:1 — x', '1) …', '> …', '### …' => []; '1. VERIFIED F1', '1. CHALLENGE high — x', '1. ACCEPTED C1' => []. Mixed reply: findings [], answers [VERIFIED F1], isReview true; numbered-only reply: isReview false. Local Codex agent messages: 62 parsed FINDING lines, 0 numbered, quoted or heading forms.

#### Bold markers around FINDING, FIXED or ACCEPTED lines lose the evidence and leave '** ' in the saved text

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `shared-logic-findings-bold-markers`
- **Where:** `shared/findings.ts:46`
- **What the code does:** The patterns allow '**' only directly around the keyword. Scratch run: '**FINDING high** src/a.ts:12 — leaks the token' => evidence '', text '** src/a.ts:12 — leaks the token'; '**FIXED F1:** escaped the output' => note '** escaped the output'; 'FINDING high — the problem' => text '— the problem'; 'FIXED F1 — escaped' => note '— escaped'.
- **What goes wrong:** The reviewer bolds the keyword and severity together. The finding is recorded without its file reference and its text starts with '** '; that text is shown in the record, sent back to the driver by findingsFor, and saved as an open item. Not observed in this computer's Codex sessions (0 bold forms).
- **Suggested fix:** Strip '*', '_' and backticks after the severity or id before splitting (as taskSignal does for its line), and let FINDING and ANSWER skip em and en dashes after the severity or id the way CHALLENGE and RESPONSE already do (`[:,.—–-]*`). Add the bold forms to findings.test.ts.
- **Checker's notes:** Outputs match the claim exactly; FINDING (line 46) and ANSWER (47) lack the em and en dash that CHALLENGE and RESPONSE (92-93) accept, and none strips emphasis after the severity or id. Impact is a lost file reference and stray characters in the record, not a lost finding. The trigger depends on how the model formats its line and was not seen in this computer's sessions, so it is not established.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch run: '**FINDING high** src/a.ts:12 — leaks the token' => evidence '', text '** src/a.ts:12 — leaks the token'; '**FIXED F1:** escaped the output' => note '** escaped the output'; 'FINDING high — the problem' => text '— the problem'; 'FIXED F1 — escaped' => note '— escaped'; 'FINDING **high** …' => []; '**CHALLENGE high** — x' => text '** — x'. Local Codex messages: 0 bold or dash-without-path forms in 62.

#### Shift, Ctrl or Alt with F3 is taken for a cursor-position reply and never reaches a linked computer's channel

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `shared-logic-shift-f3-dropped`
- **Where:** `shared/terminal-input.ts:5`
- **What the code does:** TERMINAL_REPLIES starts with `\x1b\[\??[\d;]*R`. xterm 6.0.0 sends ESC[1;<mod>R for F3 with a modifier (node_modules/@xterm/xterm/src/common/input/Keyboard.ts:236-238). Scratch run: Shift+F3 '\x1b[1;2R' => isPersonInput false, withoutTerminalReplies ''. Same for Ctrl+F3 and Alt+F3. All real xterm replies I listed from its source are matched.
- **What goes wrong:** In a linked computer's terminal the person presses Shift+F3 (find previous in several terminal programs). src/runtime/linked-terminals.ts:47-48 gets an empty string and sends nothing. In a local or phone channel the key is delivered but does not clear the 'needs you' mark (pty-manager.ts:156, terminals.ts:194).
- **Suggested fix:** Do not treat ESC[1;2R to ESC[1;8R as a reply: a cursor report in row 1 at columns 2-8 is the only real collision, so exclude `\x1b\[1;[2-8]R` from the pattern, or filter replies in the linked view by xterm's own distinction (onData for replies arrives without a key event). Add a test for the modified F3 keys.
- **Checker's notes:** linked-terminals.ts:47-48 returns when the filtered string is empty, so the key is never sent; the custom key handler (lines 53-60) lets these keys through to xterm, and no menu accelerator uses F3. Reachable by one key press, for example PowerShell's Shift+F3 (search backward for a character). Locally the key is written and only the 'needs you' mark stays (terminals.ts:194, pty-manager.ts:156). Not pressed in the running app.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch run: Shift+F3 '\x1b[1;2R', Alt+F3 '\x1b[1;3R', Ctrl+F3 '\x1b[1;5R', Ctrl+Shift+F3 '\x1b[1;6R' => isPersonInput false, withoutTerminalReplies ''. Plain F3 '\x1bOR' and Shift+F1/F2/F4/F5 pass through. xterm 6.0.0 Keyboard.ts:52 and 236-238 build ESC[1;(modifiers+1)R for key code 114.

#### A session summary lists up to 20 commits but only the first 10 are checked, with no word about the rest

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `shared-logic-commit-refs-ten`
- **Where:** `shared/commit-refs.ts:17`
- **What the code does:** commitRefs returns `[...out].slice(0, MAX_REFS)` (10). auto-memory.ts:328 lists `git log -n 20`. Scratch run on a 20-commit summary: 10 refs, abc1000 to abc1009. describeCommits (line 26-28) adds no 'and N more'. Read-only count of this computer's notes: 6 of 147 session summaries list more than 10 commits.
- **What goes wrong:** A long session makes 15 commits. Shared memory and read_memory show 'Checked … : <10 hashes> on origin/main' for the newest ten. The person or the next agent reads that as the whole session being pushed, while the five oldest were never checked.
- **Suggested fix:** Either raise MAX_REFS to the 20 the summary can list, or have commitRefs report how many it left out and describeCommits end with '; N more not checked'. Add a test with a 20-commit summary.
- **Checker's notes:** Code is as described (line 17; commit-status.ts:20 caps again). The stated harm does not hold, though: auto-memory.ts:328 lists `head..HEAD` newest first, so every listed commit is an ancestor of the first, and commit-status.ts:22 uses `git branch -r --contains`. If the newest are on a remote branch, the unchecked older ones are too. What remains is an incomplete line (older commits may be pushed while the ten shown are not), and the note still lists all hashes.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch run on a 20-commit summary: 10 refs, abc1000 to abc1009; describeCommits lists those ten with no note of more. Read-only count of local notes: 13 summaries have a commits list, 6 of them with more than 10 lines.

#### Removing a Gemini tile on load leaves an empty gap in the grid

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `shared-logic-migration-layout-hole`
- **Where:** `shared/workspace-migration.ts:24`
- **What the code does:** Line 24 only filters the saved tiles. Scratch run on shared/workspace-120.fixture.ts: migrated tiles = [{sessionId:'lead', x:0, w:4, …}] in layoutMode 'columns'. hydrate keeps saved tiles unless a seated one is present (src/state/workspace-store.ts:176), fitTiles returns them as they are, and the grid compacts only vertically (SessionGrid.tsx:41). Not run in the app.
- **What goes wrong:** A workspace saved by 1.19 or 1.20 with Claude, Gemini and Codex side by side is opened: Claude and Codex keep a third of the width each and the middle third stays blank until the person picks a layout. Only matters for saves and templates from those versions.
- **Suggested fix:** When removedChannels > 0, return no tiles from migrateWorkspace and let hydrate call arrangeTiles(sessions, layoutMode), or arrange in hydrate whenever the migration removed a tiled channel. Extend the fixture test to assert the remaining tiles fill the row.
- **Checker's notes:** Line 24 only filters. hydrate (workspace-store.ts:176) lays out again only when a seated session has a tile; fitTiles (95-99) keeps tiles whose count matches, so the gap is also saved; SessionGrid.tsx:41 compacts vertically only. No other caller rearranges after load. Reachable only from a workspace, backup or template saved by 1.19 or 1.20 with a Gemini tile, and one click on a layout fixes it. Not seen in the running app.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch run: fixture => tiles [{lead, x:0, w:4}], layout 'columns', valid. Claude, Gemini, Codex side by side => [{a, x:0, w:4},{c, x:8, w:4}]. react-grid-layout 1.5.4 compact(…,'vertical',12) on those tiles returns x 0 and x 8 unchanged.

#### The diagnostic export hides the home and project folder only when they are spelled exactly as given

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: security. ID: `shared-logic-redact-spellings`
- **Where:** `shared/productivity.ts:47`
- **What the code does:** redactDiagnostic replaces the literal `project` and `home` strings. Scratch run: message 'D:\\clients\\acme-corp' with project 'D:\\clients\\acme-corp\\' => unchanged; 'C:/Users/kg/AppData/…' with home 'C:\\Users\\kg' => unchanged; '/private/var/folders/x/proj' with project '/var/folders/x/proj' => '/private<project>'; 'C:\\Users\\kgeary\\x' => '~eary\\x'.
- **What goes wrong:** The channel's folder is reached through a junction or symbolic link, or is stored with a trailing separator. diagnostics.ts:29-30 reports fs.realpathSync of it, main.ts:720 redacts with the unresolved request.directory, so the exported report names the real folder (a client or project name) although the export promises paths are hidden.
- **Suggested fix:** Redact with both the given and the resolved folder (pass `cwd` as well), compare with separators normalised to one form and trailing separators removed, and require a separator or end of string after the match so 'kg' does not match 'kgeary'. Add these four cases to the tests.
- **Checker's notes:** productivity.ts:47 replaces only the literal home and project strings (case-insensitive). main.ts:720 passes the unresolved request.directory, while diagnostics.ts:29-30 puts directory() = fs.realpathSync (local-store.ts:21) into the 'Working directory' step. One correction: the export sends the workspace root (WorkflowCenter.tsx:45-46), not a channel's folder. Needs a root reached through a junction or symbolic link; the trailing-separator and forward-slash cases were not shown reachable from the folder picker. The file is saved locally by the person.
- **Checker's measurements:** All four claimed outputs reproduced: 'D:\\clients\\acme-corp' unchanged with project ending in a separator; 'C:/Users/kg/AppData/x' unchanged; '/private<project>'; '~eary\\x'. Junction test in scratch: realpathSync(link) ends in 'real-client-acme'; redactDiagnostic of that step message with project=link still contains 'real-client-acme' (only the home part became '~').

#### Recovery advice is chosen by loose substrings of the CLI's last output, so unrelated words pick the wrong advice

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `shared-logic-recovery-advice-substrings`
- **Where:** `shared/productivity.ts:39`
- **What the code does:** Lines 39-42 test unanchored patterns (auth, 401, 429, json, model, rules, 'no such file or directory') on session.error, which is 'CLI exited with code N.' plus the terminal's tail (pty-manager.ts:465). Scratch run: '…The author field is missing…' and '…port 14290' => account advice; '…bash: jq: No such file or directory' => 'Choose an existing project folder'; '…Updated AGENTS.md then crashed: out of memory' => 'Apply current shared rules'.
- **What goes wrong:** A channel exits after printing ordinary output that contains 'author', a number with 401 or 429 in it, or a missing-command message. Workflow → Recovery tells the person to review accounts, re-apply rules or choose another folder, none of which is the cause.
- **Suggested fix:** Use word boundaries and more specific phrases (`\b(401|429)\b`, `\bauth(entication|orization)?\b`, 'not logged in'), match the directory case only on the host's own launch error codes, and add the misleading examples above to productivity.test.ts.
- **Checker's notes:** productivity.ts:39-42 test unanchored patterns on the whole message. WorkflowCenter.tsx:27 passes session.error, which workspace-store.ts:205 sets from the exit diagnostic; pty-manager.ts:461-465 builds that as 'CLI exited with code N.' plus up to 2000 characters of terminal tail. So ordinary output of a failed channel decides the advice title and the view 'Open suggested setup' opens. No guard found. Impact is a wrong hint only; the raw diagnostic is shown beneath it.
- **Checker's measurements:** '…The author field is missing in package.json' and '…listening on port 14290' => 'Review the CLI account and provider limits'; '…bash: jq: No such file or directory' => 'Choose an existing project folder'; '…Updated AGENTS.md then crashed: out of memory' => 'Apply current shared rules'. Control: '…Cannot find module x' => 'Investigate CLI exit 1'.

#### Severity is matched without a word boundary and answer keywords in any case, so ordinary sentences change the review record

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `shared-logic-findings-prose-misread`
- **Where:** `shared/findings.ts:45`
- **What the code does:** SEVERITY (line 45) has no boundary after it and ANSWER (line 47) has the `i` flag, unlike FINDING. Scratch run: 'FINDING lower bound is off by one in src/a.ts:3' => severity low, text 'er bound is off…'; 'CHALLENGE lowercase names…' => 'ercase names…'; reviewer line 'Verified F3 still fails on Windows' => VERIFIED F3; 'FIXED F1, F2, F3: renamed' => only F1.
- **What goes wrong:** The reviewer writes 'Verified F3 still fails on Windows' as a sentence: F3 becomes verified and leaves the list of findings waiting, though the reviewer said it still fails. Or a finding written without a severity word loses the start of its text. Not observed in this computer's sessions.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `\b` after the severity group, drop the `i` flag from ANSWER and RESPONSE (as the comment on line 44 intends for FINDING), and read every id on a line such as 'FIXED F1, F2, F3: …'. Add these lines to the 'only the fixed forms count' test.
- **Checker's notes:** Regexes at findings.ts:45-47 behave as claimed, and review-chain.ts:523-528 and round-table.ts:297-303 apply the results. But agents are told the fixed forms (round-table.ts:111, review-chain.ts:222), role filtering blocks cross-role verbs, and apply() only moves fixed or declined findings to verified. Every failing input needs an agent to leave the form: omit the severity, or start a line with 'Verified F3' while meaning the opposite. With a boundary added, a severity-less finding would be dropped instead. Not observed.
- **Checker's measurements:** 'FINDING lower bound is off by one in src/a.ts:3' => severity low, text 'er bound is off by one in src/a.ts:3'; 'CHALLENGE lowercase names break on Linux' => low, 'ercase names break on Linux'; reviewer 'Verified F3 still fails on Windows' => VERIFIED F3, note 'still fails on Windows'; 'I verified F3 …' => []; 'FIXED F1, F2, F3: renamed' => only F1, note 'F2, F3: renamed'.

#### A channel's topic loses meaningful first characters and can end in half an emoji

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `shared-logic-topic-leading-characters`
- **Where:** `shared/channel-name.ts:24`
- **What the code does:** Line 24 strips every leading character that is not a letter, digit, quote, '(' or '[', then cuts at 80 UTF-16 units. Scratch run: '/review the PR' => 'review the PR'; '.env handling' => 'env handling'; '~/.zshrc cleanup' => 'zshrc cleanup'; '`npm test` fails on CI' => 'npm test` fails on CI'; a title with an emoji at position 80 => length 80, last unit d83d, isWellFormed() false.
- **What goes wrong:** Claude Code titles a session '.env handling' or '/review the PR'. The tile, the phone and linked computers show 'env handling' or 'review the PR'. A long title with an emoji at the cut shows a replacement character at its end. The same strip is repeated in src/state/channel-names.ts:11.
- **Suggested fix:** Strip only the known spinner and status symbols (braille U+2800-28FF, ✳ ✢ · * and spaces) instead of all punctuation, cut by code points (`Array.from(text).slice(0, 80).join('')`), and reuse one function in channel-names.ts. Add these titles to channel-name.test.ts.
- **Checker's notes:** channel-name.ts:24 strips every leading character that is not a letter, digit, quote, '(' or '[', then slices 80 UTF-16 units; the same strip is at src/state/channel-names.ts:11. Callers: channel-names.ts:28 (tile) and remote-server.ts:568 (phone, linked computers). The comment shows leading symbols are dropped on purpose to remove spinners. I could not establish that Claude Code sets titles starting with '.', '/', '~' or a backtick, or over 80 units with an emoji at the cut. Display only.
- **Checker's measurements:** '/review the PR' => 'review the PR'; '.env handling' => 'env handling'; '~/.zshrc cleanup' => 'zshrc cleanup'; '`npm test` fails on CI' => 'npm test` fails on CI'; also '#123 fix' => '123 fix', '$PATH broken' => 'PATH broken'. 79 letters + U+1F600 + ' tail' => length 80, unit 79 = d83d, isWellFormed() false.

#### A completion line with a full stop after the bold markers, or a tick after it, is not taken as TASK COMPLETE

- **Check:** Plausible, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `shared-logic-task-signal-near-misses`
- **Where:** `shared/task-signal.ts:38`
- **What the code does:** Line 38 strips '*', '_', backticks and spaces from the line's ends only, then line 40 requires `^TASK COMPLETE\.?$`. Scratch run: 'Done.\n**TASK COMPLETE**.' => null (while '**TASK COMPLETE.**' => complete); 'TASK COMPLETE ✅' => null; '## TASK COMPLETE' => null; 'TASK BLOCKED: **which branch?**' => reason '**which branch?'.
- **What goes wrong:** An agent ends a task from Tasks with '**TASK COMPLETE**.' The task is marked unconfirmed and stays in Working until the person moves it, although the agent gave the signal. Not observed here; I found no task transcripts to count.
- **Suggested fix:** Strip '.', '#', emphasis marks and trailing symbols in a loop until nothing changes (or test `/^[\W_]*TASK COMPLETE[\W_]*$/i`), and strip emphasis inside the blocked reason. Add the three forms to task-signal.test.ts.
- **Checker's notes:** task-signal.ts:38-41 behave as claimed, and task-store.ts:132-135 marks the task 'unconfirmed' (kept in Working) when the result is null; task-store.ts:222 and review-chain.ts:442 also use it. The parser is strict by design and fails safe: the person can move the task. The trigger is an agent deviating from the instructed line (task-signal.ts:9). I found no real task reply in a near-miss form: the only local transcript hits were in a development conversation about this repository.
- **Checker's measurements:** 'Done.\n**TASK COMPLETE**.' => null; '**TASK COMPLETE.**' => complete; 'TASK COMPLETE ✅' => null; '## TASK COMPLETE' => null; 'TASK BLOCKED: **which branch?**' => reason '**which branch?'. Also null: 'TASK COMPLETE!', '✅ TASK COMPLETE', '`TASK COMPLETE`.'. Accepted: '> TASK COMPLETE', '- TASK COMPLETE', 'Task complete.'.

#### A model alias with the 1M suffix is shown raw while the full id and the bare alias are named

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `shared-logic-model-alias-1m`
- **Where:** `shared/model-name.ts:13`
- **What the code does:** Line 13 matches only `^(opus|sonnet|haiku|fable)$`. shared/schemas.ts:4 names `sonnet[1m]` as an expected model value. Scratch run: modelLabel('opus[1m]') => 'opus[1m]', modelLabel('sonnet[1m]') => 'sonnet[1m]', while 'opus' => 'Opus' and 'claude-sonnet-5-5[1m]' => 'Sonnet 5.5 (1M)'. Also 'GPT-6.1-SOL' => 'GPT-6.1 SOL'.
- **What goes wrong:** The person sets a channel's model to opus[1m]. Its tile shows 'opus[1m]' next to other tiles that read 'Opus' and 'Sonnet 5.5 (1M)'. Display only.
- **Suggested fix:** Allow the suffix on the alias: `/^(opus|sonnet|haiku|fable)(\[1m\])?$/i` returning 'Opus (1M)', and lower-case the GPT suffix words before capitalising. Add both to model-name.test.ts.

The model field is free text (EngineControls.tsx:54, modelValue) and shared/schemas.ts:4-5 allows 'sonnet[1m]'. SessionTile.tsx:128 shows modelLabel(reportedModel || model || lastModel), so the raw alias shows whenever the CLI has not reported a full id (idle, or before the first usage record). Cosmetic only, at the bottom of low; the upper-case GPT case needs the person to type capitals.
- **Checker's measurements:** modelLabel('opus[1m]') => 'opus[1m]'; 'sonnet[1m]' => 'sonnet[1m]'; 'opus' => 'Opus'; 'claude-sonnet-5-5[1m]' => 'Sonnet 5.5 (1M)'; 'GPT-6.1-SOL' => 'GPT-6.1 SOL'; 'gpt-6.1-sol' => 'GPT-6.1 Sol'.

### Second round: stylesheets, help text and Experiments (12)

#### A channel's hover controls have had no row of their own since 1.16.1: they are drawn over the header and the terminal

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: ux. ID: `CSS-01`
- **Where:** `src/tile.css:71`
- **What the code does:** tile.css:71 positions .tr-tile-detail with top: var(--tr-tile-head-h); bottom: 0 inside .tr-tile-top. SessionTile.tsx:121-152 now puts only the 30 px header there (ScopeStrip moved to line 168 in commit 3b2f541). Headless Chrome on the real CSS: detail box 1 px high (y 59-60), its 22 px buttons at y 48-70, header y 29-59; elementFromPoint 3 px inside the header returns the detail button.
- **What goes wrong:** Point at any channel header: Stop, Settings, Task, Duplicate, Maximize and Close appear with no background, half over the header (model, topic, account, state chip show through) and half over the terminal's top edge. A click on the lower 7 px of the header's Start, Answer or Retry lands on a detail button instead.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the row its own height: replace bottom: 0 with height: var(--tr-tile-detail-h) (it then covers the terminal's first 30 px while shown), or lay it over the header (top: 0). Add a workbench-smoke check that .tr-tile-detail is at least 22 px high and does not intersect .tr-state-chip; the smoke test checks only opacity today (scripts/workbench-smoke.cjs:62-65).
- **Checker's notes:** tile.css:71 gives .tr-tile-detail top: 30px and bottom: 0 inside .tr-tile-top, which SessionTile.tsx:121-152 now fills with only the 30 px header (ScopeStrip is at line 168; commit 3b2f541 moved it). No other rule sets a height except .config-open (tile.css:73). The row is absolutely positioned after the header, so it paints above it and takes clicks while the header is hovered. No guard found; the smoke test checks opacity only.
- **Checker's measurements:** Built renderer (dist, 1.22.0) in headless Chrome, 1280x800, header hovered: .tr-tile-top and header y 49-79; .tr-tile-detail 1 px high (y 79-80), opacity 1, pointer-events auto; its five buttons y 68-90; Start button y 53-75. elementFromPoint down the centre of Start: rows 53-67 return Start, rows 68-74 return "Isolate session 1" (Maximize). Screenshot shows the buttons across Start and the IDLE chip with no background.

#### Help says Trace adds no permission-bypass flags; by default it starts agents with edits allowed, and Full access bypasses prompts

- **Check:** Confirmed, medium (auditor said medium). Category: docs. ID: `HELP-01`
- **Where:** `shared/help-content.ts:101`
- **What the code does:** Line 101: "Trace inherits those controls and does not add permission-bypass flags." engine-adapter.ts:70-71 adds --permission-mode acceptEdits (Claude) or --ask-for-approval on-request --sandbox workspace-write (Codex) at the default level, shared/schemas.ts:19 DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS = 'edits', and at 'full' adds --allow-dangerously-skip-permissions --permission-mode bypassPermissions or --ask-for-approval never --sandbox danger-full-access.
- **What goes wrong:** A person reads Help > Permissions, trust, and prompt injection to decide how far to trust an agent, and concludes the CLI's own approval settings apply unchanged. In fact a new channel edits files without asking, and a channel set to Full access runs commands with no prompt at all.
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite the paragraph to describe the Permissions control: Ask (the CLI's own settings), Edits (the default: file edits without asking), Full access (no prompts), where it is set (channel Settings, a chat's message box) and that it applies when the channel starts. Add a unit test that the help text names the three levels in src/runtime/permissions.
- **Checker's notes:** help-content.ts:101 says Trace "does not add permission-bypass flags". engine-adapter.ts:62-72 adds flags for every level but Ask; shared/schemas.ts:19-20 makes 'edits' the level when a config has no permissions field; discovery.ts:214 enables it when the CLI advertises the flags. HelpPanel.tsx shows this text unchanged. The Permissions control itself is labelled correctly (src/runtime/permissions.ts:7-14), so only the Help topic is wrong.
- **Checker's measurements:** Bundled electron/services/cli/engine-adapter.ts with esbuild and ran buildLaunch: Claude, no permissions field -> ["--permission-mode","acceptEdits"]; Claude full -> ["--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions","--permission-mode","bypassPermissions"]; Claude chat default -> ["--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions","--permission-mode","acceptEdits"]; Codex default -> ["--ask-for-approval","on-request","--sandbox","workspace-write"]; Codex full -> ["--ask-for-approval","never","--sandbox","danger-full-access"]; Claude ask -> [].

#### On tiles 310 to 380 px wide the header overflows and the state chip is cut off

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `CSS-02`
- **Where:** `src/tile.css:243`
- **What the code does:** tile.css:243-244 give the name min-width 48 px and the account chip 44 px; action and chip are flex: none (33, 46); .session-tile clips (styles.css:31). Measured in headless Chrome: at 310 px the chip is clipped by 37 px (idle + account), 24 px (waiting + account), 47 px (failed), 101 px (failed + account); at 380 px by 31 px (failed + account). The comment at tile.css:91 says they never clip.
- **What goes wrong:** Four channels in the default columns layout (workspace-store.ts:78, 4 across, minW 3) on a 1280 px window, or 1600 px at 125% scale, give 310 px tiles. A channel that fails shows Retry and Open log, and its FAULT chip is pushed out of view; a waiting channel's HOLD chip loses 24 px.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a step for the narrowest tiles, for example @container (max-width: 400px) { .tr-tile-topic, .tr-action.ghost { display: none } .tr-tile-name { min-width: 0 } .tr-profile-chip.account-profile-chip[data-account] { min-width: 0 } }, and order the shrink so the state chip is last to go. Cover it with a layout test at 310 px.
- **Checker's notes:** Rules are as cited (tile.css:33, 46, 243-244; styles.css:31 overflow hidden). One correction to the scenario: adding a channel switches the layout to grid (workspace-store.ts:228), so four channels sit 2x2 at 628 px. 310 px tiles need the Columns button with four channels, or seven or eight channels in grid (arrangeTiles, line 78), at about 1280 px of window. The state still shows in the tile border and status bar, so low is right.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real renderer, four channels, Columns, 1280 px window: tile 310 px. State chip clipped by 23 px (idle + account), 14 px (waiting + account), 36 px (failed), 90 px (failed + account: chip wholly out of view, Open log cut). 1366 px window (332 px tile): 14 px (failed), 68 px (failed + account). 1600 px (390 px tile): 10 px (failed + account). 1920 px: none. States were injected as the markup SessionTile renders; no topic or focus reticle, which add 10 and 24 px more.

#### Toasts sit above the Needs-you list and cover its buttons on windows narrower than about 1500 px

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `CSS-03`
- **Where:** `src/shell.css:116`
- **What the code does:** shell.css:116 gives .needs-you-list z-index 60, anchored above the status bar; screens.css:46 gives .tr-toasts z-index 70, fixed at right 12 px just above the same bar. Measured: at 1280 and 1366 px the toast overlaps the list by 314 and 232 px and elementFromPoint on the list's Answer button returns the toast; at 1600 px 27 px; none at 1920. Also the Needs-you cell is 16 px high, top-aligned in a 31 px bar.
- **What goes wrong:** A channel asks for approval: a toast appears and the status bar shows Needs you. On a 1366 px window (or 1920 px at 125% scale) the person opens the list; the right part of every row, with Answer, Open Tasks or Save to memory, is under the toast until each toast is dismissed.
- **Suggested fix:** Raise .needs-you-list above the toasts (z-index 80) or hide .tr-toasts while the list is open. Make .needs-you display: flex so its button stretches to the bar's height like the other cells.
- **Checker's notes:** shell.css:116 (z-index 60) and screens.css:46 (z-index 70, fixed, right 12 px, 420 px wide) are as cited, and both sit in the same stacking context under .app-shell (App.tsx:38-51). Overlap depends on the width of the cells right of Needs you, so the amounts differ from the report. Only rows level with the toast stack are covered, not always every row. For an approval the toast has its own Answer button, which limits the harm.
- **Checker's measurements:** Real renderer, two channels, both plan-limit cells, one waiting toast (state injected as NeedsYou.tsx/Toasts.tsx markup). Horizontal overlap of list and toast: 181 px at 1280, 120 px at 1366, 0 at 1600 and 1920 (reported: 314, 232, 27, 0). One-row list at 1280 and 1366: elementFromPoint on the list's Answer returns the toast. Two-row list: Answer free, Open Tasks under the toast. .needs-you-cell is 16 px high (y 769-785); other cells 31 px.

#### Higher contrast changes almost nothing; control borders are 2.0:1, the scrollbar thumb 1.5:1 and an offline computer's tab 2.25:1

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `CSS-04`
- **Where:** `src/styles.css:341`
- **What the code does:** Computed in Chrome with data-contrast normal then high: only .storage-card/.hub-form borders and .quiet-button text change; buttons, inputs, selects, the tile border and tile controls are unchanged, because flight-deck.css:21, workbench.css:89 and tile.css:2 outrank styles.css:341 and workbench.css:170. Ratios from tokens.css: --tr-line-control on --tr-bg 2.05, scroll thumb --tr-control-on (workbench.css:118) on --tr-term 1.50, --tr-fg-off text (shell.css:96) on --tr-surface 2.25. All other text tokens are 4.7:1 or more. There is no light theme (tokens.css:3).
- **What goes wrong:** A person with low vision turns on Settings > Higher contrast. Button and field outlines stay at 2.0:1 (WCAG 1.4.11 asks 3:1), the 7 px scrollbar thumb in a long terminal or diff stays nearly invisible, and a linked computer that is offline keeps a tab label at 2.25:1 (AA text asks 4.5:1).
- **Suggested fix:** Under [data-contrast="high"] redefine the tokens instead of single rules: --tr-line-control and --tr-tile-border to --tr-fg-dim, --tr-control-on for the scroll thumb to --tr-line-strong or lighter, --tr-fg-off to --tr-fg-faint. Use --tr-fg-faint for the offline tab in both modes.
- **Checker's notes:** styles.css:341 and workbench.css:170 are outranked as described (flight-deck.css:21, workbench.css:76 and 89, tile.css:2 come later at equal or higher specificity). Two small corrections: .control-note and .eyebrow text and a few dividers using --border also change; and a terminal's scrollbar is xterm 6's own slider (src/runtime/terminal-theme.ts:9, rgba(61,98,116,.35)), not the ::-webkit thumb, which applies to diffs, chats and lists. The offline tab is a clickable button (Topbar.tsx:72).
- **Checker's measurements:** Diffed computed colours of every element, data-contrast normal vs high, on 15 screens of the real renderer: no .button, input, select, textarea, .session-tile, .tr-detail-button or .tr-action changed. Changed: .storage-card and .hub-form borders, .quiet-button, .control-note and .eyebrow text, a few divider lines. Tile settings select border rgb(61,98,116) and detail button border rgb(42,74,90) in both modes. Ratios: --tr-line-control on --tr-bg 2.05; --tr-control-on on --tr-term 1.50; --tr-fg-off on --tr-surface 2.25; --tr-fg-faint on --tr-surface 6.76.

#### Help's daily-workflow topics describe a read-only diff viewer, a Restart that never resumes and a footer that shows the directory

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: docs. ID: `HELP-02`
- **Where:** `shared/help-content.ts:94`
- **What the code does:** Line 94: "The viewer does not stage, discard, or commit"; GitDeliveryPanel.tsx:44-54 has Stage, Commit, Push and Pull request beside the diff (delivery.ts:72-76 runs git add). Line 90: Restart is "not an automatically resumed conversation"; workspace-store.ts:207 stores the conversation ID and engine-adapter.ts:48 passes --resume. Line 89: "The footer shows the directory" and "two to eight" panels; StatusBar.tsx:87 shows the project name only, SessionTile.tsx:150 allows one. Line 12: a task moves to review when "its channel stops"; task-store.ts:212 keeps it in Working, marked stopped.
- **What goes wrong:** A person presses Restart expecting a clean conversation, as Help says, and the agent continues the old one with its full context. Another looks for the running directory in the footer and finds only the project name. A third believes Trace cannot commit, and misses that the Changes screen stages and commits.
- **Suggested fix:** Update the review, workspace and workflow-tools topics: Changes includes Stage, Commit, Push and Pull request with a confirmation each; Start and Restart continue the recorded conversation unless "Start a fresh conversation next time" is chosen; the directory is in the channel's hover row; one to eight channels; a stopped channel leaves its task in Working, marked stopped.
- **Checker's notes:** All four statements are contradicted by code. Stage and Commit sit beside the diff (RepositoryPanel.tsx:58, GitDeliveryPanel.tsx:44-54, delivery.ts:76 runs git add). Restart resumes: use-native-host.ts:63 stores each run's conversation ID as resumeId for both engines (the main path; workspace-store.ts:207 covers only a switched conversation) and engine-adapter.ts:48 passes it. ScopeStrip.tsx has no directory; StatusBar.tsx:87 shows the project name. task-store.ts:212 keeps a stopped channel's task active. Help lines 5 and 7 already say the opposite of lines 90 and 94.
- **Checker's measurements:** Static text, nothing to execute beyond buildLaunch: with resumeId set it returns ["--permission-mode","acceptEdits","--resume","11111111-2222-4333-8444-555555555555"]. Read help-content.ts lines 5, 7, 12, 89, 90, 94 and the cited code. SessionTile.tsx:150 disables Close only at one channel, so one channel is allowed, against "two to eight". The fresh-conversation control exists at SessionPreferences.tsx:12.

#### Help's usage and memory topics give rules the code no longer follows

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: docs. ID: `HELP-03`
- **Where:** `shared/help-content.ts:98`
- **What the code does:** Line 98: activity is grouped "by their start date" and the dashboard shows "the latest 14 active days"; usage-summary.ts:57-60 adds a run's tokens to each day they were used on, and USAGE_WINDOWS (line 5) offers Today, 7, 14 and 30 calendar days. Line 123: aggregate cost "stays unknown if any included run is unpriced"; usageTotals on one priced (1.5) and one unpriced run returned cost 1.5, unpriced 1. Line 80: "Install it from Shared memory"; HubPanels.tsx:104 says channels started in Trace get memory automatically.
- **What goes wrong:** On this computer every run is unpriced or partly priced (first-round finding on rate names); Help tells the person the total would then show unknown, but Usage shows a dollar total with "N unpriced" under it, so the figure reads as complete. A person also installs the memory server into a project because Help says it is required.
- **Suggested fix:** Correct the three paragraphs: tokens count on the day they were used; windows are Today, 7, 14 and 30 days; the cost total sums priced runs and names how many are unpriced; memory tools are added at launch, and installing into a project is only for CLIs run outside Trace.
- **Checker's notes:** All three paragraphs are stale. src/state/usage-summary.ts:5 offers Today/7/14/30 calendar days and :59-60 adds a run's tokens to each day used; usageTotals (:39) sums priced records and counts unpriced; HubPanels.tsx:104 says Trace sessions already get memory. One overstatement: if every record is unpriced the total is null and shows a dash, so Help is only wrong for partly priced windows.
- **Checker's measurements:** esbuild bundle of usage-summary.ts: usageTotals(priced 1.5 + unpriced) -> cost 1.5, unpriced 1; all unpriced -> cost null. dailySeries on a run with days {11-15: 100, 11-16: 900} put 100 and 900 on separate days. USAGE_WINDOWS = Today, 7 d, 14 d, 30 d.

#### An experiment's "Measured tokens" count cache re-reads, unlike every other token figure in Trace

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: bug. ID: `EXP-01`
- **Where:** `shared/experiments.ts:6`
- **What the code does:** Line 6 sums u.input + u.output + (claude ? u.cached + u.cacheWrite : 0). shared/hub.ts:71-74 defines the figure used by tiles, the status bar, Usage and budgets as freshInput + output, with cache reads left out. Run on a Claude record (input 1,000, output 2,000, cached 500,000, cacheWrite 20,000): compareExperiment gives averageTokens 523000, newTokens gives 23000. A Codex record (input 520,000 including 500,000 cached, output 2,000): 522000 against 22000.
- **What goes wrong:** A person links two runs to variants A and B. The table shows 523,000 for a run whose tile and Usage row show 23k. Cache re-reads grow with the length of the conversation, not with the wording of the prompt, so the longer conversation looks worse whatever the prompt was.
- **Suggested fix:** Use newTokens(u) from shared/hub.ts for averageTokens, and show cache reads in a separate column if wanted. Add a unit test for shared/experiments.ts (none exists) with one Claude and one Codex record. shared/coaching.ts:56 uses the same sum and should follow.
- **Checker's notes:** Line 6 sums cache reads; shared/hub.ts:71-74 excludes them. Git log shows the 'tok means new tokens everywhere' change (2026-10-25) came after experiments (2026-10-12), which were not moved over. Two corrections: shared/experiments.test.ts does exist and pins the old sum (Codex 100+30 with 20 cached expects 130), so a fix must change it; AI coaching (shared/coaching.ts:56) uses the same sum, so 'unlike every other figure' is overstated.
- **Checker's measurements:** compareExperiment on Claude record (1,000 / 2,000 / cached 500,000 / cacheWrite 20,000): averageTokens 523000, newTokens 23000. Codex record (input 520,000 incl. 500,000 cached, output 2,000): 522000 against 22000.

#### A trial saved with the form's defaults records 0 minutes and 0 rework as real values, and no experiment or trial can be corrected or removed

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `EXP-02`
- **Where:** `src/components/ExperimentsPanel.tsx:10`
- **What the code does:** Line 10 starts minutes and rework at 0 and outcome at 'partial'; the inputs at line 14 are not required, and an emptied field becomes Number('') = 0. shared/experiments.ts:6 averages every trial: trials of 30 and blank gave averageMinutes 15. The host (electron/services/workbench/experiments.ts:8) accepts only experiment-list, experiment-save and trial-add, and refuses new records at 100 experiments (line 14) and 1,000 trials (line 18).
- **What goes wrong:** A person records a trial and fills in only the evidence. It is stored as 0 minutes, 0 rework, Partial, and halves that variant's average time. Nothing in the app can edit or delete it, or the experiment; after 100 experiments, creating one fails for good unless experiments.json is edited by hand.
- **Suggested fix:** Make minutes and rework nullable (empty means not recorded) and average only recorded values, as tokens already do. Add trial-delete and experiment-delete actions (with the trials of a deleted experiment), behind a confirmation, and cover them in workbench.test.ts.
- **Checker's notes:** Line 10 defaults are 0, 0 and 'partial'; only evidence is required (line 14). The host schema (electron/services/workbench/experiments.ts:8) has only experiment-list, experiment-save and trial-add, and main.ts:735 routes nothing else; limits at lines 14 and 18. Nuance: the fields are controlled, so an emptied field shows 0 again, and the 0 is visible before saving. Reaching 100 experiments is unlikely.
- **Checker's measurements:** compareExperiment with trials of 30 minutes and Number('') = 0: averageMinutes 15, averageRework 0. grep for 'experiment-' and 'trial-' actions in shared, electron, src: only the three actions exist; no delete or edit.

#### Dead CSS: 17 rules and 8 selector parts match no element, and several rules are always overridden

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: improvement. ID: `CSS-05`
- **Where:** `src/workbench.css:24`
- **What the code does:** Class names compared with every className in src/, shared/ and phone/ (scratch script). Of 1,340 desktop rules, 17 match nothing: .navigation (styles.css:18, 216, 285; workbench.css:24, 25), .version (styles.css:20; workbench.css:30), .command-search (styles.css:94-95; workbench.css:113-114), .tr-wordmark-bars (shell.css:13-15), .tr-scope-window (tile.css:60, 62), flight-deck.css:61. Dead parts: flight-deck.css:7, 14, 45, 70; views.css:36; tokens.css:107 (.tr-brackets). phone.css: none of 182.
- **What goes wrong:** Always overridden or hidden: backdrop-filter (styles.css:92, cancelled by screens.css:64); the header's ctx and tok figures (tile.css:274 hides what SessionTile.tsx:132-133 still renders); the body's grid (tokens.css:105) lies under the opaque .app-shell (shell.css:3) and is reset on the phone (phone.css:4), so the design's grid is never seen. Someone editing .navigation or the grid changes nothing.
- **Suggested fix:** Delete the 17 rules and 8 selector parts, the two hidden metric spans in SessionTile.tsx and the cancelled backdrop-filter. Decide whether the grid is wanted: if so, make .app-shell and .main-shell transparent over it. A scripts/ check that every class in the stylesheets appears in a className would keep this from returning.
- **Checker's notes:** No className uses navigation, version, command-search, tr-wordmark-bars, tr-scope-window or help-filters/chip; my count also gives 17 rules and 8 parts. command-backdrop is always used with tr-scrim (CommandMenu.tsx:128); tile.css:274 hides the spans at SessionTile.tsx:132-133 (inside the header, closed at 138); .app-shell and .main-shell are opaque (shell.css:3-4). Maintenance only, no wrong behaviour; workbench.css:170 has a ninth dead part. Totals of 1,340 and 182 not rechecked.
- **Checker's measurements:** Scratch script (class names in selectors vs whole-word search of src, shared, phone, electron): unreferenced static names were panel-heading, view-title, changes-title, tool-card, tr-key-title, help-filters, tr-tabs-inline, tr-wordmark-bars, tr-scope-window, tr-brackets, usage-chart. Headless Chromium with phone.css: body background-image none.

#### Phone: the person's messages in Conversation have no left bar, and the remove-image button is 18 by 44 px

- **Check:** Confirmed, low (auditor said low). Category: ux. ID: `CSS-06`
- **Where:** `phone/phone.css:108`
- **What the code does:** phone.css:108 sets border-left: 2px solid var(--tr-focus), but --tr-focus is itself "2px solid var(--tr-fg-strong)" (tokens.css:101), so the declaration is invalid; Chrome computes border-left 0px none for .said.you (phone/main.ts:570). phone.css:7 gives every button min-height 44px, which outranks height: 18px on .chat-attachments button (tile.css:174): measured 18 x 44 px over a 56 px thumbnail.
- **What goes wrong:** In a terminal channel's Conversation view on the phone, the person's messages are told apart from the agent's only by a slightly lighter background. In a chat, each attached image carries a tall oval over its right edge instead of a small round remove button.
- **Suggested fix:** Use border-left: 2px solid var(--tr-fg-strong) at phone.css:108. Add .phone-chat .chat-attachments button { min-height: 0; } (or scope the 44 px minimum to the phone's own buttons) and keep the touch target with padding or a larger hit area.
- **Checker's notes:** phone.css:108 puts --tr-focus (a full '2px solid colour', tokens.css:101) after '2px solid', so the declaration is invalid at computed-value time. phone.css:7's 44 px min-height applies to .chat-attachments button (tile.css:174), which RemoteChat.tsx:96 renders on the phone. One overstatement: main.ts:570 also prefixes each of the person's messages with a bold 'You', so the background is not the only cue.
- **Checker's measurements:** Headless Chromium (playwright-core) on a scratch page loading tokens.css, phone.css, tile.css: .said.you border-left-width 0px, style none, background rgb(13, 21, 27) against transparent for .said.agent; remove button 18 x 44 px (min-height 44px, radius 50%) over a 56 px image.
